## I — "Love That Passeth Knowledge" / I ——「超越知识的爱」

> 「能以明白基督的爱是何等长阔高深，并知道这爱是过于人所能测度的。」
> ——以弗所书 3:19

===Original===
If I could only make men understand the real meaning of the words of the apostle John—“**God is love**,” I would take that single text, and would go up and down the world proclaiming this glorious truth. If you can convince a man that you love him you have won his heart. If we really make people believe that God loves them, how we should find them crowding into the kingdom of heaven! The trouble is that men think God hates them; and so they are all the time running away from Him.

We built a church in Chicago some years ago; and were very anxious to teach the people the love of God. We thought if we could not preach it into their hearts we would try and burn it in; so we put right over the pulpit in gas-jets these words—**God is Love**. A man going along the streets one night glanced through the door, and saw the text. He was a poor prodigal. As he passed on he thought to himself, “God is Love! No! He does not love me; for I am a poor miserable sinner.” He tried to get rid of the text; but it seemed to stand out right before him in letters of fire. He went on a little further; then turned round, went back, and went into the meeting. He did not hear the sermon; but the words of that short text had got deeply lodged in his heart, and that was enough. It is of little account what men say if the Word of God only gets an entrance into the sinner’s heart. He stayed after the first meeting was over; and I found him there weeping like a child. As I unfolded the Scriptures and told him how God had loved him all the time, although he had wandered so far away, and how God was waiting to receive him and forgive him, the light of the Gospel broke into his mind, and he went away rejoicing.

===Chinese===
倘若我能叫人明白使徒约翰那句话的真意——「**神就是爱**」——我愿只取这一句经文，便走遍天下，传扬这荣耀的真理。你若能说服一个人你爱他，你便已赢得了他的心。我们若真能叫人相信神爱他们，你看他们该是怎样地涌进天国！难处只在于：人总以为神恨他们，于是时时刻刻都在躲避祂。

几年前我们在芝加哥建了一座教堂；急切地要教导众人神的爱。我们想，若不能把这道理讲进他们心里，便索性用火把它烧进去——于是我们在讲台正上方，用煤气灯管拼出这几个字：**神就是爱**。一天夜里，一个人走在街上，朝门里一瞥，看见了那节经文。他是个可怜的浪子。他往前走着，心里想：「神就是爱！不！祂并不爱我；因为我是个可怜悲惨的罪人。」他想把这节经文从心头赶走；可它却像火写的字一样赫然立在他眼前。他又走了一段；然后转身折回，走进了聚会。他没听到讲道；可那短短一节经文已深深扎进他心里，这便够了。只要神的话语进到了罪人的心里，人所讲的便算不得什么。第一场聚会散了，他还留下；我发现他在那里，哭得像个孩子。我把圣经向他展开，告诉他这些年来神一直爱他，纵使他流浪得那么远；告诉他神正等着收留他、赦免他——福音的光便照进了他的心，他欢欢喜喜地走了。

===Original===
There is nothing in this world that men prize so much us they do Love. Show me a person who has no one to care for or love him, and I will show you one of the most wretched beings on the face of the earth. Why do people commit suicide? Very often it is because this thought steals in upon them—that no one loves them; and they would rather die than live.

I know of no truth in the whole Bible that ought to come home to us with such power and tenderness as that of the Love of God; and there is no truth in the Bible that Satan would so much like to blot out. For more than six thousand years he has been trying to persuade men that God does not love them. He succeeded in making our first父母 believe this lie; and he too often succeeds with their children.

===Chinese===
这世上再没有一样东西，是人所珍视如「爱」的。你指给我看一个无人关怀、无人疼爱的人，我便指给你看全地上最悲惨的人之一。人为何寻死？十之八九，是因为这样一个念头悄悄潜入他们心头——没有谁爱我；他们宁可死，也不愿活。

整本圣经里，我想不出有哪一条真理，该像「神的爱」这条这般带着能力与温柔临到我们；整本圣经里，我也想不出有哪一条真理，是撒但如此想要涂抹掉的。六千多年来，它一直试图使人相信神并不爱他们。它曾成功地叫我们的人类始祖信了这条谎言；而它也屡屡在他们的子孙身上得手。

===Original===
The idea that God does not love us often comes from false teaching. Mothers make a mistake in teaching children that God does not love them when they do wrong; but only when they do right. That is not taught in Scripture. You do not teach your children that when they do wrong you hate them. Their wrongdoing does not change your love to hate; if it did, you would change your love a great many times. Because your child is fretful, or has committed some act of disobedience, you do not cast him out as though he did not belong to you! No! he is still your child; and you love him. And if men have gone astray from God it does not follow that He hates them. It is the sin that He hates.

I believe the reason why a great many people think God does not love them is because they are measuring God by their own small rule, from their own standpoint. We love men as long as we consider them worthy of our love; when they are not we cast them off. It is not so with God. There is a vast difference between human love and Divine love.

===Chinese===
「神不爱我们」这个念头，往往来自错谬的教导。母亲们教导孩子说：孩子做错时神不爱他们，惟有做对时神才爱——这是一个错误。圣经里没有这样的教训。你不会教导你的孩子说，他们做错事时你便恨他们。他们的过错并不会把你的爱变成恨；若是会，你的爱早已变过无数回了。你的孩子烦躁也好，犯了什么悖逆之事也好，你不会把他赶出家门，仿佛他不是你的骨肉！不！他仍是你的孩子；你爱他。同理，人纵然远离了神，也不等于神恨恶他们。神所恨恶的是罪。

我相信许许多多的人之所以觉得神不爱他们，是因为他们用自己的小尺子、站在自己的立场上来量神。我们爱人，是趁着我们还觉得他们配得我们的爱；一旦不配，我们便把他们抛开。神却不是这样。人的爱与神的爱，其间相差不可以道里计。

===Original===
In Ephesians 3:18, we are told of the breadth, and length, and depth, and height, of God’s love. Many of us think we know something of God’s love; but centuries hence we shall admit we have never found out much about it. Columbus discovered America; but what did he know about its great lakes, rivers, forests, and the Mississippi Valley? He died, without knowing much about what he had discovered. So, many of us have discovered something of the love of God; but there are heights, depths and lengths of it we do not know. That Love is a great ocean; and we require to plunge into it before we really know anything of it. It is said of a Roman Catholic Archbishop of Paris, that when he was thrown into prison and condemned to be shot, a little while before he was led out to die, he saw a window in his cell in the shape of a cross. Upon the top of the cross he wrote “height,” at the bottom “depth,” and at the end of each arm “length.” He had experienced the truth conveyed in the hymn—

When I survey the wondrous Cross,
On which the Prince of Glory died.

When we wish to know the love of God we should go to Calvary. Can we look upon that scene, and say God did not love us? That cross speaks of the love of God. Greater love never has been taught than that which the cross teaches. What prompted God to give up Christ?—what prompted Christ to die?—if it were not love? “Greater love hath no man than this, that a man lay down his life for his friends.” Christ laid down His life for His enemies; Christ laid down His life for His murderers; Christ laid down His life for them that hated Him; and the spirit of the cross, the spirit of Calvary, is love. When they were mocking Him and deriding Him, what did He say? “Father, forgive them, for they know not what they do.” That is love. He did not call down fire from heaven to consume them; there was nothing but love in His heart.

===Chinese===
以弗所书 3:18 告诉我们，神的爱有何等的阔、长、深、高。我们许多人以为自己多少懂得一点神的爱；可再过几世纪，我们也要承认：关于它，我们所知的实在不多。哥伦布发现了美洲；可他对它的大湖、大河、森林、密西西比河谷又知道多少？他到死也未能对他所发现的晓得许多。照样，我们许多人发现了神的爱的一鳞半爪；可它的高、深、长，我们仍不晓得。那爱是一片汪洋；我们必须投身其中，方能真正认识它一二。据说巴黎有一位天主教大主教，被下在监里、判了枪决，临被押出去受刑之前不久，他看见自己牢房里有一扇十字架形的窗。他在十字架的顶端写下「高」，底端写下「深」，两臂末端写下「长」。他已经亲历过那首圣诗所道的真理——

> 妾每仰望奇妙十架，
> 荣耀之主于焉受死。

我们要想认识神的爱，便当去各各他。我们能注视那一幕，却说神不爱我们吗？那十字架所说的，正是神的爱。再没有比十字架所教导的更大的爱了。若不是爱，是什么促使神交出基督？——是什么促使基督去死？——「人为朋友舍命，人的爱心没有比这个大的。」基督为仇敌舍命；基督为杀祂的人舍命；基督为恨祂的人舍命；而十字架的灵、各各他的灵，便是爱。他们戏弄祂、辱骂祂时，祂说什么？「父啊，赦免他们，因为他们所做的，他们不晓得。」那便是爱。祂没有求天降烈火把他们烧灭；祂心里除了爱，一无所有。

===Original===
If you study the Bible you will find that the love of God is *unchangeable*. Many who loved you at one time have perhaps grown cold in their affection, and turned away from you: it may be that their love is changed to hatred. It is not so with God. It is recorded of Jesus Christ, just when He was about to be parted from His disciples and led away to Calvary, that: “having loved His own which were in the world, He loved them unto the end” (John 13:1). He knew that one of His disciples would betray Him; yet He loved Judas. He knew that another disciple would deny Him, and swear that he never knew Him; and yet He loved Peter. It was the love which Christ had for Peter that broke his heart, and brought him back in penitence to the feet of his Lord. For three years Jesus had been with the disciples trying to teach them His love, not only by His life and words, but by His works. And, on the night of His betrayal, He takes a basin of water, girds Himself with a towel, and taking the place of a servant, washes their feet; He wanted to convince them of His unchanging love.

There is no portion of Scripture I read so often as John 14; and there is none that is more sweet to me. I never tire of reading it. Hear what our Lord says, as He pours out His heart to His Disciples: “At that day ye shall know that I am in My Father, and ye in Me, and I in you. He that hath My commandments, and keepeth them, he it is that loveth Me: and he that loveth Me shall be loved by My Father” (14:20–21). Think of the great God who created heaven and earth loving you and me!… “If a man love Me, he will keep My words; and My Father will love him; and We will come unto him, and make Our abode with him” (v. 23).

===Chinese===
你若查考圣经，便知道神的爱是*不改变*的。许多一度爱你的人，也许情分已冷，转身而去；他们的爱也许已化为恨。神却不如此。论到耶稣基督，正当祂将要与门徒分离、被带往各各他之时，圣经记着说：「祂既然爱世间属自己的人，就爱他们到底」（约翰福音 13:1）。祂知道门徒中有一人要出卖祂；然而祂爱犹大。祂知道另一人要否认祂、起誓说从不认识祂；然而祂爱彼得。正是基督对彼得的爱，击碎了他的心，使他痛悔着回到主的脚前。三年来，耶稣与门徒同在，想用自己的生命、言语，更用祂的作为，把祂的爱教导他们。而就在被卖的那一夜，祂端起一盆水，用毛巾束腰，取了仆人的样式，为他们洗脚；祂要使他们确信祂那永不改变的爱。

约翰福音第 14 章，是我读得最多的一段经文；再没有一段比它更甘甜。我百读不厌。请听我们的主向门徒倾心吐意时所说的话：「到那日，你们就知道我在父里面，你们在我里面，我也在你们里面。有了我的命令又遵守的，这人就是爱我的；爱我的必蒙我父爱他」（14:20–21）。请想一想，那创造天地的伟大神，竟爱你爱我！……「人若爱我，就必遵守我的道；我父也必爱他，并且我们要到他那里去，与他同住」（23 节）。

===Original===
Would to God that our puny minds could grasp this great truth, that the Father and the Son so love us that They desire to come and abide with us. Not to tarry for a night, but to come and abide in our hearts.

We have another passage more wonderful still in John 17:23. “I in them, and thou in Me, that they may be made perfect in one; and that the world may know that Thou hast sent Me, and hast loved them as Thou hast loved Me.” I think that is one of the most remarkable sayings that ever fell from the lips of Jesus Christ. There is no reason why the Father should not love him. He was obedient unto death; He never transgressed the Father’s law, or turned aside from the path of perfect obedience by one hair’s breadth. It is very different with us; and yet, notwithstanding all our rebellion and foolishness, He says that if we are trusting in Christ, the Father loves us as He loves the Son. Marvellous love! Wonderful love! That God can possibly love us as He loves His own Son seems too good to be true. Yet that is the teaching of Jesus Christ.

===Chinese===
但愿我们这微薄的心智能抓住这一伟大的真理：父与子如此爱我们，以至于祂们愿意来与我们同住。不是只歇一夜，而是来*住*在我们心里。

约翰福音 17:23 还有一段更奇妙的：「我在他们里面，你在我里面，使他们完完全全地合而为一，叫世人知道你差了我来，也知道你爱他们如同爱我一样。」我想，这是从耶稣基督口中出来的最不寻常的话之一。父不爱子是毫无道理的；子顺服至死，从未违背父的律法，从未偏离完全顺服的道路一丝一毫。我们则截然不同；然而尽管我们悖逆又愚昧，祂说：我们若信靠基督，父爱我们就如祂爱子一样。奇哉妙哉的爱！何等奇妙的爱！神竟能像爱自己的独生子那样爱我们——这好得简直不像真的。然而这便是耶稣基督的教训。

===Original===
It is hard to make a sinner believe in this unchangeable love of God. When a man has wandered away from God he thinks that God hates him. We must make a distinction between sin and the sinner. God loves the sinner; but He hates the sin. He hates sin, because it mars human life. It is just because God loves the sinner that He hates sin.

God’s love is not only unchangeable, but *unfailing*. In Isaiah 49:15–16 we read: “Can a woman forget her sucking child that she should not have compassion on the son of her womb? yea, they may forget; yet will I not forget thee. Behold I have graven thee upon the palms of My hands; thy walls are continually before Me.”

Now the strongest human love that we know of is a mother’s love. Many things will separate a man from his wife. A father may turn his back on his child; brothers and sisters may become inveterate enemies; husbands may desert their wives; wives, their husbands. But a mother’s love endures through all. In good repute, in bad repute, in the face of the world’s condemnation, a mother loves on, and hopes that her child may turn from his evil ways and repent. She remembers the infant smiles, the merry laugh of childhood, the promise of youth; and she can never be brought to think him unworthy. Death cannot quench a mother’s love; it is stronger than death.

===Chinese===
要叫一个罪人相信神这永不改变的爱，实在很难。一个人远离了神，便以为神恨他。我们必须把罪与罪人分开来看。神爱罪人；但祂恨罪。祂恨罪，是因为罪损伤了人的生命。正因为神爱罪人，祂才恨罪。

神的爱不仅不改变，而且是*不衰竭*的。以赛亚书 49:15–16 记着：「妇人焉能忘记她吃奶的婴孩，不怜恤她所生的儿子？即或有忘记的，我却不忘记你。看哪，我将你铭刻在我掌上。」

我们所知人间最强的爱，是*母爱*。许多事能把夫妻分开；父亲也许背弃孩子；兄弟姐妹也许成为死敌；丈夫也许抛弃妻子；妻子也许抛弃丈夫。但母爱能熬过这一切。无论声名好坏，无论世人如何定罪，母亲照旧爱下去，盼着孩子终有一天改邪归正、悔悟回头。她记得婴孩时的笑、童年时的欢声、年少时的指望；她永不能被说服去认为他不配。死亡也熄不灭母爱；它比死亡更强。

===Original===
You have seen a mother watching over her sick child. How willingly she would take the disease into her own body if she could thus relieve her child! Week after week she will keep watch; she will let no one else take care of that sick child.

A friend of mine, some time ago, was visiting in a beautiful home where he met a number of friends. After they had all gone away, having left something behind, he went back to get it. There he found the lady of the house, a wealthy lady, sitting behind a poor fellow who looked like a tramp. He was her own son. Like the prodigal, he had wandered far away: yet the mother said, “This is my boy; I love him still.” Take a mother with nine or ten children, if one goes astray, she seems to love that one more than any of the rest.

===Chinese===
你见过母亲守在病儿床前的样子。若能以此解脱孩子，她多么情愿把那病接到自己身上！她一周接一周地守着，不让旁人去照料那病儿。

我有一位朋友，前些时候去一户漂亮人家作客，在那里会见了若干朋友。客人都散了，他因落了东西，回去取。在那里他看见那家的主妇——一位阔太太——坐在一个看上去像流浪汉的穷汉背后。他是她的亲生儿子。像那浪子一样，他远走他乡；然而母亲说：「这是我的孩子；我仍爱他。」一个母亲有九个十个孩子，若有一个走迷了路，她似乎爱那一个胜过其余所有的。

===Original===
A leading minister in the state of New York once told me of a father who was a very bad character. The mother did all she could to prevent the contamination of the boy; but the influence of the father was stronger, and he led his son into all kinds of sin until the lad became one of the worst of criminals. He committed murder, and was put on his trial. All through the trial, the widowed mother (for the father had died) sat in the court. When the witnesses testified against the boy it seemed to hurt the mother much more than the son. When he was found guilty and sentenced to die, everyone else feeling the justice of the verdict, seemed satisfied at the result. But the mother’s love never faltered. She begged for a reprieve; but that was denied. After the execution she craved for the body; and this also was refused. According to custom, it was buried in the prison yard. A little while afterwards the mother herself died; but, before she was taken away, she expressed a desire to be buried by the side of her boy. She was not ashamed of being known as the mother of a murderer.

The story is told of a young woman in Scotland, who left her home, and became an outcast in Glasgow. Her mother sought her far and wide, but in vain. At last, she caused her picture to be hung upon the walls of the Midnight Mission rooms, where abandoned women resorted. Many gave the picture a passing glance. One lingered by the picture. It is the same dear face that looked down upon her in her childhood. She has not forgotten nor cast off her sinning child; or her picture would never have been hung upon those walls. The lips seemed to open, and whisper, “Come home; I forgive you, and love you still.” The poor girl sank down overwhelmed with her feelings. She was the prodigal daughter. The sight of her mother’s face had broken her heart. She became truly penitent for her sins, and with a heart full of sorrow and shame, returned to her forsaken home; and mother and daughter were once more united.

===Chinese===
纽约州一位知名的牧师曾告诉我一件事：有一个父亲，人品极坏。母亲千方百计想使儿子免受污染；可父亲的影响力更大，他把儿子带进了种种罪恶里，直到那少年成了最坏的罪犯之一。他犯了谋杀罪，被送上审判。整个审判期间，寡母（父亲已死）坐在法庭里。证人作证控告那孩子时，受伤害的似乎是母亲而不是儿子。他被判有罪、判了死刑，旁人都觉得判得公允、对此结果满意。可母爱从未动摇。她恳求缓刑；被拒绝。行刑之后，她索求遗体；这也被拒绝。按惯例，遗体葬在监狱院子里。不久母亲自己也死了；可她在被抬走之前，表示希望能葬在她孩子身边。她并不以「杀人犯的母亲」这一身份为耻。

还有一个故事，说的是苏格兰一位年轻女子离家出走，在格拉斯哥沦为流浪人。她母亲四处找她，徒劳无功。末了，母亲把自己的画像挂在「午夜济良所」的墙上——那是失足女子聚集之所。许多人从画像前匆匆一瞥。有一个在画像前留连。那是她童年时俯视着她的同一张慈爱的脸。母亲并未忘记、也未抛弃她那犯罪的孩子；否则她的画像不会挂上那面墙。那嘴唇似乎张开，低语：「回家来吧；我赦免你，我仍爱你。」那可怜的姑娘承受不住情感，瘫倒在地。她便是那浪女。母亲的面容击碎了她的心。她真心为罪痛悔，满怀悲伤与羞愧，回到那曾被她抛弃的家；母女重又团聚。

===Original===
But let me tell you that no mother’s love is to be compared with the love of God; it does not measure the height of the depth of God’s love. No mother in this world ever loved her child as God loves you and me. Think of the love that God must have had when He gave His Son to die for the world. I used to think a good deal more of Christ than I did of the Father. Somehow or other I had the idea that God was a stern judge; that Christ came between me and God, and appeased the anger of God. But after I became a father, and for years had an only son, as I looked at my boy I thought of the Father giving His Son to die; and it seemed to me as if it required more love for the Father to give His Son than for the Son to die. Oh, the love that God must have had for the world when He gave His Son to die for it! “God so loved the world, that He gave His only begotten Son, that whosoever believeth in Him should not perish, but have everlasting life” (John 3:16). I have never been able to preach from that text. I have often thought I would; but it is so high that I can never climb to its height; I have just quoted it and passed on. Who can fathom the depth of those words: “God so loved the world?” We can never scale the heights of His love or fathom its depths. Paul prayed that he might know the height, the depth, the length, and the breadth, of the love of God; but it was past his finding out. It “passeth knowledge” (Ephesians 3:19).

Nothing speaks to us of the love of God, like the cross of Christ. Come with me to Calvary, and look upon the Son of God as He hangs there. Can you hear that piercing cry from His dying lips: “Father, forgive them; for they know not what they do!” and say that He does not love you? “Greater love hath no man than this, that a man lay down his life for his friends” (John 15:13). But Jesus Christ laid down His life for his enemies.

===Chinese===
然而容我告诉你：任何母爱都无法与神的爱相比；它量不出神之爱的高深。这世上没有一个母亲爱自己的孩子，能像神爱你我那样。请想一想，神把祂的儿子赐下为世人而死，祂心中怀着的，是何等的爱！我从前对基督的看重，远胜过对父的重看。不知怎的，我总以为神是一位严厉的法官；是基督来到我与神之间，平息了神的怒气。可等我做了父亲，多年间只有独子一个，我看着我的孩子，便想到父把祂的儿子交出去受死；我觉着，父舍去子所需要的爱，似乎比子去受死所需要的爱更大。哦，神把祂的儿子赐下为之而死时，祂对世人的爱是何等的爱！「神爱世人，甚至将祂的独生子赐给他们，叫一切信祂的，不至灭亡，反得永生」（约翰福音 3:16）。我从未能就这节经文讲过道。我常想讲；可它太高，我永远攀不到它的高处；我只是引用了，便过去了。谁能测透「神爱世人」这句话的深呢？我们永远攀不到祂爱的高度，也测不透它的深处。保罗曾求能明白神之爱的高、深、长、阔；可它远超他所能寻究。它「过于人所能测度」（以弗所书 3:19）。

再没有一样东西，能像基督的十字架那样向我们述说神的爱。请随我到各各他，看那悬挂在上面的神之子。你能听见祂临死的嘴唇所发出的那声刺心的呼喊——「父啊，赦免他们，因为他们所做的，他们不晓得」——却说祂不爱你吗？「人为朋友舍命，人的爱心没有比这个大的」（约翰福音 15:13）。但耶稣基督是为*仇敌*舍命。

===Original===
Another thought is this: He loved us long before we ever thought of Him. The idea that he does not love us until we first love Him is not to be found in Scripture. In 1 John 4:10, it is written: “Herein is love, not that we loved God, but that He loved us, and sent His Son to be the propitiation for our sins.” He loved us before we ever thought of loving Him. You loved your children before they knew anything about your love. And so, long before we ever thought of God, we were in His thoughts.

What brought the prodigal home? It was the thought that his father loved him. Suppose the news had reached him that he was cast off, and that his father did not care for him any more, would he have gone back? Never! But the thought dawned upon him that his father loved him still: so he rose up, and went back to his home. Dear reader, the love of the Father ought to bring us back to Him. It was Adam’s calamity and sin that revealed God’s love. When Adam fell God came down and dealt in mercy with him. If anyone is lost it will not be because God does not love him: it will be because he has resisted the love of God.

===Chinese===
还有一个念头：在我们尚未想到祂之前，祂早已爱我们了。「祂要等到我们先爱祂，祂才爱我们」这种想法，在圣经里是找不到的。约翰一书 4:10 写着：「不是我们爱神，乃是神爱我们，差祂的儿子为我们的罪作了挽回祭，这就是爱了。」在我们尚未想到爱祂之前，祂已爱我们。你爱你的孩子，是在他们还对你这份爱一无所知之时。照样，在我们尚未想到神之前，我们已在祂的思念之中。

是什么把浪子带回家的？是「父亲爱我」这一念。倘若传到他耳中的消息是「你被赶出家门了，父亲再也不管你了」，他还会回去吗？绝不会！可是一缕念头在他心头亮起：父亲仍爱我；于是他起来，回到了家。亲爱的读者，父的爱本当把我们带回祂那里。正是亚当的灾厄与罪，显明了神的爱。亚当堕落时，神下来以慈悲待他。若有人终归沉沦，绝不会是因为神不爱他：必是因为他抗拒了神的爱。

===Original===
What will make Heaven attractive? Is it the pearly gates or the golden streets? No. Heaven will be attractive, because there we shall behold Him who loved us so much as to give His only-begotten Son to die for us. What makes home attractive? Is it the beautiful furniture and stately rooms? No; some homes with all these are like whited sepulchres. In Brooklyn a mother was dying; and it was necessary to take her child from her, because the little child could not understand the nature of the sickness, and disturbed her mother. Every night the child sobbed herself to sleep in a neighbor’s house, because she wanted to go back to her mother’s; but the mother grew worse, and they could not take the child home. At last the mother died; and after her death they thought it best not to let the child see her dead mother in her coffin. After the burial the child ran into one room crying “Mamma! mamma!” and then into another crying “Mamma! mamma!” and so went over the whole house: and when the little creature failed to find that loved one she cried to be taken back to the neighbors. So what makes heaven attractive is the thought that we shall see Christ who has loved us and given Himself for us.

If you ask me why God should love us, I cannot tell. I suppose it is because He is a true Father. It is His nature to love; just as it is the nature of the sun to shine. He wants you to share in that love. Do not let unbelief keep you away from Him. Do not think that, because you are a sinner, God does not love you, or care for you. He does! He wants to save you and bless you.

===Chinese===
天堂之所以令人向往，是因为什么呢？是珍珠的门、黄金的街吗？不。天堂之所以令人向往，是因为在那里我们将得见祂——那位爱我们爱到把独生子赐下为我们受死的主。家之所以令人向往，是因为什么呢？是漂亮的家具、气派的房间吗？不；有些家纵有这一切，却像粉饰的坟墓。布鲁克林有一位母亲临终；必须把孩子从她身边带走，因为孩子不懂病情，会打扰母亲。每夜孩子在邻家哭着入睡，因为想回母亲身边；可母亲的病越来越重，他们不能把孩子带回家。母亲终于死了；他们觉得最好不要让孩子看见棺材里的母亲。下葬之后，孩子跑进一个房间哭着「妈妈！妈妈！」又跑进另一个房间哭着「妈妈！妈妈！」——把整座房子都跑遍了；当这小小的人儿找不到那心爱的人儿，便哭着要被送回邻家。天堂之所以令人向往，正是这一念：我们将看见那爱我们、为我们舍己的基督。

你若问我，神为何爱我们，我说不上来。我想，是因为祂是一位真正的父。爱是祂的本性，正如发光是太阳的本性。祂愿你有分于这份爱。不要让不信把你挡在祂外面。不要以为，因为你是罪人，神便不爱你、不顾念你。祂爱！祂愿救你、赐福给你。

===Original===
“When we were yet without strength, in due time Christ died for the ungodly” (Romans 5:6). Is that not enough to convince you that He loves you? He would not have died for you if he had not loved you. Is your heart so hard that you can brace yourself up against His love, and spurn and despise it? You can do it; but it will be at your peril.

I can imagine some saying to themselves, “Yes, we believe that God loves us, if we love Him; we believe that God loves the pure and the holy.” Let me say, my friend, not only does God love the pure and the holy: He also loves the ungodly. “God commendeth His love toward us, in that, while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us” (Romans 5:8). God sent him to die for the sins of the whole world. If you belong to the world, then you have part and lot in this love that has been exhibited in the cross of Christ.

===Chinese===
「因我们还软弱的时候，基督就按所定的日期为罪人死」（罗马书 5:6）。这还不足以叫你信祂爱你吗？祂若不爱你，便不会为你死。你的心竟硬到能够挺身抗拒祂的爱，践踏它、鄙弃它吗？你*能*这样；但这要付上你的危险。

我能想象有人心里说：「是啊，我们相信神爱我们——只要我们先爱祂；我们相信神爱那清洁圣洁的人。」容我说，朋友，神不仅爱那清洁圣洁的：祂也爱那不虔不敬的人。「惟有基督在我们还作罪人的时候为我们死，神的爱就在此向我们显明了」（罗马书 5:8）。神差祂来，是为全世界的罪而死。你既属于这世界，便在这份借着基督十字架所显明的爱里有分。

===Original===
There is a passage in Revelation 1:5 which I think a great deal of—“Unto Him that loved us, and washed us.” It might be thought that God would first wash us, and then love us. But no, He first loved us. About eight years ago the whole country was intensely excited about Charlie Ross, a child of four years old, who was stolen. Two men in a gig asked him and an elder brother if they wanted some candy. They then drove away with the younger boy, leaving the elder one. For many years a search has been made in every State and territory. Men have been over to Great Britain, France, and Germany, and have hunted in vain for the child. The mother still lives in the hope that she will see her long lost Charlie. I never remember the whole country to have been so much agitated about any event unless it was the assassination of President Garfield. Well, suppose the mother of Charlie Ross were in some meeting; and that while the preacher was speaking, she happened to look down amongst the audience and see her long lost son. Suppose that he was poor, dirty and ragged, shoeless and coatless, what would she do? Would she wait till he was washed and decently clothed before she would acknowledge him? No, she would get off the platform at once, rush towards him and take him in her arms. After that she would cleanse and clothe him. So it is with God. He loved us, and washed us. I can imagine one saying, “If God loves me, why does He not make me good?” God wants sons and daughters in heaven; He does not want machines or slaves. He could break our stubborn hearts, but He wants to draw us towards Himself by the cords of love.

===Chinese===
启示录 1:5 有一节经文，我十分宝贝——「那爱我们，用自己的血洗净我们」。也许有人以为，神必是先洗净我们，然后才爱我们。不，祂是先爱了我们。约莫八年前，全国为一桩事激动万分——四岁的孩子查理·罗斯（Charlie Ross）被拐走了。两个男人驾着一辆双轮马车，问他和他哥哥要不要吃糖，然后载着小的那个扬长而去，丢下了大的。多年来，每一个州、每一片领地都在搜寻。有人远赴英国、法国、德国，徒劳地找这孩子。母亲仍活着，盼着能再见她那久失的查理。我所记忆中，举国为一事如此震动的，除加菲尔德总统（President Garfield）遇刺之外，再无第二桩。好，设想查理·罗斯的母亲某日在一处聚会里；讲员正在讲道，她无意中朝听众席一望，竟看见她那久失的儿子。设想他贫穷、肮脏、衣衫褴褛，赤脚无衣，她会怎样？她会等他洗净了、穿上体面衣裳才认他吗？不，她会立刻从讲台上下来，冲过去，把他拥进怀里。然后再去洗净他、给他穿衣。神也是这样。祂爱了我们，洗净了我们。我能想象有人说：「神若爱我，为何不叫我变好？」神所要的，是天上的儿子与女儿；祂不要机器，也不要奴隶。祂本可以击碎我们顽梗的心，但祂愿用爱的绳索把我们吸引到祂自己面前。

===Original===
He wanted you to sit down with Him at the marriage supper of the Lamb; to wash you, and make you whiter than snow. He wants you to walk with Him the crystal pavement of yonder blissful world. He wants to adopt you into His family; and to make you a son or a daughter of heaven. Will you trample His love under your feet? or will you, this hour, give yourself to Him?

When our terrible civil war was going on, a mother received the news that her boy had been wounded in the battle of the Wilderness. She took the first train, and started for her boy, although the order had gone forth from the War Department that no more women should be admitted within the lines. But a mother’s love knows nothing about orders so she managed by tears and entreaties to get through the lines to the Wilderness. At last she found the hospital where her boy was. Then she went to the doctor and she said: “Will you let me go to the ward and nurse my boy?”

The doctor said: “I have just got your boy to sleep; he is in a very critical state; and I am afraid if you wake him up the excitement will be so great that it will carry him off. You had better wait awhile, and remain without until I tell him that you have come, and break the news gradually to him.” The mother looked into the doctor’s face and said: “Doctor, supposing my boy does not wake up, and I should never see him alive! Let me go and sit down by his side; I won’t speak to him.” “If you will not speak to him you may do so,” said the doctor.

===Chinese===
祂愿你在羔羊的婚筵上与祂同坐；祂要洗净你，使你白如雪。祂愿你与祂在那边那有福之地的水晶地上同行。祂愿收纳你入祂的家，使你成为天上的儿子或女儿。你要把祂的爱踩在脚下吗？还是就在此时此刻，把自己交给祂？

我们那场惨烈的南北战争进行之时，一位母亲接到消息：她的孩子在莽原之役（the Wilderness）中受了伤。她搭上头一班火车，奔赴孩子那里，尽管陆军部已经下令：不许再有妇人进入战线之内。但母爱哪管什么命令，她靠着眼泪与哀求穿过了战线，到了莽原。她终于找到了孩子所在的医院。她去见医生，说：「您肯让我进病房去照看我的孩子吗？」

医生说：「我刚刚哄你的孩子睡下；他病情十分危急；我怕你一旦把他弄醒，那激动太大，会把他送掉。你还是等一会儿，先在外面待着，等我去告诉他你来了，把这消息慢慢透给他。」母亲望着医生的脸，说：「医生，要是我的孩子再也醒不过来，我岂不是再也见不到他活的样儿！让我去坐在他身旁吧；我不跟他说话。」「你若不跟他说话，那便随你，」医生说。

===Original===
She crept to the cot and looked into the face of her boy. How she had longed to look at him! How her eyes seemed to be feasting as she gazed upon his countenance! When she got near enough she could not keep her hands off; she laid that tender, loving hand upon his brow. The moment the hand touched the forehead of her boy, he, without opening his eyes, cried out: “Mother, you have come!” He knew the touch of that loving hand. There was love and sympathy in it.

Ah, sinner, if you feel the loving touch of Jesus you will recognize it; it is so full of tenderness. The world may treat you unkindly; but Christ never will. You will never have a better Friend in this world. What you need is—to come today to Him. Let His loving arm be underneath you; let His loving hand be about you; and He will hold you with mighty power. He will keep you, and fill that heart of yours with His tenderness and love.

===Chinese===
她悄悄走到床边，看着孩子的脸。她多么渴望能看他一眼！她注视着他的面容，眼睛像是在尽情享用！等她靠近了，她忍不住不伸手；她把那温柔慈爱的手，轻轻放在他额上。那只手一触到孩子的前额，他没睁眼，便喊出来：「妈妈，您来了！」他认得那只慈爱之手的触摸。那里头有爱，有怜恤。

啊，罪人，你若感到耶稣那慈爱的触摸，你必认得出来；它充满温柔。这世界也许会待你刻薄；但基督永不会。这世上你再也找不到更好的朋友。你所需要的——就是今日便到祂那里去。让祂慈爱的臂膀在你底下托着；让祂慈爱的手环绕着你；祂必用大能抓住你。祂必保守你，用祂的温柔与爱充满你那颗心。

===Original===
I can imagine some of you saying, “How shall I go to Him?” Why, just as you would go to your mother. Have you done your mother a great injury and a great wrong? If so, you go to her and you say, “Mother, I want you to forgive me.” Treat Christ in the same way. Go to Him today and tell Him that you have not loved Him, that you have not treated Him right; confess you sins, and see how quickly He will bless you.

I am reminded of another incident—that of a boy who had been tried by court-martial and ordered to be shot. The hearts of the father and mother were broken when they heard the news. In that home was a little girl. She had read the life of Abraham Lincoln, and she said: “Now, if Abraham Lincoln knew how my father and mother loved their boy, he would not let my brother be shot.” She wanted her father to go to Washington to plead for his boy. But the father said: “No; there is no use; the law must take its course. They have refused to pardon one or two who have been sentenced by that court-martial, and an order has gone forth that the President is not going to interfere again; if a man has been sentenced by court-martial he must suffer the consequences.” That father and mother had not faith to believe that their boy might be pardoned.

===Chinese===
我能想象你们中有人说：「我当怎样到祂那里去呢？」哎，就像你到你母亲那里去一样。你曾大大地伤害过、亏欠过你的母亲吗？若是，你便去到她那里，说：「母亲，我求您赦免我。」你待基督也当一样。今日便到祂那里去，告诉祂：你未曾爱祂，你未曾好好待祂；把你的罪一一认了，看祂是多么快地赐福给你。

我又想起另一桩事——一个少年被军法审判，判了枪决。父母闻讯，心碎了。那家里有一个小妹妹。她读过林肯（Abraham Lincoln）的传记，便说：「要是亚伯拉罕·林肯知道我爸爸妈妈多么爱他们的孩子，他就不会让我哥哥被枪毙。」她要父亲去华盛顿为这孩子求情。可父亲说：「不；没用；法律要照走它的程序。那军法法庭判过的一两个人，赦免的请求都被拒绝了；而且已经下令，总统不再插手；凡是军法法庭判了的，必得自食其果。」那一对父母没有信心，相信孩子也许能被赦免。

===Original===
But the little girl was strong in hope; she got on the train away up in Vermont, and started off to Washington. When she reached the White House the soldiers refused to let her in; but she told her pitiful story, and they allowed her to pass. When she got to the Secretary’s room, where the President’s private secretary was, he refused to allow her to enter the private office of the President. But the little girl told her story, and it touched the heart of the private secretary; so he passed her in. As she went into Abraham Lincoln’s room, there were United States senators, generals, governors and leading politicians, who were there about important business about the war; but the President happened to see that child standing at his door. He wanted to know what she wanted, and she went right to him and told her story in her own language. He was a father, and the great tears trickled down Abraham Lincoln’s cheeks. He wrote a dispatch and sent it to the army to have that boy sent to Washington at once. When he arrived, the President pardoned him, gave him thirty days furlough, and sent him home with the little girl to cheer the hearts of the father and mother.

Do you want to know how to go to Christ? Go just as that little girl went to Abraham Lincoln. It may be possible that you have a dark story to tell. Tell it all out; keep nothing back. If Abraham Lincoln had compassion on that little girl, heard her petition and answered it, do you think the Lord Jesus will not hear your prayer? Do you think that Abraham Lincoln, or any man that ever lived on earth, had as much compassion as Christ? No! He will be touched when no one else will; He will have mercy when no one else will; He will have pity when no one else will. If you will go right to Him, confessing your sin and your need, He will save you.

===Chinese===
可那小妹妹满怀盼望；她在远在佛蒙特（Vermont）便上了火车，动身去华盛顿。她到了白宫，士兵们不让她进；可她讲了那可怜的故事，他们便放她过去。她到了秘书的房间——总统私人秘书所在之处——他不许她进总统的私室。可那小女孩讲了她的故事，触动了私人秘书的心；他便放她进去。她走进亚伯拉罕·林肯的房间时，那里有合众国的参议员、将军、州长、政界要人，都是为战时要务而来；可总统恰巧看见那孩子站在门口。他想知道她要什么，她便径直走到他跟前，用自己的话讲了她的故事。他是一个父亲，大颗大颗的泪珠顺着亚伯拉罕·林肯的面颊滚下来。他写了一封电报，发到军中，叫立刻把那少年送到华盛顿。少年到了，总统赦免了他，给他三十天假，让他和小妹妹一起回家，去安慰父母的心。

你想要知道怎样到基督那里去吗？就像那小妹妹到亚伯拉罕·林肯那里去一样。你也许有一段黑暗的故事要讲。把它全讲出来；一点也不隐瞒。亚伯拉罕·林肯既怜恤那小妹妹，听了她的恳求又答应了，你想主耶稣会不听你的祷告吗？你以为亚伯拉罕·林肯，或世上曾活过的任何人，能有基督那样的怜悯吗？不！在别人都不动心之时，祂会动心；在别人都不施怜之时，祂会施怜；在别人都不加体恤之时，祂会体恤。你若径直到祂那里去，认你的罪、说出你的需要，祂必救你。

===Original===
A few years ago a man left England and went to America. He was an Englishman; but he was naturalized, and so became an American citizen. After a few years he felt restless and dissatisfied, and went to Cuba; and after he had been in Cuba a little while civil war broke out there; it was in 1867; and this man was arrested by the Spanish government as a spy. He was tried by court-martial, found guilty and ordered to be shot. The whole trial was conducted in the Spanish language, and the poor man did not know what was going on. When they told him the verdict, that he was found guilty and had been condemned to be shot, he sent to the American Consul and the English Consul, and laid the whole case before them, proving his innocence and claiming protection. They examined the case, and found that this man whom the Spanish officers had condemned to be shot was perfectly innocent; they went to the Spanish General and said, “Look here, this man whom you have condemned to death is an innocent man; he is not guilty.” But the Spanish General said, “He has been tried by our law; he has been found guilty; he must die.” There was no electric cable; and these men could not consult with their governments.

The morning came on which the man was to be executed. He was brought out sitting on his coffin in a cart, and drawn to the place where he was to be executed. A grave was dug. They took the coffin out of the cart, placed the young man upon it, took the black cap, and were just pulling it down over his face. The Spanish soldiers awaited the order to fire. But just then the American and English Consuls rode up. The English Consul sprang out of the carriage and took the union jack, the British flag, and wrapped it around the man, and the American Consul wrapped around him the star-spangled banner, and then turning to the Spanish officers they said: “Fire upon those flags if you dare.” They did not dare to fire upon the flags. There were two great governments behind those flags. That was the secret of it.

“He brought me to the banqueting house, and His banner over me was love.… His left hand is under my head, and His right hand doth embrace me” (Song of Solomon 2:4, 6). Thank God we can come under the banner today if we will. Any poor sinner can come under that banner today. His banner of love is over us. Blessed Gospel; blessed, precious, news. Believe it today; receive it into your heart; and enter into a new life. Let the love of God be shed abroad in your heart by the Holy Ghost today: it will drive away darkness; it will drive away gloom; it will drive away sin; and peace and joy shall be yours.

===Chinese===
几年前，一个人离开英国到了美洲。他是英国人；但他入了籍，成了美国公民。过了几年，他心绪不宁，去了古巴；他在古巴没住多久，那里便爆发了内战；那是 1867 年；这人被西班牙政府当作间谍逮捕。他被军法审判，判了有罪，定了枪决。整个审判用西班牙语进行，那可怜的人完全不知道在发生什么。他们把判决告诉他——他被判有罪、要被枪毙——他便派人去请美国领事和英国领事，把全部案情摆在他们面前，证明自己无辜，请求保护。他们审查了案情，发现这些西班牙军官判处枪决的这人完全无辜；他们去见西班牙将军，说：「请看，你们判处死刑的这人是个无辜的人；他无罪。」可西班牙将军说：「他已经按我们的法律受了审；判了有罪；他必死。」那时还没有电报电缆；这些人无法与各自的政府商量。

那人行刑的早晨到了。他被押出来，坐在一口棺材上，由一辆大车拉到行刑的地方。坟坑已挖好。他们把棺材从车上抬下，让那年轻人站在棺上，拿起黑色头罩，正要把它罩到他脸上。西班牙士兵等着开枪的命令。可就在这时，美英两国领事骑马赶到。英国领事从车上跳下，拿起联合杰克旗——英国国旗——把这人裹住；美国领事则把星条旗裹在他身上，然后转向西班牙军官，说：「你们敢向这两面旗开枪，便开。」他们不敢向这两面旗开枪。这两面旗背后是两个大国。这便是其中的关键。

「他带我入筵宴所，以爱为旗在我以上。……他左手在我头下，他右手将我抱住」（雅歌 2:4, 6）。感谢神，我们今日若愿意，便可来到这面旗下。任何可怜的罪人，今日都可以来到这面旗下。祂以爱为旗，在我们以上。有福的福音；有福的、宝贵的消息。今日便信它；领它进你的心；进入一个新的生命。今日便让神的爱借圣灵浇灌在你心里：它要驱走黑暗；它要驱走阴郁；它要驱走罪恶；平安与喜乐必归于你。

## II — The Gateway Into the Kingdom / II —— 进入国度的门径

> 「人若不重生，就不能见神的国。」
> ——约翰福音 3:3

===Original===
There is no portion of the Word of God, perhaps, with which we are more familiar than this passage. I suppose if I were to ask those in any audience if they believed that Jesus Christ taught the doctrine of the New Birth, nine tenths of them would say: “Yes, I believe He did.”

Now if the words of this text are true they embody one of the most solemn questions that can come before us. We can afford to be deceived about many things rather than about this one thing. Christ makes it very plain. He says, “Except a man be born again, he cannot *see* the Kingdom of God”—much less inherit it. This doctrine of the New Birth is therefore the foundation of all our hopes for the world to come. It is really the A B C of the Christian religion. My experience has been this—that if a man is unsound on this doctrine he will be unsound on almost every other fundamental doctrine in the Bible. A true understanding of this subject will help a man to solve a thousand difficulties that he may meet with in the Word of God. Things that before seemed very dark and mysterious will become very plain.

===Chinese===
神的话语中，也许再没有哪一段比这一段我们更熟悉的了。我想，无论在什么样的会众面前，我若问他们：耶稣基督是不是教导过「重生」这道理？十之八九的人会答：「是的，我相信祂教导过。」

那么，这一节经文若是真的，它就囊括了能摆在我们面前的最严肃的问题之一。我们在许多事上受骗尚可，唯独在这一件事上不可受骗。基督说得很明白。祂说：「人若不重生，就*不能见*神的国」——更不用说承受它了。所以这「重生」的道理，乃是我们对来世一切盼望的根基。它实在是基督教的 A B C。我的经验是——一个人若在这一条道理上站得不稳，他在圣经里几乎每一条基本道理上都会站不稳。真正明白这一题目，能帮助一个人解开他在神的话语中所遇见的千般疑难。从前看着幽暗难明的事，会变得十分明白。

===Original===
The doctrine of the New Birth upsets all false religion—all false views about the Bible and about God. A friend of mine once told me that in one of his after-meetings, a man came to him with a long list of questions written out for him to answer. He said: “If you can answer these questions satisfactorily, I have made up my mind to be a Christian.” “Do you not think,” said my friend, “that you had better come to Christ first? Then you can look into these questions.” The man thought that perhaps he had better do so. After he had received Christ, he looked again at his list of questions; but then it seemed to him as if they had all been answered. Nicodemus came with his troubled mind, and Christ said to him, “Ye must be born again.” He was treated altogether differently from what he expected; but I venture to say that was the most blessed night in all his life. To be “born again” is the greatest blessing that will ever come to us in this world.

===Chinese===
「重生」的道理推翻一切虚假的宗教——推翻一切对圣经、对神的错谬看法。我有一位朋友曾告诉我，有一次散会后，一个人拿着一张写满问题的单子来找他作答。那人说：「你若能把这些题目答得叫我满意，我就决心做基督徒了。」我朋友说：「你想，你不该先到基督这里来吗？来了再看这些问题。」那人想想，也觉得这样更好。他既接受了基督，再回头看那张问题单；可这一回，那些问题仿佛都已自行解答了。尼哥底母（Nicodemus）心怀困扰地来，基督对他说：「你们必须重生。」他所受的对待，与他所预料的截然不同；可我敢说，那一夜是他一生中最蒙福的一夜。「重生」是我们在今世所能得的、最大的福气。

===Original===
Notice how the Scripture puts it. “Except a man be born again,” “born from above,” “born of the Spirit.” From amongst a number of other passages where we find this word “**except**,” I would just name three. “Except ye repent, ye shall all likewise perish.” (Luke 13:3, 5) “Except ye be converted, and become as little children, ye shall not enter into the kingdom of heaven.” (Matthew 18:3) “Except your righteousness shall exceed the righteousness of the Scribes and Pharisees, ye shall in no case enter into the kingdom of heaven.” (Matthew 5:20) They all really mean the same thing.

I am so thankful that our Lord spoke of the New Birth to this ruler of the Jews, this doctor of the law, rather than to the woman at the well of Samaria, or to Matthew the publican, or to Zaccheus. If He had reserved his teaching on this great matter for these three, or such as these, people would have said: “Oh yes, these publicans and harlots need to be converted: but I am an upright man; I do not need to be converted.” I suppose Nicodemus was one of the best specimens of the people of Jerusalem: there was nothing on record against him.

===Chinese===
请注意圣经是怎样说的。「人若不重生」「从上面生」「从灵而生」。在圣经若干处出现「**若不**」二字的地方，我只举三处。「你们若不悔改，都要如此灭亡。」（路加福音 13:3, 5）「你们若不回转，变成小孩子的样式，断不得进天国。」（马太福音 18:3）「你们的义若不胜于文士和法利赛人的义，断不能进天国。」（马太福音 5:20）它们其实指的都是同一件事。

我何等感谢主，竟把「重生」的道理对这位犹太人的官、这位律法师讲，而不是对撒玛利亚井旁的妇人、对税吏马太、对撒该讲。倘若祂把这大道理只留给这三位、或这一类人，人们就会说：「哦，是啊，这些税吏和娼妓是当悔改；可我是正直的人，我不需要悔改。」我想尼哥底母可算是耶路撒冷人中的佼佼者了：他没有任何案底。

===Original===
I think it is scarcely necessary for me to prove that we need to be born again before we are meet for heaven. I venture to say that there is no candid man but would say he is not fit for the kingdom of God, until he is born of another Spirit. The Bible teaches us that man by nature is lost and guilty, and our experience confirms this. We know also that the best and holiest man, if he turn away from God, will very soon fall into sin.

Now, let me say what Regeneration is not. It is not going to church. Very often I see people, and ask them if they are Christians. “Yes, of course I am; at least, I think I am: I go to church every Sunday.” Ah, but this is not Regeneration. Others say, “I am trying to do what is right—am I not a Christian? Is not that a new birth?” No. What has that to do with being born again? There is yet another class—those who have “turned over a new leaf,” and think they are regenerated. No; forming a new resolution is not being born again.

===Chinese===
我想，我们需重生之后才配进天堂，这一层几乎不必再证明。我敢说，没有一个坦诚的人不肯承认：在他受了另一位圣灵而生之前，他不配进神的国。圣经教导我们：人按本性是失丧、是有罪的，而我们的经验也证实了这一点。我们也晓得：最好最圣洁的人，若转脸不认神，也很快会陷入罪中。

那么，让我说「重生」不是什么。重生不是上教堂。我常常见人，问他们是不是基督徒。「是啊，当然是；至少我以为是：我每个礼拜天都上教堂。」哎，可这不是重生。另一些人说：「我尽力做对的事——难道我不是基督徒？这不就是新生吗？」不是。这与重生何干？还有一类——那些「翻开了新的一页」、就以为自己重生了的。不；立一个新的志向不等于重生。

===Original===
Nor will being baptized do you any good. Yet you hear people say, “Why, I have been baptized; and I was born again when I was baptized.” They believe that because they were baptized into the church, they were baptized into the Kingdom of God. I tell you that it is utterly impossible. You may be baptized into the church, and yet not be baptized into the Son of God. Baptism is all right in its place. God forbid that I should say anything against it. But if you put that in the place of Regeneration—in the place of the New Birth—it is a terrible mistake. You cannot be baptized into the Kingdom of God. “Except a man be **born again**, he cannot see the Kingdom of God.” If anyone reading this rests his hopes on anything else—on any other foundation—I pray that God may sweep it away.

Another class say, “I go to the Lord’s Supper; I partake uniformly of the Sacrament.” Blessed ordinance! Jesus hath said that as often as ye do it ye commemorate His death. Yet, that is not being “born again;” that is not passing from death unto life. Jesus says plainly—and so plainly that there need not be any mistake about it—“Except a man be born of the Spirit, he cannot enter into the Kingdom of God.” What has a sacrament to do with that? What has going to church to do with being born again?

===Chinese===
受洗也不能救你。可你听人说：「咦，我受过洗了；我受洗的时候就是重生了。」他们以为，既然受了洗归入教会，便是受洗归入了神的国。我告诉你，这是万万不能的。你可以受洗归入教会，却仍未归入神之子。洗礼在它该在的位置上没有错。我断不至说反对它的话。可你若把洗礼摆在「重生」的位置上——摆在「新生」的位置上——那就是可怕的错误。你不能受洗进入神的国。「人若不**重生**，就不能见神的国。」倘若读这段话的任何人，把指望寄托在别的事物上——别的根基上——我求神把它扫除。

另一些人说：「我守圣餐；我每次都领圣礼。」蒙福的圣礼啊！耶稣说过，你们每逢这样做，便是记念祂的死。然而，那不是「重生」；那不是出死入生。耶稣说得明明白白——明白到不会有任何误解的地步——「人若不是从灵生的，就不能进神的国。」圣礼与这事何干？上教堂与重生何干？

===Original===
Another man comes up and says, “I say my prayers regularly.” Still I say that is not being born of the Spirit. It is a very solemn question, then, that comes up before us; and oh! that every reader would ask himself earnestly and faithfully: “Have I been born again? Have I been born of the Spirit? Have I passed from death unto life?”

There is a class of men who say that special religious meetings are very good for a certain class of people. They would be very good if you could get the drunkard there, or get the gambler there, or get other vicious people there—that would do a great deal of good. But “we do not need to be converted.” To whom did Christ utter these words of wisdom? To Nicodemus. Who was Nicodemus? Was he a drunkard, a gambler, or a thief? No! No doubt he was one of the very best men in Jerusalem. He was an honorable Councillor; he belonged to the Sanhedrim; he held a very high position; he was an orthodox man; he was one of the very soundest men. And yet what did Christ say to him? “Except a man be born again, he cannot see the kingdom of God.”

===Chinese===
又一个人走过来说：「我按时祷告。」我还是要说，这不是从灵而生。于是摆在我们面前的，是一个极庄严的问题；哦！但愿每一位读者都诚恳、忠实地问自己：「我重生了吗？我从灵而生了吗？我出死入生了吗？」

有一类人说，那些特别的奋兴聚会，对某一类人很有好处。要是你能把醉汉弄去，把赌徒弄去，把其他作恶的人弄去——那能大有好处。可是「我们用不着悔改。」基督这番智慧之语，是对谁说的？对尼哥底母。尼哥底母是谁？他是醉汉、赌徒，还是窃贼？不！他无疑是耶路撒冷最好的人之一。他是受人尊敬的议士；他属于公会（Sanhedrim）；他地位崇高；他是正统的人；他是最稳健的人之一。然而基督对他说了什么？「人若不重生，就不能见神的国。」

===Original===
But I can imagine someone saying, “What am I to do? I cannot create life. I certainly cannot save myself.” You certainly cannot; and we do not claim that you can. We tell you it is utterly impossible to make a man better without Christ; but that is what men are trying to do. They are trying to patch up this “old Adam” nature. **There must be a new creation**. Regeneration is a new creation; and if it is a new creation it must be the work of God. In the first chapter of Genesis man does not appear. There is no one there but God. Man is not there to take part. When God created the earth He was alone. When Christ redeemed the world He was alone.

“That which is born of the flesh is flesh; and that which is born of the Spirit is spirit.” (John 3:6) The Ethiopian cannot change his skin, and the leopard cannot change his spots. You might as well try to make yourselves pure and holy without the help of God. It would be just as easy for you to do that as for the black man to wash himself white. A man might just as well try to leap over the moon as to serve God in the flesh. Therefore, “that which is born of the flesh is flesh; and that which is born of the Spirit is spirit.”

===Chinese===
可我能想象有人说：「那我该怎么办？我不能创造生命。我当然不能自救。」你当然不能；我们也不说你能够。我们告诉你，没有基督，要让一个人变好，是绝无可能的；可人们正试图这样做的。他们想修补这「老旧亚当」的本性。**必须有一个新的创造**。重生就是新的创造；既是新创造，就必是神的作为。在创世记第一章里，人尚未出场。那里除了神之外没有别人。人不在场，无分于其中。神创造大地时，祂独自一人。基督救赎世界时，祂也是独自一人。

「从肉身生的就是肉身；从灵生的就是灵。」（约翰福音 3:6）古实人不能改变他的肤色，豹子不能改变它的斑点。你想不靠神的帮助而使自己纯洁圣洁，也是同样不可能。你做这事，正如黑人想把自己洗成白人一样难。一个人想凭肉身事奉神，不如说他想一蹦跳过月亮。所以，「从肉身生的就是肉身；从灵生的就是灵。」

===Original===
Now God tells us in this chapter how we are to get into His kingdom. We are not to work our way in—not but that salvation is worth working for. We admit all that. If there were rivers and mountains in the way, it would be well worth while to swim those rivers, and climb those mountains. There is no doubt that salvation is worth all that effort; but we do not obtain it by our works. It is “to him that worketh not, but believeth” (Romans 4:5). We work because we are saved; we do not work to be saved. We work from the cross; but not towards it. It is written, “Work out your own salvation with fear and trembling” (Philippians 2:12). Why, you must have your salvation before you can work it out. Suppose I say to my little boy, “I want you to spend that hundred dollars carefully.” “Well,” he says, “let me have the hundred dollars; and I will be careful how I spend it.” I remember when I first left home and went to Boston; I had spent all my money, and I went to the post-office three times a day. I knew there was only one mail a day from home; but I thought by some possibility there might be a letter for me. At last I received a letter from my little sister; and oh, how glad I was to get it. She had heard that there were a great many pickpockets in Boston, and a large part of that letter was to urge me to be very careful not to let anybody pick my pocket. Now I required to have something in my pocket before I could have it picked. So you must have salvation before you can work it out.

===Chinese===
那么，神在这一章里告诉我们怎样进入祂的国。我们不是凭做工进去的——并不是说救恩不值得一做去求。这一切我们都承认。即便有江河、有高山挡路，泅过那些江河、翻过那些高山，也是值得的。救恩无疑配得上这一切努力；可我们不是靠行为得着它。那是「那不作工的，只信」（罗马书 4:5）。我们做工，是因为已经得救；我们做工，不是为要得救。我们是从十字架出发做工；而不是朝它做工。经上记着：「当恐惧战兢做成你们得救的工夫」（腓立比书 2:12）。哎呀，你必须先有救恩，才能把它做出来。设想我对我的小儿子说：「我要你小心地花掉那一百块。」「好啊，」他说，「先把那一百块给我；我自会小心地花。」我还记得我头一回离家去波士顿（Boston）；钱花光了，我一天跑三趟邮局。我明知家里一天只来一封信；可我总想着也许有我的一封。终于我收到了小妹妹的一封信；哎呀，接到它我多高兴啊。她听说波士顿扒手很多，信里大半都是叮嘱我千万小心别让人扒了口袋。可我得先在口袋里有东西，才能被人扒。所以你必须先有救恩，才能把它做出来。

===Original===
When Christ cried out on Calvary, “It is finished!” He meant what He said. All that men have to do now is just to accept of the work of Jesus Christ. There is no hope for man or woman so long as they are trying to work out salvation for themselves. I can imagine there are some people who will say, as Nicodemus possibly did, “This is a very mysterious thing.” I see the scowl on that Pharisee’s brow as he says, “How can these things be?” It sounds very strange to his ear. “Born again; born of the Spirit! How can these things be?” A great many people say, “You must reason it out; but if you do not reason it out, do not ask us to believe it.” I can imagine a great many people saying that. When you ask me to reason it out, I tell you frankly I cannot do it. “The wind bloweth where it listeth, and thou hearest the sound thereof, but canst not tell whence it cometh and whither it goeth: so is every one that is born of the Spirit.” (John 8). I do not understand everything about the wind. You ask me to reason it out. I cannot. It may blow due north here, and a hundred miles away due south. I may go up a few hundred feet, and find it blowing in an entirely opposite direction from what it is down here. You ask me to explain these currents of wind; but suppose that, because I cannot explain them, and do not understand them, I were to take my stand and assert, “Oh, there is no such thing as wind.” I can imagine some little girl saying, “I know more about it than that man does; often have I heard the wind, and felt it blowing against my face;” and she might say, “Did not the wind blow my umbrella out of my hands the other day? and did I not see it blow a man’s hat off in the street? Have I not seen it blow the trees in the forest, and the growing corn in the country?”

===Chinese===
基督在各各他喊出「成了！」的时候，祂是当真的。如今人所要做的，只是接受耶稣基督所做成的工作。只要人还想靠自己做出来救恩，就毫无指望。我能想象有些人——也许像尼哥底母那样——会说：「这事太奥秘了。」我看见那法利赛人皱起眉头说：「怎能有这事呢？」这话在他听来十分生疏。「重生；从灵而生！怎能有这事呢？」许多人说：「你得把它推理出来；可你要推理不出来，就别叫我们信。」我能想象许多人这样说。你叫我把它推理出来，我坦白告诉你，我做不到。「风随着意思吹，你听见风的响声，却不晓得从哪里来，往哪里去；凡从灵生的，也是如此。」（约翰福音 3:8）。风的事，我不是什么都懂。你叫我推理。我不能。它也许在这儿正往北吹，一百英里外却正往南吹。我也许往上爬几百英尺，便发觉它吹的方向与下面完全相反。你叫我解释这些气流；可设想一下，正因为我不懂、不能解释，便站定脚跟声称：「哦，根本没有风这种东西。」我能想象一位小女孩说：「这事我比那位先生懂得多；我常听见风，感到它吹在我脸上；」她也许还会说：「前些日子不是风把我的伞吹脱了手吗？我不是看见它把街上一位先生的帽子吹掉了吗？我不是看见它吹动林中的树、乡间生长的庄稼吗？」

===Original===
You might just as well tell me that there is no such thing as wind, as tell me there is no such thing as a man being born of the Spirit. I have felt the spirit of God working in my heart, just as really and as truly as I have felt the wind blowing in my face. I cannot reason it out. There are a great many things I cannot reason out, but which I believe. I never could reason out the creation. I can see the world, but I cannot tell how God made it out of nothing. But almost every man will admit there was a creative power.

There are a great many things that I cannot explain and cannot reason out, and yet that I believe. I heard a commercial traveler say that he had heard that the ministry and religion of Jesus Christ were matters of revelation and not of investigation. “When it pleased God to reveal His Son in Me,” says Paul (Galatians 1:15–16). There was a party of young men together, going up the country; and on their journey they made up their minds not to believe anything they could not reason out. An old man heard them; and presently he said, “I heard you say you would not believe anything you could not reason out.” “Yes,” they said, “that is so.” “Well,” he said, “coming down on the train today, I noticed some geese, some sheep, some swine, and some cattle all eating grass. Can you tell me by what process that same grass was turned into hair, feathers, bristles and wool? Do you believe it is a fact?” “Oh yes,” they said, “we cannot help believing that, though we fail to understand it.” “Well,” said the old man, “I cannot help believing in Jesus Christ.” And I cannot help believing in the regeneration of man, when I see men who have been reclaimed, when I see men who have been reformed. Have not some of the very worst men been regenerated—been picked up out of the pit, and had their feet set upon the Rock, and a new song put in their mouths? Their tongues were cursing and blaspheming; and now are occupied in praising God. Old things have passed away, and all things have become new. They are not reformed only, but **regenerated**—new men in Christ Jesus.

===Chinese===
你说没有「人从灵而生」这回事，无异于告诉我没有风这回事。我感受过神的灵在我心里动工，正如我真真切切感受过风吹在我脸上一样。我推理不出来。有许多事我推理不出来，可我信。我向来推理不出创造。我能看见这个世界，却说不清神是怎样从无中造出它来的。可几乎人人都会承认，有一种创造的大能。

有许多事我不能解释、推理不出来，可我信。我曾听一位旅行推销员说，他听说耶稣基督的职事与宗教，是启示的事，不是考查的事。「神既然乐意将祂儿子启示在我心里」（加拉太书 1:15–16）。有一伙年轻人结伴往乡间去；一路上他们打定主意，凡推理不出来的事概不相信。一位老人听见了；过了一会儿他说：「我听你们说，凡推理不出来的，你们都不信。」「是啊，」他们说，「是这样。」「好吧，」他说，「我今儿坐火车下来，看见一些鹅、一些羊、一些猪、一些牛，都在吃草。你们能告诉我，那同一片草是怎样变成毛、羽毛、鬃毛和羊毛的吗？你们信不信这是事实？」「哦，信啊，」他们说，「我们不能不信，虽然我们不懂。」「好，」老人说，「我也不能不信耶稣基督。」而我也不能不信人的重生——因为我看见过那些被挽回的人，看见过那些被改造的人。一些极坏的人，岂不也被重生了——被从坑里拉起来，他们的脚被立在磐石上，他们口里被放上新车？他们的舌头从前是咒骂亵渎的；如今则用来赞美神。旧事已过，都变成新的了。他们不只是被改造，更是**重生**了——在基督耶稣里的新人。

===Original===
Down there in the dark alleys of one of our great cities is a poor drunkard. I think if you want to get near hell, you should go to a poor drunkard’s home. Go to the house of that poor miserable drunkard. Is there anything more like hell on earth? See the want and distress that reign there. But hark! A footstep is heard at the door, and the children run and hide themselves. The patient wife waits to meet the man. He has been her torment. Many a time she has borne about the marks of his blows for weeks. Many a time that strong right hand has been brought down on her defenseless head. And now she waits expecting to hear his oaths and suffer his brutal treatment. He comes in and says to her: “I have been to the meeting; and I heard there that if I will I can be converted. I believe that God is able to save me.” Go down to that house again in a few weeks: and what a change! As you approach you hear someone singing. It is not the song of a reveller, but the strains of that good old hymn, “Rock of Ages.” The children are no longer afraid of the man, but cluster around his knee. His wife is near him, her face lit up with a happy glow. Is not that a picture of Regeneration? I can take you to many such homes, made happy by the regenerating power of the religion of Christ. What men want is the power to overcome temptation, the power to lead a right life.

===Chinese===
我们某座大城的阴暗巷子里，有一个可怜的醉汉。我想，你若想靠近地狱，就该到可怜醉汉的家里去看看。到那可怜悲惨的醉汉的家里去。世上还有比这更像地狱的地方吗？看看那里笼罩着的穷困与愁苦。可是你听！门口传来一阵脚步声，孩子们一哄而散躲起来。那忍耐的妻子等着迎他。他是她的折磨。多少回，她身上带着他拳脚的青紫，一帯就是几个礼拜。多少回，那只强壮的右手落在她毫无防备的头上。如今她又等着，等着听他的咒骂、受他的粗暴。他进门来，对她说：「我去聚会了；我听见那里说，只要我愿意，就能悔改。我相信神能救我。」过几个礼拜再到那屋里去看：变样了！你还没走近就听见有人唱歌。那不是纵酒者的歌，而是那首老圣诗《万古磐石》（Rock of Ages）的旋律。孩子们不再怕这人，而是围在他膝前。他的妻子在他身旁，脸上泛着幸福的光。这岂不是重生的图画吗？我能带你去看许许多多这样的家——因基督之教的重生之能而变得快乐的家。人所要的，是胜过试探的能力，是过正直生活的能力。

===Original===
The only way to get into the kingdom of God is to be “born” into it. The law of this country requires that the President should be born in the country. When foreigners come to our shores they have no right to complain against such a law, which forbids them from ever becoming Presidents. Now, has not God a right to make a law that all those who become heirs of eternal life must be “born” into His kingdom?

An unregenerated man would rather be in hell than in heaven. Take a man whose heart is full of corruption and wickedness, and place him in heaven among the pure, the holy and the redeemed; and he would not want to stay there. Certainly, if we are to be happy in heaven we must begin to make a heaven here on earth. Heaven is a prepared place for a prepared people. If a gambler or a blasphemer were taken out of the streets of New York and placed on the crystal pavement of heaven and under the shadow of the tree of life, he would say, “I do not want to stay here.” If men were taken to heaven just as they are by nature, without having their hearts regenerated, there would be another rebellion in heaven. Heaven is filled with a company of those who have been **twice born**.

===Chinese===
进入神之国的唯一办法，是「生」进去。我国法律要求总统必须在本国出生。外国人来到我们的海岸，无权抱怨这样一条不许他们日后当总统的法律。那么，神难道没有权立一条律法，规定凡承受永生的人都必须「生」入祂的国吗？

一个未重生的人，宁可下地狱，也不愿上天堂。取一个满心腐败奸恶的人，把他放到天堂里、放在纯洁圣洁和得赎的人中间；他也不愿待在那儿。当然，我们要在天堂快活，就得先在地上把天堂动起来。天堂是为预备好的人预备的地方。倘若一个赌徒或一个亵渎者，从纽约街头被提去、放在天堂的水晶地上、放在生命树的荫下，他会说：「我不想待在这儿。」倘若人按其本性、心未重生便被提去天堂，天堂里就要再起一场叛乱。天堂里坐满了一群**两次出生**的人。

===Original===
In the 14th and 15th verses of this chapter we read “As Moses lifted up the serpent in the wilderness, even so must the Son of Man be lifted up; that **whosoever** believeth in Him should not perish, but have eternal life.” “**Whosoever.**” Mark that! Let me tell you who are unsaved what God has done for you. He has done everything that He could do toward your salvation. You need not wait for God to do anything more. In one place he asks the question, what more could he have done (Isaiah 5:4). He sent His prophets, and they killed them; then He sent His beloved Son, and they murdered Him. Now He has sent the Holy Spirit to convince us of sin, and to show how we are to be saved.

In this chapter we are told how men are to be saved, namely, by Him who was lifted up on the cross. Just as Moses lifted up the brazen serpent in the wilderness, so must the Son of Man be lifted up, “that whosoever believeth in Him should not perish, but have eternal life.” Some men complain and say that it is very unreasonable that they should be held responsible for the sin of a man six thousand years ago. It was not long ago that a man was talking to me about this injustice, as he called it. If a man thinks he is going to answer God in that way, I tell you it will not do him any good. If you are lost, it will not be on account of Adam’s sin.

===Chinese===
本章 14、15 节我们读到：「摩西在旷野怎样举蛇，人子也必照样被举起来；叫**一切**信祂的都得永生。」「**一切**。」请记住！让我告诉你们这些未得救的人，神为你们做了什么。祂已经做了祂所能做的一切，为着你们的救恩。你不必等神再做别的了。祂在一处问过：我还能做什么呢（以赛亚书 5:4）。祂差遣先知来，他们杀了；于是祂差遣祂的爱子来，他们谋杀了祂。如今祂差遣圣灵来，叫我们知罪，又指明我们当怎样得救。

这一章告诉我们，人是怎样得救的——即借着那位被举起在十字架上的。正如摩西在旷野举起铜蛇，人子也必照样被举起来，「叫一切信祂的，不至灭亡，反得永生。」有人抱怨说，要他们为一个六千年前的人的罪负责，很不合理。不久前还有一个人跟我谈论他所称的这种不公。一个人若想这样回答神，我告诉你，对他没有半点好处。你若沉沦，绝不会是因为亚当的罪。

===Original===
Let me illustrate this; and perhaps you will be better able to understand it. Suppose I am dying of consumption, which I inherited from my father or mother. I did not get the disease by any fault of my own, by any neglect of my health; I inherited it, let us suppose. A friend happens to come along: he looks at me, and says: “Moody, you are in a consumption.” I reply, “I know it very well; I do not want anyone to tell me that.” “But,” he says, “there is a remedy.” “But, sir, I do not believe it. I have tried the leading physicians in this country and in Europe; and they tell me there is no hope.” “But you know me, Moody; you have known me for years.” “Yes, sir.” “Do you think, then, I would tell you a falsehood?” “No.” “Well, ten years ago I was as far gone. I was given up by the physicians to die; but I took this medicine and it cured me. I am perfectly well: look at me.” I say that it is “a very strange case.” “Yes, it may be strange; but it is a fact. This medicine cured me: take this medicine, and it will cure you. Although it has cost me a great deal, it shall not cost you anything. Do not make light of it, I beg of you.” “Well,” I say, “I should like to believe you; but this is contrary to my reason.”

===Chinese===
让我打个比方；也许你就更容易明白。设想我正患着肺痨，是从父亲或母亲那里遗传来的。我得这病并非出于自己的过失，并非亏待了自己的身子；是遗传来的，我们姑且这样假设。一位朋友恰巧路过：他瞧瞧我，说：「慕迪，你害了痨病。」我答：「我清楚得很；不劳谁告诉我。」「可是，」他说，「有药可治。」「可是先生，我不信。我找过本国和欧洲的名医；他们都说没指望了。」「可你认识我，慕迪；你认识我多年了。」「是的，先生。」「那么，你以为我会跟你说谎吗？」「不会。」「那么，十年前我也病到这步田地。医生都放弃我了，让我等死；可我吃了这药，它治好了我。我全好了：你瞧我。」我说：「这倒是奇事一桩。」「是啊，也许奇；可这是事实。这药治好了我：服下这药，它也能治好你。它虽花了我许多，却不收你分文。求你别小瞧它。」「嗯，」我说，「我倒愿意信你；可这与我的理性相悖。」

===Original===
Hearing this, my friend goes away and returns with another friend, and that one testifies to the same thing. I am still disbelieving; so he goes away, and brings in another friend, and another, and another, and another; and they all testify to the same thing. They say they were as bad as myself; that they took the same medicine that has been offered to me; and that it has cured them. My friend then hands me the medicine. I dash it to the ground; I do not believe in its saving power; I die. The reason is then that I spurned the remedy. So, if you perish, it will not be because Adam fell; but because you spurned the remedy offered to save you. You will choose darkness rather than light. “How then shall ye escape, if ye neglect so great salvation?” There is no hope for you if you neglect the remedy. It does no good to look at the wound. If we had been in the Israelitish camp and had been bitten by one of the fiery serpents, it would have done us no good to look at the wound. Looking at the wound will never save anyone. What you must do is to look at the Remedy—look away to Him who hath power to save you from your sin.

===Chinese===
听了这话，我朋友走开，又带了一位朋友回来，那一位也作同样的见证。我仍是不信；于是他又走开，又带进来一位，又一位，又一位，又一位；他们都作同样的见证。他们说他们曾和我一样病重；他们说他们服了摆在我面前的同一味药；他们说它治好了他们。我朋友于是把药递给我。我把它摔在地上；我不信它的救命之能；我死了。原因便在于我拒绝了那药。所以，你若灭亡，不会是因为亚当堕落了；而是因为你拒绝了那摆在面前救你的药。你会选择黑暗，不爱光明。「我们若忽略这么大的救恩，怎能逃罪呢？」你若忽略这药，便毫无指望。光盯伤口没用。倘若我们在以色列人的营中、被火蛇咬了，光盯伤口也无济于事。盯伤口救不了人。你当做的是去看那药——转眼望向那位有能力救你脱离罪的主。

===Original===
Behold the camp of the Israelites; look at the scene that is pictured to your eyes! Many are dying because they neglect the remedy that is offered. In that arid desert is many a short and tiny grave; many a child has been bitten by the fiery serpents. Fathers and mothers are bearing away their children. Over yonder they are just burying a mother; a loved mother is about to be laid in the earth. All the family, weeping, gather around the beloved form. You hear the mournful cries; you see the bitter tears. The father is being borne away to his last resting place. There is wailing going up all over the camp. Tears are pouring down for thousands who have passed away; thousands more are dying; and the plague is raging from one end of the camp to the other.

===Chinese===
请看以色列人的营；请看摆在眼前的这一幕！许多人正死去，因为他们忽略了那药。在那干旱的旷野里，有许许多多小小的、短短的坟；许多孩子被火蛇咬了。父母们正抬走他们的孩子。在那边，他们正埋葬一位母亲；一位受敬爱的母亲将要入土。全家哭着，围拢那亲爱的人。你听见悲号；你看见苦泪。父亲正被抬往他最后的安息之所。整座营都在哀号。为已逝的千万而流下的泪如雨下；还有千万正在死去；瘟疫从营的这头肆虐到那头。

===Original===
I see in one tent an Israelitish mother bending over the form of a beloved boy just coming into the bloom of life, just budding into manhood. She is wiping away the sweat of death that is gathering upon his brow. Yet a little while, and his eyes are fixed and glassy, for life is ebbing fast away. The mother’s heartstrings are torn and bleeding. All at once she hears a noise in the camp. A great shout goes up. What does it mean? She goes to the door of the tent. “What is the noise in the camp?” she asks those passing by. And someone says: “Why, my good woman, have you not heard the good news that has come into the camp?” “No,” says the woman, “Good news! What is it?” “Why, have you not heard about it? God has provided a remedy.” “What! for the bitten Israelites? Oh, tell me what the remedy is!” “Why, God has instructed Moses to make a brazen serpent, and to put it on a pole in the middle of the camp; and He has declared that whosoever looks upon it shall live. The shout that you hear is the shout of the people when they see the serpent lifted up.” The mother goes back into the tent, and she says: “My boy, I have good news to tell you. You need not die! My boy, my boy, I have come with good tidings; you can live!” He is already getting stupefied; he is so weak he cannot walk to the door of the tent. She puts her strong arms under him and lifts him up. “Look yonder; look right there under the hill!” But the boy does not see anything; he says—“I do not see anything; what is it, mother?” And she says: “Keep looking, and you will see it.” At last he catches a glimpse of the glistening serpent; and lo, he is well! And thus it is with many a young convert. Some men say, “Oh, we do not believe in sudden conversions.” How long did it take to cure that boy? How long did it take to cure those serpent-bitten Israelites? It was just a look; and they were well.

===Chinese===
我看见一座帐棚里，一位以色列母亲俯身在她那爱子的身上——他正要开花人生，正要长成男子汉。她正擦去他额上凝起的死汗。再过一会儿，他的眼就要发直、发翳，因为生命正飞快地流逝。母亲的心都撕裂了、淌血了。忽然她听见营中一阵喧嚷。一声大喊腾起。怎么回事？她走到帐棚门口。「营里闹什么？」她问过路的人。有人说：「哎，这位好妇人，您没听见营里传来的好消息吗？」「没有，」妇人说，「好消息！什么消息？」「怎么，您没听说吗？神预备了药。」「什么！给被咬的以色列人的？哦，告诉我那药是什么！」「是这样的：神吩咐摩西造一条铜蛇，挂在营中间的杆子上；祂宣告：凡仰望它的，就必活。您听见的那声喊，便是众人看见蛇被举起来时欢呼的声音。」母亲回到帐棚里，说：「我的孩子，我有好消息告诉你。你不用死了！我的孩子，我的孩子，我带好消息来了；你能活！」他已经神志昏沉；他虚弱得走不到帐棚门口。她用有力的双臂托起他。「望那边；就在那小山底下望！」可那孩子什么都看不见；他说——「我什么也看不见；是什么，妈妈？」她说：「一直望，你就会看见。」终于他瞥见那闪闪发亮的蛇；哎呀，他好了！许多初信者也是这样。一些人说：「哦，我们不信瞬间的悔改。」治好那孩子用了多久？治好那些被蛇咬的以色列人用了多久？不过一望；他们就痊愈了。

===Original===
That Hebrew boy is a young convert. I can fancy that I see him now calling on all those who were with him to praise God. He sees another young man bitten as he was; and he runs up to him and tells him, “You, need not die.” “Oh,” the young man replies, “I cannot live; it is not possible. There is not a physician in Israel who can cure me.” He does not know that he need not die. “Why, have you not heard the news? God has provided a remedy.” “What remedy?” “Why, God has told Moses to lift up a brazen serpent, and has said that none of those who look upon that serpent shall die.” I can just imagine the young man. He may be what you call an intellectual young man. He says to the young convert “You do not think I am going to believe anything like that? If the physicians in Israel cannot cure me, how do you think that an old brass serpent on a pole is going to cure me?” “Why, sir, I was as bad as yourself!” “You do not say so!” “Yes, I do.” “That is the most astonishing thing I ever heard,” says the young man: “I wish you would explain the philosophy of it.” “I cannot. I only know that I looked at that serpent, and I was cured: that did it. I just looked; that is all. My mother told me the reports that were being heard through the camp; and I just believed what my mother said, and I am perfectly well.” “Well, I do not believe you were bitten as badly as I have been.” The young man pulls up his sleeve. “Look there! That mark shows where I was bitten; and I tell you I was worse than you are.” “Well, if I understood the philosophy of it I would look and get well.” “Let your philosophy go: *look and live*.” “But, sir, you ask me to do an unreasonable thing. If God had said, Take the brass and rub it into the wound, there might be something in the brass that would cure the bite. Young man, explain the philosophy of it.” I have often seen people before me who have talked in that way. But the young man calls in another, and takes him into the tent, and says: “Just tell him how the Lord saved you;” and he tells just the same story; and he calls in others, and they all say the same thing.

===Chinese===
那希伯来少年是个初信者。我好像看见他此刻正招呼周围所有的人一同赞美神。他看见另一个少年像他先前那样被咬了；便跑过去对他说：「你，不用死。」「哦，」那少年答道，「我活不了；不可能。以色列没有一位大夫能治我。」他不知道自己本不必死。「怎么，你没听见消息吗？神预备了药。」「什么药？」「是这样的：神叫摩西举起一条铜蛇，又说凡仰望那蛇的都不致死。」我可以想象那少年。他也许是你所谓有学问的少年。他对那初信者说：「你以为我会信这种事？以色列的大夫都治不好我，你以为一根杆子上一条铜蛇就能治我？」「哎，先生，我从前跟你一样病重！」「你不会吧！」「是真的。」「这是我所听过最惊人的一桩事，」那少年说，「你倒给我讲讲其中的道理。」「我讲不出。我只知道我望了那蛇，就好了：就是这么一回事。我就是望了望；就这些。我妈妈把营中传开的消息告诉我；我就信了我妈妈的话，我全好了。」「嗯，我不信你被咬得像我这么重。」那少年捋起袖子。「你瞧！这个印子就是我被咬的地方；我告诉你，我比你病得还重。」「好吧，我要是懂其中的道理，我就会去望、去好。」「让你的道理见鬼去吧：*望而得生*。」「可是先生，你叫我做一件不合理的事。要是神说，把铜取下来，揉进伤口里，那也许铜里有什么东西能治蛇咬。少年人，你讲讲其中的道理。」我常看见我面前的人这样说话。可那少年又喊来另一位，把他带进帐棚，说：「你只管告诉他主是怎样救了你的；」他便讲了同样的故事；他又喊来别人，他们说法都一样。

===Original===
The young man says it is a very strange thing. “If the Lord had told Moses to go and get some herbs, or roots, and stew them, and take the decoction as a medicine, there would be something in that. But it is so contrary to nature to do such a thing as look at the serpent, that I cannot do it.” At length his mother, who has been out in the camp, comes in, and she says, “My boy, I have just the best news in the world for you. I was in the camp, and I saw hundreds who were very far gone, and they are all perfectly well now.” The young man says: “I should like to get well; it is a very painful thought to die; I want to go into the promised land, and it is terrible to die here in this wilderness; but the fact is—I do not understand the remedy. It does not appeal to my reason. I cannot believe that I can get well in a moment.” And the young man dies in consequence of his own unbelief.

God provided a remedy for this bitten Israelite—“Look and live!” And there is eternal life for every poor sinner. Look, and you can be saved, my reader, this very hour. God has provided a remedy; and it is offered to all. The trouble is, a great many people are looking at the pole. Do not look at the pole; that is the church. You need not look at the church; the church is all right, but the church cannot save you. Look beyond the pole. Look at the Crucified One. Look to Calvary. Bear in mind, sinner, that Jesus died for all. You need not look at ministers; they are just God’s chosen instruments to hold up the Remedy, to hold up Christ. And so, my friends, take your eyes off from men; take your eyes off from the church. Lift them up to Jesus; who took away the sin of the world, and there will be life for you from this hour.

===Chinese===
那少年说这事很怪。「要是主叫摩西去找些草药，或树根，炖了，把那汤药当药喝，那还说得过去。可像望蛇这种事，太违反常理了，我做不到。」终于，他一直在营中的母亲进来了，说：「我的孩子，我有天大的好消息告诉你。我在营中，看见成百病得很重的人，如今全好了。」那少年说：「我也想好；一想到死就难受；我想进应许之地，死在这旷野里太可怕了；可问题是——我不懂这药。它不合我的理性。我不能相信我能一下子就好。」那少年就这样因自己的不信而死。

神为这被咬的以色列人预备了药——「望而得生！」每一个可怜的罪人都有永生。读者啊，望，你此时此刻就能得救。神已经预备了药；它向一切人敞开。麻烦在于，许多人望的是那杆子。不要望那杆子；那是教会。你不必望教会；教会没错，可教会救不了你。望过杆子去。望那位钉十字架的主。望各各他。罪人啊，要记住，耶稣为所有人而死。你不必望牧师；他们不过是神所拣选、用来举起那药、举起基督的器皿。所以，朋友们，把眼睛从人身上移开；把眼睛从教会身上移开。举目望耶稣；祂曾除去世人的罪，从此时起你必有生命。

===Original===
Thank God, we do not require an education to teach us how to look. That little girl, that little boy, only four years old, who cannot read, can look. When the father is coming home, the mother says to her little boy, “Look! look! look!” and the little child learns to look long before he is a year old. And that is the way to be saved. It is to look at the Lamb of God “who taketh away the sin of the world;” and there is life this moment for everyone who is willing to look.

Some men say, “I wish I knew how to be saved.” Just take God at His word and trust His Son this very day—this very hour—this very moment. He will save you, if you will trust Him. I imagine I hear someone saying, “I do not feel the bite as much as I wish I did. I know I am a sinner, and all that; but I do not feel the bite enough.” How much does God want you to feel it?

When I was in Belfast I knew a doctor who had a friend, a leading surgeon there; and he told me that the surgeon’s custom was, before performing any operation, to say to the patient, “Take a good look at the wound, and then fix your eyes on me; and do not take them off till I get through.” I thought at the time that was a good illustration. Sinner, take a good look at your wound; and then fix your eyes on Christ, and do not take them off. It is better to look at the Remedy than at the wound. See what a poor wretched sinner you are; and then look at the Lamb of God who “taketh away the sin of the world.” He died for the ungodly and the sinner. Say “I will take Him!” And may God help you to lift your eye to the Man on Calvary. And as the Israelites looked upon the serpent and were healed, so may you look and live.

===Chinese===
感谢神，仰望无须教育来教。那小女孩，那小男孩，才四岁，还不会认字，却能望。父亲要回家时，母亲对小儿子说：「瞧！瞧！瞧！」那小孩子不到一岁就学会瞧了。得救也正是这样。就是仰望神的羔羊，「除去世人罪孽的」；此时此刻，凡愿意仰望的人都有生命。

一些人说：「我真想知道怎样才可以得救。」今日便信神的话、今日便信靠祂的儿子——此时此刻——这一刹那。你若肯信祂，祂必救你。我仿佛听见有人说：「我觉得蛇咬得还不够厉害。我知道我是个罪人，这些我都知道；可我觉得咬得还不够厉害。」神要你觉得多厉害才算够呢？

我在贝尔法斯特（Belfast）时认识一位医生，他有位朋友，是当地一位主刀的名医；他告诉我，那位名医的习惯是，每次动手术前都对病人说：「好好瞧一瞧伤口，然后把眼睛盯在我身上；我不做完，眼睛别移开。」我那时就想，这是个好比方。罪人啊，好好瞧一瞧你的伤口；然后把眼睛盯在基督身上，别移开。望那药，胜过望那伤口。看看你自己是个多么可怜悲惨的罪人；然后望那「除去世人罪孽的」神之羔羊。祂为不虔敬的人和罪人而死。说一句「我接受祂！」愿神助你举目望各各他上的那人。以色列人怎样望蛇而得医治，愿你也能望而得生。

===Original===
After the battle of Pittsburgh Landing I was in a hospital at Murfreesbro. In the middle of the night I was aroused and told that a man in one of the wards wanted to see me. I went to him and he called me “chaplain”—I was not the chaplain—and said he wanted me to help him die. And I said, “I would take you right up in my arms and carry you into the kingdom of God if I could; but I cannot do it: I cannot help you die!” And he said, “Who can?” I said, “The Lord Jesus Christ can—He came for that purpose.” He shook his head, and said, “He cannot save me; I have sinned all my life.” And I said, “But He came to save sinners.” I thought of his mother in the north, and I was sure that she was anxious that he should die in peace; so I resolved I would stay with him. I prayed two or three times, and repeated all the promises I could; for it was evident that in a few hours he would be gone. I said I wanted to read him a conversation that Christ had with a man who was anxious about his soul. I turned to the third chapter of John. His eyes were riveted on me; and when I came to the 14th and 15th verses—the passage before us—he caught up the words, “As Moses lifted up the serpent in the wilderness, even so must the Son of Man be lifted up; that whosoever believeth in Him should not perish, but have eternal life.” He stopped me and said, “Is that there?” I said “Yes.” He asked me to read it again; and I did so. He leant his elbows on the cot and clasping his hands together, said, “That’s good; won’t you read it again?” I read it the third time; and then went on with the rest of the chapter. When I had finished, his eyes were closed, his hands were folded, and there was a smile on his face. Oh, how it was lit up! What change had come over it! I saw his lips quivering, and leaning over him I heard in a faint whisper, “As Moses lifted up the serpent in the wilderness, even so must the Son of Man be lifted up; that whosoever believeth in Him should not perish, but have eternal life.” He opened his eyes and said, “That’s enough; don’t read any more.” He lingered a few hours, pillowing his head on those two verses; and then went up in one of Christ’s chariots, to take his seat in the kingdom of God.

===Chinese===
匹兹堡 Landing 之役（即夏伊洛之役）之后，我在默弗里斯伯勒（Murfreesbro）的一所医院里。半夜里有人叫醒我，说有一个病房里的人要见我。我到他跟前，他唤我「牧师」——我并不是牧师——说他要我帮他死。我说：「要是我能，我恨不得把你一把抱起，抱进神的国里；可我做不到：我帮不了你死！」他说：「那谁能？」我说：「主耶稣基督能——祂正是为此而来。」他摇头，说：「祂救不了我；我作了一辈子的罪。」我说：「可祂正是来救罪人的。」我想到了他北方的母亲，我敢说她一定盼着他能安息；于是我决心陪着他。我祷告了两三次，又把记得的应许都背了一遍；因为显然再过几个钟头他就要去了。我说我想念一段给他听，是基督与一个为灵魂焦虑的人的对话。我翻到约翰福音第三章。他的眼直勾勾盯着我；当我念到 14、15 节——就是摆在我们面前这一段——他接过去念道：「摩西在旷野怎样举蛇，人子也必照样被举起来；叫一切信祂的都得永生。」他止住我，说：「这话在吗？」我说「在。」他要我再念一遍；我便再念。他以肘支在床上，双手紧握，说：「真好；你再念一遍好不好？」我念了第三遍；然后接着把这一章念完。念完时，他眼已闭，手已交叠，脸上带着笑。哎呀，那笑多么亮！那神情变得多么不一样！我看见他嘴唇在动，俯身过去，听见他用极轻的低语念：「摩西在旷野怎样举蛇，人子也必照样被举起来；叫一切信祂的都得永生。」他睁开眼说：「够了；别再念了。」他又撑了几个钟头，把头枕在那两节经文上；然后乘着基督的一辆战车升上去，在神的国里坐了他的位。

===Original===
Christ said to Nicodemus: “Except a man be born again, he cannot see the kingdom of God.” You may see many countries; but there is one country—the land of Beulah, which John Bunyan saw in vision—you shall never behold, unless you are born again—regenerated by Christ. You can look abroad and see many beautiful trees; but the tree of life, you shall never behold, unless your eyes are made clear by faith in the Saviour. You may see the beautiful rivers of the earth—you may ride upon their bosoms; but bear in mind that your eye will never rest upon the river which bursts out from the Throne of God and flows through the upper Kingdom, unless you are born again. God has said it; and not man. You will never see the kingdom of God except you are born again. You may see the kings and lords of the earth; but the King of kings and Lord of lords you will never see except you are born again. When you are in London you may go to the Tower and see the crown of England, which is worth thousands of dollars, and is guarded there by soldiers; but bear in mind that your eye will never rest upon the crown of life except you are born again.

===Chinese===
基督对尼哥底母说：「人若不重生，就不能见神的国。」你可见许多国度；却有一处地方——那本仁·约翰（John Bunyan）在异象中所见的比拉之地（land of Beulah）——你永远见不着，除非你重生——为基督所重生。你可极目远眺，看见许多美丽的树；可生命树你永远见不着，除非你的眼借信救主而得明亮。你可见大地上美丽的江河——可在其上泛舟；但要记住，你的眼永远落不到那条从神宝座涌出、流过上界国度去的河上，除非你重生。这话是神说的；不是人说的。你若不重生，就永远见不到神的国。你可见地上的君王与贵族；可万王之王、万主之主，你若不重生就永远见不到。你在伦敦时可去伦敦塔看英格兰的王冠，那王冠值数千元，有士兵在那里把守；可要记住，生命的冠冕，你若不重生，眼就永远落不到它上面。

===Original===
You may hear the songs of Zion which are sung here; but one song—that of Moses and the Lamb—the uncircumcised ear shall never hear; its melody will only gladden the ear of those who have been born again. You may look upon the beautiful mansions of earth, but bear in mind the mansions which Christ has gone to prepare you shall never see unless you are born again. It is God who says it. You may see ten thousand beautiful things in this world; but the city that Abraham caught a glimpse of—and from that time became a pilgrim and sojourner—you shall never see unless you are born again (Hebrews 11:8, 10–16). You may often be invited to marriage feasts here; but you will never attend the marriage supper of the Lamb except you are born again. It is God who says it, dear friend. You may be looking on the face of your sainted mother tonight, and feel that she is praying for you; but the time will come when you shall never see her more unless you are born again.

The reader may be a young man or a young lady who has recently stood by the bedside of a dying mother; and she may have said, “Be sure and meet me in heaven,” and you made the promise. Ah! you shall never see her more, except you are born again. I believe Jesus of Nazareth, sooner than those infidels who say you do not need to be born again. Parents, if you hope to see your children who have gone before, you must be born of the Spirit. Possibly you are a father or a mother who has recently borne a loved one to the grave; and how dark your home seems! Never more will you see your child, unless you are born again. If you wish to be reunited to your loved one, you must be born again. I may be addressing a father or a mother who has a loved one up yonder. If you could hear that loved one’s voice, it would say, “Come this way.” Have you a sainted friend up yonder? Young man or young lady, have you not a mother in the world of light? If you could hear her speak, would not she say, “Come this way, my son,”—“Come this way, my daughter?” If you would ever see her more you must be born again.

We all have an Elder Brother there. Nearly nineteen hundred years ago He crossed over, and from the heavenly shores He is calling you to heaven. Let us turn our backs upon the world. Let us give a deaf ear to the world. Let us look to Jesus on the Cross and be saved. Then we shall one day see the King in His beauty, and we shall go no more out.

===Chinese===
你在这里可听见所唱的锡安之歌；可有一首——摩西与羔羊之歌——未受割礼的耳朵永远听不见；它的旋律只叫那些重生之人的耳朵欢喜。你可见世上华美的宅第，可要记住，基督去为你预备的那些宅第，你若不重生就永远见不着。这话是神说的。你在这世上可见一万样美丽的事物；可亚伯拉罕所瞥见的那座城——并从此成为客旅和寄居者——你若不重生就永远见不着（希伯来书 11:8, 10–16）。你在这里常常受邀赴婚宴；可羔羊的婚筵，你若不重生就永远赴不了。亲爱的朋友，这话是神说的。今夜你也许正望着你那位已在天上的母亲的脸，感到她在为你祷告；可终有一日，你若不重生，就再也见不着她。
