读者也许是位少年或少女，新近才在临终母亲的床前侍过；她也许说过：「在天上一定要再见我」，你也答应了她。哎！你若不重生，就再也见不着她了。我信拿撒勒人耶稣，胜过那些说你不需重生的不信者。父母们，你们若盼望见着已先你而去的孩子，你们就必须从灵而生。你也许是位父亲或母亲，新近才把一位所爱的人送进了坟墓；你的家看上去多么暗淡！你若不重生，就再也见不着你的孩子。你若想与所爱的人重聚，就必须重生。我也许正在对一位天上有所爱之人的父亲或母亲说话。你若能听见那位亲人的声音，他会说：「往这边来。」你在天上有一位已得安息的朋友吗？少年或少女，你在光明之世不是有一位母亲吗？你若能听见她说话，她岂不是说：「往这边来，我的儿子，」——「往这边来，我的女儿？」你若还想再见她，就必须重生。

我们在那里都有一位长兄。将近一千九百年前祂渡了过去，从天上的彼岸祂正召你上天。让我们转身背对世界。让我们对世界充耳不闻。让我们仰望十字架上的耶稣而得救。如此，终有一日我们必得见那王在祂的荣美之中，我们必不再出去。

## III — The Two Classes / III —— 两类人

> 「两个人上殿里去祷告。」
> ——路加福音 18:10

===Original===
I now want to speak of two classes: First, those who do not feel their need of a Saviour who have not been convinced of sin by the Spirit; and Second, those who are convinced of sin and cry, “What must I do to be saved?”

All inquirers can be ranged under two heads: they have either the spirit of the Pharisee, or the spirit of the publican. If a man having the spirit of the Pharisee comes into an after-meeting, I know of no better portion of Scripture to meet his case than Romans 3:10: “As it is written, There is none righteous, no, not one: there is none that understandeth; there is none that seeketh after God.” Paul is here speaking of the natural man. “They are all gone out of the way, they are together become unprofitable; there is none that doeth good, no, not one.” And in the 17th verse and those which follow, we have “And the way of peace have they not known; there is no fear of God before their eyes. Now we know what things soever the law saith, it saith to them who are under the law; that every mouth may be stopped, and all the world may become guilty before God.”

Then observe the last clause of verse 22: “For there is no difference; for all have sinned, and come short of the glory of God.” Not part of the human family—but *all*—“have sinned, and come short of the glory of God.” Another verse which has been very much used to convict men of their sin is 1 John 1:8: “If we say that we have no sin, we deceive ourselves, and the truth is not in us.”

===Chinese===
我现在要讲的是两类人：第一类，是那些不觉得自己需要救主、尚未被圣灵知罪的人；第二类，是那些已被知罪、呼喊「我当怎样行才可以得救」的人。

一切慕道者都可归在这两类之下：他们身上要么有法利赛人的灵，要么有税吏的灵。倘若一个有法利赛人之灵的人来到散会后的谈话聚会，我想不出有哪一段圣经比罗马书 3:10 更能对付他：「如经上所记：没有义人，连一个也没有。没有明白的，没有寻求神的。」保罗在这里所说的是属肉体的人。「都是偏离正路，一同变为无用。没有行善的，连一个也没有。」而在第 17 节及以下，我们有「平安的路，他们未曾知道；他们眼中不怕神。我们晓得律法上的话都是对律法以下之人说的，好塞住各人的口，叫普世的人都伏在神审判之下。」

再看第 22 节末了一句：「因为世人都犯了罪，亏缺了神的荣耀。」不是人类中的一部分——而是*所有的人*——「都犯了罪，亏缺了神的荣耀」。还有一节常被用来使人知罪的经文，是约翰一书 1:8：「我们若说自己无罪，便是自欺，真理不在我们心里了。」

===Original===
I remember that on one occasion we were holding meetings in an eastern city of forty thousand inhabitants; and a lady came and asked us to pray for her husband, whom she purposed bringing into the after meeting. I have traveled a good deal and met many pharisaical men; but this man was so clad in self-righteousness that you could not get the point of the needle of conviction in anywhere. I said to his wife: “I am glad to see your faith; but we cannot get near him; he is the most self-righteous man I ever saw.” She said: “You must! My heart will break if these meetings end without his conversion.” She persisted in bringing him; and I got almost tired of the sight of him.

But towards the close of our meetings of thirty days, he came up to me and put his trembling hand on my shoulder. The place in which the meetings were held was rather cold, and there was an adjoining room in which only the gas had been lighted; and he said to me, “Can’t you come in here for a few minutes?” I thought that he was shaking from cold, and I did not particularly wish to go where it was colder. But he said: “I am the worst man in the State of Vermont. I want you to pray for me.” I thought he had committed a murder, or some other awful crime; and I asked: “Is there any one sin that particularly troubles you?” And he said: “My whole life has been a sin. I have been a conceited, self-righteous Pharisee. I want you to pray for me.” He was under deep conviction. Man could not have produced this result; but the Spirit had. About two o’clock in the morning light broke in upon his soul: and he went up and down the business street of the city and told what God had done for him; and has been a most active Christian ever since.

===Chinese===
我记得有一次，我们在东部一座有四万居民的城市举行聚会；有位女士来请我们为她的丈夫祷告，她打算把丈夫带到散会后的谈话聚会里。我走过的地方不少，遇见过许多法利赛式的人物；可这个人满身披戴着自义，你简直无处能下「知罪之针」的针尖。我对他的妻子说：「我很高兴看见你的信心；可我们靠不近他；他是我所见过的最自义的人。」她说：「你们一定要！这些聚会若不能叫他归主，我的心就要碎了。」她坚持把他带来；我简直看他的身影都看厌了。

可是在为期三十天的聚会快结束时，他走到我跟前，把他那发抖的手搭在我肩上。聚会的场所相当冷，旁边有一间屋子只点着煤气灯；他对我说：「您能不能进来几分钟？」我以为他是冻得发抖，并不特别想去更冷的地方。可他说：「我是佛蒙特州（Vermont）最坏的人。我求您为我祷告。」我以为他犯过谋杀，或别的什么可怕的罪；我便问：「有什么特别的罪在困扰您吗？」他说：「我一生就是个罪。我一直是个自负自义的法利赛人。我求您为我祷告。」他正处在深深的知罪之下。人不能造成这样的结果；是圣灵造成的。约莫凌晨两点，光照进了他的灵魂；他在这城的大街上上下下地走，告诉人神为他作了何等大的事；从那以后，他一直是个极其热心侍奉的基督徒。

===Original===
There are four other passages in dealing with inquirers, which were used by Christ Himself. “Verily, verily, I say unto thee, Except a man be born again, he cannot see the kingdom of God.” (John 3:3).

In Luke 13:3, we read: “Except ye repent, ye shall all likewise perish.”

In Matthew 18, when the disciples came to Jesus to know who was to be the greatest in the kingdom of heaven, we are told that He took a little child and set him in the midst and said, “Verily I say unto you, Except ye be converted, and become as little children, ye shall not enter the kingdom of heaven” (18:1–3).

There is another important “Except” in Matthew 5:20: “Except your righteousness shall exceed the righteousness of the Scribes and Pharisees, ye shall in no case enter the kingdom of heaven.”

===Chinese===
对付慕道者，还有四处经文，是基督自己用过的。「我实实在在地告诉你，人若不重生，就不能见神的国。」（约翰福音 3:3）。

路加福音 13:3 记着：「你们若不悔改，都要如此灭亡。」

马太福音 18 章里，门徒到耶稣跟前，问天国里谁是最大的，圣经告诉我们，祂便领一个小孩子来，叫他站在当中，说：「我实在告诉你们，你们若不回转，变成小孩子的样式，断不得进天国」（18:1–3）。

马太福音 5:20 还有一个重要的「若不」：「你们的义若不胜于文士和法利赛人的义，断不能进天国。」

===Original===
A man must be made meet before he will want to go into the kingdom of God. I would rather go into the kingdom with the younger brother than stay outside with the elder. Heaven would be hell to such an one. An elder brother who could not rejoice at his younger brother’s return would not be “fit” for the kingdom of God. It is a solemn thing to contemplate; but the curtain drops and leaves him outside, and the younger brother within. To him the language of the Saviour under other circumstances seems appropriate: “Verily I say unto you, That the publicans and the harlots go into the kingdom of God before you” (Matthew 21:31).

A lady once came to me and wanted a favor for her daughter. She said: “You must remember I do not sympathize with you in your doctrine.” I asked: “What is your trouble?” She said: “I think your abuse of the elder brother is horrible. I think he is a noble character.” I said that I was willing to hear her defend him; but that it was a solemn thing to take up such a position; and that the elder brother needed to be converted as much as the younger. When people talk of being moral it is well to get them to take a good look at the old man pleading with his boy who would not go in.

===Chinese===
一个人必须先被「配得」了，才会愿意进神的国。我宁可同那小儿子一同进国里去，也不愿同那大儿子一起留在外面。对这样的人，天堂便是地狱。一个不能为小儿子归来而欢喜的哥哥，是不「配」神之国的。这是一件可深思而严肃的事；可幕布落下，把他留在外面，把小儿子留在里面。救主在另一处场合所说的话，似乎正适用于他：「我实在告诉你们，税吏和娼妓倒比你们先进神的国」（马太福音 21:31）。

有一次一位女士来找我，为她女儿求一件事。她说：「您要记得，我对您所讲的道并不赞同。」我问：「您难处在哪儿？」她说：「我觉得您那样攻击那大儿子，简直可怕。我觉得他是个高尚的人物。」我说，我愿意听她为他辩护；可这是件严肃的事，竟站在这一边；而且大儿子也和那小儿子一样，需要悔改归正。人若一味讲自己的道德，最好叫他们好好看看那位老父亲——他正在恳求他那不肯进去的儿子。

===Original===
But we will pass on now to the other class with which we have to deal. It is composed of those who are convinced of sin and from whom the cry comes as from the Philippian jailer, “What must I do to be saved?” To those who utter this penitential cry there is no necessity to administer the law. It is well to bring them straight to the Scripture: “Believe on the Lord Jesus Christ, and thou shalt be saved.” (Acts 16:31). Many will meet you with a scowl and say, “I don’t know what it is to believe;” and though it is the law of heaven that they must believe, in order to be saved—yet they ask for something besides that. We are to tell them what, and where, and how, to believe.

In John 3:35 and 36 we read: “The Father loveth the Son, and hath given all things into His hand. He that believeth on the Son hath everlasting life; and he that believeth not the Son shall not see life; but the wrath of God abideth on him.”

===Chinese===
现在我们要转到我们必须对付的另一类人。这一类是那些已被知罪的人，从他们口中发出像腓立比禁卒那样的呼喊：「我当怎样行才可以得救？」对发出这悔改之呼的人，已无需再用律法来对付。最好是直接把他们带到圣经面前：「当信主耶稣，你和你一家都必得救。」（使徒行传 16:31）。许多人会皱着眉对你说：「我不知道信是怎么回事」；尽管天国的律法是——他们必须信才能得救——可他们还要在这一条之外另有所求。我们当告诉他们信的是什么，到哪里信，以及怎样信。

约翰福音 3:35–36 记着：「父爱子，已将万有交在祂手里。信子的人有永生；不信子的人得不着永生，神的震怒常在他身上。」

===Original===
Now this looks reasonable. Man lost life by unbelief—by not believing God’s word; and we got life back again by believing—by taking God at His word. In other words we get up where Adam fell down. He stumbled and fell over the stone of unbelief; and we are lifted up and stand upright by believing. When people say they cannot believe, show them chapter and verse, and hold them right to this one thing: “Has God ever broken His promise for these six thousand years?” The devil and men have been trying all the time and have not succeeded in showing that He has broken a single promise; and there would be a jubilee in hell today if one word that He has spoken could be broken. If a man says that he cannot believe it is well to press him on that one thing.

I can believe God better today than I can my own heart. “The heart is deceitful above all things, and desperately wicked: who can know it?” (Jeremiah 17:9). I can believe God better than I can myself. If you want to know the way of Life, believe that Jesus Christ is a personal Saviour; cut away from all doctrines and creeds, and come right to the heart of the Son of God. If you have been feeding on dry doctrine there is not much growth on that kind of food. Doctrines are to the soul what the streets which lead to the house of a friend who has invited me to dinner are to the body. They will lead me there if I take the right one; but if I remain in the streets my hunger will never be satisfied. Feeding on doctrines is like trying to live on dry husks; and lean indeed must the soul remain which partakes not of the Bread sent down from heaven.

===Chinese===
这话听来合情合理。人是因着不信——因不信神的话语——而失丧了生命；我们也是因着信——因信神口中所出的话——而重新得着生命。换句话说，我们在亚当跌倒的地方站了起来。他在那块「不信」的石头上绊跌了；我们却因信被扶起，直立得住。当人说他们不能信时，就把章节指给他们看，把他们牢牢扣在这一件事上：「这六千年来，神可曾违背过祂的应许吗？」魔鬼和人一直在设法证明，却始终未能指出祂曾违背过一个应许；倘若祂所说的一句话能被废去，今日地狱里便要大大欢庆了。若有人说他不能信，就该在这一件事上紧紧逼问他。

今日我信神，胜过信我自己的心。「人心比万物都诡诈，坏到极处，谁能识透呢？」（耶利米书 17:9）。我信神，胜过信我自己。你若想知道生命之道，就当信耶稣基督是你个人的救主；撇开一切的道理与信条，径直来到神儿子的心前。你若一直拿干巴巴的道理当粮食，这种食物叫你长不大。道理之于灵魂，正如那些引我到朋友家赴席的街道之于身体。我若走对了，它们能引我到那里；可我若老待在街上，我的饥饿永远不得饱足。以道理为食，就像想拿干豆荚充饥；那不分受从天降下之粮的灵魂，必仍是瘦骨嶙峋。

===Original===
Some ask: “How am I to get my heart warmed?” It is by believing. You do not get power to love and serve God until you believe.

The apostle John says “If we receive the witness of men, the witness of God is greater: for this is the witness of God which He hath testified of His Son. He that believeth on the Son of God hath the witness in himself: he that believeth not God hath made Him a liar; because he believeth not the record that God gave of His Son. And this is the record, that God hath given to us eternal life, and this life is in His Son. He that hath the Son hath life; and he that hath not the Son of God hath not life” (1 John 5:9).

Human affairs would come to a standstill if we did not take the testimony of men. How should we get on in the ordinary intercourse of life, and how would commerce get on, if we disregarded men’s testimony? Things social and commercial would come to a deadlock within forty-eight hours! This is the drift of the apostle’s argument here. “If we receive the witness of men, the witness of God is greater.” God has borne witness to Jesus Christ. And if man can believe his fellow men who are frequently telling untruths and whom we are constantly finding unfaithful, why should we not take God at His word and believe His testimony?

===Chinese===
有人问：「我当怎样叫我这颗心火热起来？」是借着信。你尚未信之时，断不能得着爱神、事奉神的能力。

使徒约翰说：「我们既领受人的见证，神的见证更该领受了，因神的见证是为祂儿子作的。信神儿子的，就有这见证在他心里；不信神的，就是将神当作说谎的，因不信神为他儿子作的见证。这见证就是神赐给我们永生，这永生也是在祂儿子里面。人有了神的儿子就有生命；没有神的儿子就没有生命」（约翰一书 5:9）。

我们若不信人的见证，人间一切事务都要停顿。我们若无视人的见证，日常的交往如何进行，商业如何运转？社会与商业的事务四十八小时之内便要陷于瘫痪！这便是使徒在此论证的脉络。「我们既领受人的见证，神的见证更该领受了。」神已经为耶稣基督作了见证。人既常常说谎、又屡屡显出不可靠，人尚且能信他们，我们为何不抓住神口中的话、信祂的见证呢？

===Original===
Faith is a belief in testimony. It is not a leap in the dark, as some tell us. That would be no faith at all. God does not ask any man to believe without giving him something to believe. You might as well ask a man to see without eyes; to hear without ears; and to walk without feet—as to bid him believe without giving him something to believe.

When I started for California I procured a guidebook. This told me, that after leaving the State of Illinois, I should cross the Mississippi, and then the Missouri; get into Nebraska; then over the Rocky Mountains to the Mormon settlement at Salt Lake City, and by the way of the Sierra Nevada into San Francisco. I found the guidebook all right as I went along; and I should have been a miserable sceptic if, having proved it to be correct three-fourths of the way, I had said that I would not believe it for the remainder of the journey.

Suppose a man, in directing me to the Post Office, gives me ten landmarks; and that, in my progress there, I find nine of them to be as he told me; I should have good reason to believe that I was coming to the Post Office.

===Chinese===
信心就是相信见证。它并不像有人所说的那样，是「在黑暗中的一跳」。那根本算不得信心。神决不叫人无凭无据地信——祂必先给人一些可信的内容。你叫一个没有眼的人去看，叫一个没有耳的人去听，叫一个没有脚的人去走——和你叫一个人无所凭信地去信，一样的不通。

我动身去加利福尼亚时，买了一本旅行指南。这本指南告诉我，离开伊利诺伊州之后，我要渡过密西西比河，再渡过密苏里河；进入内布拉斯加；然后翻过落基山，到盐湖城的摩门教聚居地，再取道内华达山脉进入旧金山。一路走来，我发现这本指南果然不错；倘若我已经验证了它四分之三的路程都对，却说我不再信它余下的路程，那我便是个可悲的怀疑论者了。

设想有一个人给我指去邮局的路，告诉我十个路标；我在途中发现其中九个正如他所说；我便大有理由相信，我正朝邮局走去。

===Original===
And if, by believing, I get a new life, and a hope, a peace, a joy, and a rest to my soul, that I never had before; if I get self-control, and find that I have a power to resist evil and to do good, I have pretty good proof that I am in the right road to the “city which hath foundations, whose builder and maker is God.” And if things have taken place, and are now taking place, as recorded in God’s Word, I have good reason to conclude that what yet remains will be fulfilled. And yet people talk of doubting. There can be no true faith where there is fear. Faith is to take God at His word, unconditionally. There cannot be true peace where there is fear. “Perfect love casteth out fear.” How wretched a wife would be if she doubted her husband! and how miserable a mother would feel if after her boy had gone away from home she had reason, from his neglect, to question that son’s devotion! True love never has a doubt.

There are three things indispensable to faith—knowledge, assent, and appropriation.

===Chinese===
倘若我因信而得着一个新生命，得着我从前未曾有过的盼望、平安、喜乐，和灵魂的安息；倘若我得着了自制之力，发觉自己有了抵挡罪恶、行出善事的能力，我便有相当好的凭据，证明我正走在通往那「有根基的城」——就是神所建造所经营的那座——的正确道路上。倘若神的话语上所记载的事已经应验、如今也在应验，我便大有理由断定，尚未成就的也必成就。可人们还是说要怀疑。哪里有惧怕，哪里就没有真信心。信心就是无条件地抓住神口中所出的话。哪里有惧怕，哪里就没有真正的平安。「完全的爱把惧怕驱除。」一个妻子若怀疑她的丈夫，是多么痛苦！一个母亲，儿子离家之后，因儿子的疏忽而怀疑那儿子的孝心，是何等难受！真爱从不怀疑。

信心有三种不可或缺的要素——认识、同意、领受。

===Original===
We must know God. “And this is life eternal, that they might know Thee, the only true God, and Jesus Christ whom Thou hast sent” (John 17:3). Then we must not only give our assent to what we know; but we must lay hold of the truth. If a man simply give his assent to the plan of salvation, it will not save him: he must accept Christ as his Saviour. He must receive and appropriate Him.

Some say they cannot tell how a man’s life can be affected by his belief. But let someone cry out that some building in which we happen to be sitting, is on fire; and see how soon we should act on our belief and get out. We are all the time influenced by what we believe. We cannot help it. And let a man believe the record that God has given of Christ, and it will very quickly affect his whole life.

Take John 5:24. There is enough truth in that one verse for every soul to rest upon for salvation. It does not admit the shadow of a doubt. “Verily, verily”—which means truly, truly—“I say unto you, He that heareth My word, and believeth on Him that sent Me, hath—hath—everlasting life, and shall not come into condemnation; but is passed from death unto life.”

===Chinese===
我们必须认识神。「认识你独一的真神，并且认识你所差来的耶稣基督，这就是永生」（约翰福音 17:3）。再者，我们不仅要赞同我们所认识的，更要抓住真理。一个人若只是赞同救恩的计划，这并不能救他：他必须接受基督作他的救主。他必须领受祂、以祂为己有。

有人说，他们看不出一个人的信念如何能影响他的生活。可只要有人喊一声「我们正坐着的这栋房子着火了」——你看我们是多么快地照着所信的起来逃出去。我们无时无刻不在被自己所信的影响着。我们不由自主。一个人若信神为基督所作的见证，这见证要很快地影响他整个的生活。

请看约翰福音 5:24。这一节里的真理，已足够让每一个灵魂安心倚靠而得救。它不容许一毫的疑影。「我实实在在地」——意即真真实实地——「告诉你们，那听我话、又信差我来者的，就有——就有——永生，不至于定罪，是已经出死入生了。」

===Original===
Now if a person really hears the word of Jesus and believes with the heart on God who sent the Son to be the Saviour of the world, and lays hold of and appropriates this great salvation, there is no fear of judgment. He will not be looking forward with dread to the Great White Throne; for we read in 1 John 4:17: “Herein is our love made perfect, that we may have boldness in the day of judgment: because as He is, so are we in this world.”

If we believe, there is for us no condemnation, no judgment. That is behind us, and passed; and we shall have boldness in the day of judgment.

I remember reading of a man who was on trial for his life. He had friends with influence; and they procured a pardon for him from the king on condition that he was to go through the trial, and be condemned. He went into court with the pardon in his pocket. The feeling ran very high against him, and the judge said that the court was shocked that he was so much unconcerned. But, when the sentence was pronounced, he pulled out the pardon, presented it, and walked out a free man. He has been pardoned; and so have we. Then let death come, we have nought to fear. All the gravediggers in the world cannot dig a grave large enough and deep enough to hold eternal life; all the coffin makers in the world cannot make a coffin large enough and tight enough to hold eternal life. Death has had his hand on Christ once, but never again.

===Chinese===
如今，一个人若真正听见了耶稣的话，心里信那差儿子作世人救主的神，又抓住、领受这极大的救恩，他便不再惧怕审判。他不会怀着恐惧前瞻那白色大宝座；因为我们读到约翰一书 4:17：「这样，爱在我们身上得以完全，我们就可以在审判的日子坦然无惧。因为祂如何，我们在这世上也如何。」

我们若信，便不再有定罪，不再有审判。那都在我们身后，已过去了；我们在审判的日子必坦然无惧。

我记得读过一个人的事：他因性命受审。他有些有权势的朋友；他们为他向国王求得赦免，条件是他仍要受审，并被判罪。他带着赦免书进了法庭。众人对他情绪激愤，法官说法庭很震惊，他竟如此若无其事。可判词一经宣布，他便掏出赦免书，呈了上去，作为一个自由人走了出去。他已被赦免了；我们也是。那么，任凭死亡来临吧，我们一无所惧。世上一切掘墓人，掘不出一个又大又深得足以装下永生的坟；世上一切造棺人，造不出一口又大又紧得足以装下永生的棺。死亡曾一次伸手按在基督身上，却再也不能了。

===Original===
Jesus said: “I am the Resurrection, and the Life: he that believeth in Me, though he were dead, yet shall he live: and whosoever liveth and believeth in Me shall never die” (John 11:25–26). And in the Apocalypse we read that the risen Saviour said to John, “I am He that liveth, and was dead; and, behold, I am alive forevermore” (Revelation 1:18). Death cannot touch Him again.

We get life by believing. In fact we get more than Adam lost; for the redeemed child of God is heir to a richer and more glorious inheritance than Adam in Paradise could ever have conceived; yea, and that inheritance endures forever—it is inalienable.

I would much rather have my life hid with Christ in God than have lived in Paradise; for Adam might have sinned and fallen after being there ten thousand years. But the believer is safer, if these things become real to him. Let us make them a fact, and not a fiction. God has said it; and that is enough. Let us trust Him even where we cannot trace Him. Let the same confidence animate us that was in little Maggie as related in the following simple but touching incident which I read in the *Bible Treasury*:—

===Chinese===
耶稣说：「复活在我，生命也在我。信我的人虽然死了，也必复活。凡活着信我的人必永远不死」（约翰福音 11:25–26）。而在启示录里，我们读到那复活的救主对约翰说：「我曾死过，现在又活了，直活到永永远远」（启示录 1:18）。死亡再不能摸着祂。

我们因信得着生命。事实上，我们所得的，比亚当所失的更多；因为神所救赎的儿女，所承受的产业比亚当日日在乐园中所能想象的更丰盛、更荣耀；而且那产业存到永远——它是不能夺去的。

我宁可将自己的生命与基督一同藏在神里面，也不愿曾住在乐园里；因为亚当纵使在那里住上一万年，仍可能犯罪堕落。但信徒却更稳妥——只要这些事对他成为真实的。让这些事成为事实，而非虚谈吧。神既说了，这就够了。让我们即使在不能踪迹祂的地方，也信靠祂。愿那感动小女孩玛吉（Maggie）的同样信心，也激励我们——下面这件朴素而感人的事，是我在《圣经宝库》（*Bible Treasury*）里读到的：——

===Original===
“I had been absent from home for some days, and was wondering, as I again draw near the homestead, if my little Maggie, just able to sit alone, would remember me. To test her memory, I stationed myself where I could see her, but could not be seen by her, and called her name in the familiar tone, ‘Maggie!’ She dropped her playthings, glanced around the room, and then looked down upon her toys. Again I repeated her name, ‘Maggie!’ when she once more surveyed the room; but, not seeing her father’s face, she looked very sad, and slowly resumed her employment. Once more I called, ‘Maggie!’ when, dropping her playthings, and bursting into tears, she stretched out her arms in the direction whence the sound proceeded, knowing that, though she could not see him, her father must be there, for she knew his voice.”

Now, we have power to see and to hear, and we have power to believe. It is all folly for the inquirers to take the ground that they cannot believe. They can, if they will. But the trouble with most people is that they have connected feeling with believing. Now Feeling has nothing whatever to do with Believing. The Bible does not say—He that feeleth, or he that feeleth and believeth, hath everlasting life. Nothing of the kind. I cannot control my feelings. If I could, I should never feel ill, or have a headache or toothache. I should be well all the while. But I can believe God; and if we get our feet on that rock, let doubts and fears come and the waves surge around us, the anchor will hold.

===Chinese===
「我离家已有数日，再走近家门时，心里想着：我的小玛吉——刚刚能独自坐稳——会不会还记得我？为要试一试她的记忆，我站在一处能看见她、她却看不见我的地方，用那熟悉的语调唤她的名字：『玛吉！』她放下玩具，朝屋里四处张望，然后低头看她的玩具。我又叫了一声：『玛吉！』她再一次把屋里环视一遍；可看不见父亲的脸，她一脸凄然，慢慢地又回到她的玩耍上。我再叫一声：『玛吉！』——她丢下玩具，哇地哭了出来，朝着声音来的方向伸出双臂，因为她知道，纵然看不见他，父亲必在那里，因为她认得他的声音。」

如今，我们有看的能力，有听的能力，也有信的能力。慕道者站出来说他们不能信，全属徒然。他们若愿意，就能信。可大多数人的难处在于：他们把「感觉」与「相信」连在了一起。须知感觉与相信毫无干系。圣经并非说——那感觉的，或那感觉又相信的，有永生。绝非如此。我不能左右我的感觉。我若能左右，我便永不会生病，不会头痛牙痛，必一直康健。可我能信神；我们若把脚立在这磐石上，任凭疑惑与恐惧来袭、波浪在我们四周翻涌，这锚也必持住。

===Original===
Some people are all the time looking at their faith. Faith is the hand that takes the blessing. I heard this illustration of a beggar. Suppose you were to meet a man in the street whom you had known for years as being accustomed to beg; and you offered him some money, and he were to say to you: “I thank you; I don’t want your money: I am not a beggar.” “How is that?” “Last night a man put a thousand dollars into my hands.” “He did! How did you know it was good money?” “I took it to the bank and deposited it and have got a bank book.” “How did you get this gift?” “I asked for alms; and after the gentleman talked with me he took out a thousand dollars in money and put it in my hand.” “How do you know that he put it in the right hand?” “What do I care about which hand; so that I have got the money.” Many people are always thinking whether the faith by which they lay hold of Christ is the right kind—but what is far more essential is to see that we have the right kind of Christ.

Faith is the eye of the soul; and who would ever think of taking out an eye to see if it were the right kind so long as the sight was perfect? It is not my taste, but it is what I taste, that satisfies my appetite. So, dear friends, it is taking God at His Word that is the means of our salvation. The truth cannot be made too simple.

===Chinese===
有人终日盯着自己的信心看。信心不过是那只接取福气的手。我听过这样一个乞丐的比方。设想你在街上遇见一个人，你多年来都晓得他是个惯于乞讨的；你给了他一些钱，他竟对你说：「谢谢您；我不要您的钱：我不是乞丐。」「这是怎么回事？」「昨夜有个人把一千块钱放在我手里。」「真的！你怎么知道那钱是真的？」「我拿到银行存了，存折我都拿到了。」「你怎么得着这份馈赠的？」「我求他赒济；那位先生与我谈了一会儿之后，便拿出一千块钱来，放在我手里。」「你怎么知道他放在了那只对的手里？」「我才不管哪一只手，只要我拿到了钱就好。」许多人总在想：他们用以抓住基督的信心，是不是那种对的信心——可更要紧得多的一件事，是看见我们所抓住的，是不是那位对的基督。

信心是灵魂的眼睛；只要视力无碍，谁会想到把眼睛挖出来看看它是不是那种对的眼睛？叫我胃口满足的，不是我的味觉，而是我所尝到的味道。照样，亲爱的朋友们，抓住神口中所出的话，这就是我们得救之法。这真理不能讲得太简单了。

===Original===
There is a man living in the city of New York who has a home on the Hudson River. His daughter and her family went to spend the winter with him: and in the course of the season the scarlet fever broke out. One little girl was put in quarantine, to be kept separate from the rest. Every morning the old grandfather used to go and bid his grandchild, “Goodbye,” before going to his business. On one of these occasions the little thing took the old man by the hand, and, leading him to a corner of the room, without saying a word she pointed to the floor where she had arranged some small crackers so they would spell out, “Grandpa, I want a box of paints.” He said nothing. On his return home he hung up his overcoat and went to the room as usual: when his little grandchild, without looking to see if her wish had been complied with, took him into the same corner, where he saw spelled out in the same way, “Grandpa, I thank you for the box of paints.” The old man would not have missed gratifying the child for anything. That was faith.

Faith is taking God at His Word; and those people who want some token are always getting into trouble. We want to come to this: God says it—let us believe it.

===Chinese===
纽约城里住着一个人，他在哈德逊河畔有一处家。他的女儿一家来与他同过冬：那一季里，猩红热发了出来。一个小女孩被隔离起来，要与其他人分开。每天早晨，老祖父出门办公之前，总要去对他的外孙女说声「再见」。有一天，那小家伙拉着老人的手，领他到房间的一角，一言不发，指着地板——她在地上摆了一些小饼干，拼出这样的话：「外公，我要一盒颜料。」他什么也没说。他回家时挂好外套，照常走进那房间：他的小外孙女也不看看她的心愿是否已得满足，便把他领到同一个角落，他在那里看见用同样方式拼出的话：「外公，谢谢您的颜料。」老人便是付上任何代价，也不肯错过使这孩子心愿得偿的机会。这便是信心。

信心就是抓住神口中所出的话；那些想要某种凭据的人，总是不断地陷入难处。我们要达到这一地步：神既说了——我们就信。

===Original===
But some say, Faith is the gift of God. So is the air; but you have to breathe it. So is bread; but you have to eat it. So is water; but you have to drink it. Some are wanting a miraculous kind of feeling. That is not faith. “Faith cometh by hearing, and hearing by the Word of God” (Romans 10:17). That is whence faith comes. It is not for me to sit down and wait for faith to come stealing over me with a strange sensation; but it is for me to take God at His Word. And you cannot believe, unless you have something to believe. So take the Word as it is written, and appropriate it, and lay hold of it.

In John 6:47–48 we read: “Verily, verily, I say unto you, He that believeth on Me hath everlasting life. I am that Bread of life.” There is the bread right at hand. Partake of it. I might have thousands of loaves within my home, and as many hungry men in waiting. They might assent to the fact that the bread was there; but unless they each took a loaf and commenced eating, their hunger would not be satisfied. So Christ is the Bread of heaven; and as the body feeds on natural food, so the soul must feed on Christ.

===Chinese===
可有人说，信心是神的恩赐。空气也是；可你得去呼吸它。饼也是；可你得去吃它。水也是；可你得去喝它。有人想要一种奇迹般的感觉。那不是信心。「信道是从听道来的，听道是从基督的话来的」（罗马书 10:17）。信心便是这样来的。我不可坐下干等，等信心带着某种奇异的感觉悄悄漫过我全身；我当抓住神口中所出的话。而且你若无所凭信，便不能信。所以，当照着所记的话去领受它，以它为己有，紧紧抓住它。

约翰福音 6:47–48 记着：「我实实在在地告诉你们，信的人有永生。我就是生命的粮。」粮就在眼前。来吃吧。我家里可以有几千块面包，也可以有同样多饥饿的人等着。他们可以承认那里有饼这一事实；可他们若不各拿一块饼开始吃，他们的饥饿仍不得饱足。照样，基督是天上之粮；身体怎样靠属世的食物得养，灵魂也必照样靠基督得养。

===Original===
If a drowning man sees a rope thrown out to rescue him he must lay hold of it; and in order to do so he must let go everything else. If a man is sick he must take the medicine—for simply looking at it will not cure him. A knowledge of Christ will not help the inquirer, unless he believes in Him, and takes hold of Him, as his only hope. The bitten Israelites might have believed that the serpent was lifted up; but unless they had looked they would not have lived (Numbers 21:6–9).

I believe that a certain line of steamers will convey me across the ocean, because I have tried it: but this will not help another man who may want to go, unless he acts upon my knowledge. So a knowledge of Christ does not help us unless we act upon it. That is what it is to believe on the Lord Jesus Christ. It is to act on what we believe. As a man steps on board a steamer to cross the Atlantic, so we must take Christ and make a commitment of our souls to Him; and He has promised to keep all who put their trust in Him. To believe on the Lord Jesus Christ, is simply to take Him at His word.

===Chinese===
一个溺水的人看见一条绳抛来救他，他必须抓住它；而要做到这一点，他必须放开其余一切。一个病人必须服药——因为光看着药是治不好病的。关于基督的知识不能帮助那慕道者，除非他信祂、抓住祂，以祂为他惟一的指望。被蛇咬的以色列人也许可以相信铜蛇已被举起来；可他们若不举目看，就不得存活（民数记 21:6–9）。

我相信某一条航线的轮船能载我渡过海洋，因为我试过；可这一点并不能帮助另一个想去的人，除非他照着我所知的去行。照样，关于基督的知识也不能帮助我们，除非我们照着去行。这便是「信主耶稣基督」的真意。就是照着我们所信的去行。正如一个人踏上一艘轮船横渡大西洋，我们也当接受基督，把我们的灵魂交托给祂；祂已应许要保守一切信靠祂的人。信主耶稣基督，无非就是抓住祂口中所出的话。

## IV — Words of Counsel / IV —— 劝勉之言

> 「压伤的芦苇，他不折断。」
> ——以赛亚书 42:3；马太福音 12:20

===Original===
It is dangerous for those who are seeking salvation to lean upon the experience of other people. Many are waiting for a repetition of the experience of their grandfather or grandmother. I had a friend who was converted in a field; and he thinks the whole town ought to go down into that meadow and be converted. Another was converted under a bridge; and he thinks that if any enquirer were to go there he would find the Lord. The best thing for the anxious is to go right to the Word of God. If there are any persons in the world to whom the Word ought to be very precious it is those who are asking how to be saved.

For instance a man may say, "I have no strength." Let him turn to Romans 5:6. "For when we were yet without strength, in due time Christ died for the ungodly." It is because we have no strength that we need Christ. He has come to give strength to the weak.

===Chinese===
对于那些正在寻求救恩的人来说，倚靠别人的经历是危险的。许多人都在等候重演他们祖父或祖母的经历。我有一位朋友在一片田野里归主；他便以为全城的人都该下到那片草地去归主。另一个人在一座桥下归主；他便以为任何慕道者只要去到那里，就能寻见主。对于心里焦急的人，最好的办法就是直接回到神的话语。这世上若有一等人，神的道对他们当格外宝贵，那就是那些正在求问怎样才可以得救的人。

比如有人会说：「我没有力量。」让他翻到罗马书 5:6。「因我们还软弱的时候，基督就按所定的日期为罪人死。」正因为我们没有力量，才需要基督。祂来，正是要赐力量给软弱的人。

===Original===
Another may say, "I cannot see." Christ says, "I am the Light of the world" (John 8:12). He came, not only to give light, but "to open the blind eyes" (Isaiah 42:7).

Another may say, "I do not think a man can be saved all at once." A person holding that view was in the Enquiry-room one night; and I drew his attention to Romans 6:23. "The wages of sin is death; but the *gift* of God is eternal life through Jesus Christ our Lord." How long does it take to accept a gift? There must be a moment when you have it not, and another when you have it﻿—a moment when it is another’s, and the next when it is yours. It does not take six months to get eternal life. It may however in some cases be like the mustard seed, very small at the commencement. Some people are converted so gradually that, like the morning light, it is impossible to tell when the dawn began; while, with others, it is like the flashing of a meteor, and the truth bursts upon them suddenly.

===Chinese===
另一个人也许会说：「我看不见。」基督说：「我是世界的光」（约翰福音 8:12）。祂来，不仅要赐下光，更要「睁开瞎子的眼」（以赛亚书 42:7）。

又有人也许会说：「我不信人能顷刻之间就得救。」一天夜里，有位持这看法的人在询道室里；我便把他的注意力引到罗马书 6:23。「因为罪的工价乃是死；惟有神的恩赐，在我们的主基督耶稣里，乃是永生。」接受一份礼物要用多久？必有一刻你还没有它，又有另一刻你已经有了——前一刻它还是别人的，下一刻它就是你的了。得着永生，用不着六个月。只是有些情形下，它或许像一粒芥菜种，起先极小。有些人的归正是那样渐进，如同晨光，无法说清破晓始于何时；又有些人，却如同流星一闪，真理忽然之间照亮了他们。

===Original===
I would not go across the street to prove when I was converted; but what is important is for me to know that I really have been.

It may be that a child has been so carefully trained that it is impossible to tell when the new birth began; but there must have been a moment when the change took place, and when he became a partaker of the Divine nature.

Some people do not believe in **sudden conversion**. But I will challenge anyone to show a conversion in the New Testament that was not instantaneous. "As Jesus passed by He saw Levi, the son of Alpheus, sitting at the receipt of custom, and said unto him, ‘Follow Me’: and he arose and followed Him" (Matthew 9:9). Nothing could be more sudden than that.

===Chinese===
我不会为证明自己是在何时归主而走过街去；要紧的是我当知道自己实在已经归主了。

也许一个孩子从小受这样周全的教导，以致无法说出他重生始于何时；但必曾有那么一刻，改变发生了，他也成了与神的性情有分的人。

有些人不相信**顷刻归主**。但我敢请任何人在新约里指出一桩不是霎时之间发生的归主。「耶稣走过的时候，看见亚勒腓的儿子利未坐在税关上，就对他说：『你跟从我来。』他就起来跟从了祂」（马太福音 9:9）。再没有比这更突然的了。

===Original===
Zaccheus, the publican, sought to see Jesus; and because he was little of stature he climbed up a tree. When Jesus came to the place He looked up and saw him, and said, "Zaccheus, make haste, and come down" (Luke 19:5). His conversion must have taken place somewhere between the branch and the ground. We are told that he received Jesus joyfully, and said, "Behold, Lord, the half of my goods I give to the poor; and if I have taken anything from any man by false accusation, I restore him fourfold" (Luke 19:8). Very few in these days could say that in proof of their conversion.

The whole house of Cornelius was converted suddenly; for so Peter preached Christ to him and his company the Holy Ghost fell on them, and they were baptized. (Acts 10).

On the day of Pentecost three thousand gladly received the Word. They were not only converted, but they were baptized the same day. (Acts 2).

And when Philip talked to the eunuch, as they went on their way, the eunuch said to Philip, "See, here is water: what doth hinder me to be baptized?" Nothing hindered. And Philip said, "If thou believest with all thine heart, thou mayest." And they both went down into the water; and the man of great authority under Candace, the queen of the Ethiopians, was baptized, and went on his way rejoicing. (Acts 8:26﻿–﻿38). You will find all through Scripture that conversions were sudden and instantaneous.

===Chinese===
税吏撒该想要见耶稣；又因他身量矮小，便爬上一棵树。耶稣到了那地方，抬头看见他，说：「撒该，快下来！」（路加福音 19:5）他的归主必是发生在那根树枝与地面之间。圣经告诉我们，他欢欢喜喜地接待耶稣，说：「主啊，我把所有的一半给穷人；我若讹诈了谁，就还他四倍」（路加福音 19:8）。如今能这样说来证明自己归主的人，少之又少。

哥尼流全家是顷刻之间归主的；因为彼得向他和他一家传讲基督时，圣灵就降在他们身上，他们就受了洗。（使徒行传 10）

五旬节那一天，有三千人乐意领受这道。他们不仅归了主，更在同一天受了洗。（使徒行传 2）

腓利对那太监讲话的时候，他们正走在路上，太监对腓利说：「看哪，这里有水，我受洗有什么妨碍呢？」没有什么妨碍。腓利说：「你若一心相信，就可以。」于是两人一同下水里去；那在埃提阿伯女王干大基手下掌大权的太监，就受了洗，欢欢喜喜地走路回去。（使徒行传 8:26–38）你们查考整本圣经，处处都能看见归主都是这样突然而又霎时的。

===Original===
A man has been in the habit of stealing money from his employer. Suppose he has taken $1,000 in twelve months; should we tell him to take $500 the next year, and less the next year, and the next, until in five years the sum taken would be only $50? That would be upon the same principle as gradual conversion.

If such a person were brought before the court and pardoned, because he could not change his mode of life all at once, it would be considered a very strange proceeding.

===Chinese===
有一个人惯于从雇主那里偷钱。假设他在十二个月里偷了 $1,000；我们要劝他第二年只偷 $500，再下一年偷得更少，年复一年，直到五年之后所偷的数目只剩 $50 吗？这与渐进归主的道理如出一辙。

倘若这样的人被带上法庭，却因为他不能顷刻间改掉生活方式而得了赦免，这必被看作一桩极奇怪的判决。

===Original===
But the Bible says, "Let him that stole steal no more" (Ephesians 4:28). It is "right about face!" Suppose a person is in the habit of cursing one hundred times a day: should we advise him not to utter more than ninety oaths the following day, and eighty the next day; so that in the course of time he would get rid of the habit? The Saviour says, "Swear not at all." (Matthew 5:34).

Suppose another man is in the habit of getting drunk and beating his wife twice a month; if he only did so once a month, and then only once in six months, that would be, upon the same ground, as reasonable as gradual conversion. Suppose Ananias had been sent to Paul, when he was on his way to Damascus breathing out threatenings and slaughter against the disciples, and casting them into prison, to tell him not to kill so many as he intended; and to let enmity die out of his heart gradually, but not all at once. Suppose he had been told that it would not do to stop breathing out threatenings and slaughter, and to commence preaching Christ all at once, because the philosophers would say that the change was so sudden it would not hold out; this would be the same kind of reasoning as is used by those who do not believe in instantaneous conversion.

===Chinese===
但圣经说：「从前偷窃的，不要再偷」（以弗所书 4:28）。这就是「向后转！」假设一个人惯于一天咒骂一百次：我们要劝他第二天只咒骂九十次，再下一天八十次，好叫他日久之后戒掉这习惯吗？救主说：「什么誓都不可起。」（马太福音 5:34）。

又假设另一个人惯于喝醉了酒、每月打妻子两次；若他只每月打一次，再后来每半年打一次，照同样的道理，这便与渐进归主一样合理了。试想当年保罗正往大马士革去，口吐威吓凶杀的话对付门徒、要把他们下在监里；倘若亚拿尼亚被差去见他，叫他不要杀他所打算杀的那么多，又叫他心里的仇恨渐渐消亡，却不要一时除尽；又倘若有人告诉他：不可一下子止住威吓凶杀的话、立刻就开始传扬基督，因为哲学家会说这变化太突然，不能持久——这便与那些不信顷刻归主的人所用的，正是同一套道理。

===Original===
Then another class say that they are afraid that they will not hold out. This is a numerous and very hopeful class. I like to see a man distrust himself. It is a good thing to get such to look to God, and to remember that it is not he who holds God, but that it is God who holds him. Some want to get hold of Christ; but the thing is to get Christ to take hold of you in answer to prayer. Let such read Psalm 121; "I will lift up mine eyes unto the hills, from whence cometh my help. My help cometh from the Lord, which made heaven and earth. He will not suffer thy foot to be moved: He that keepeth thee will not slumber. Behold, He that keepeth Israel shall neither slumber nor sleep. The Lord is thy keeper; the Lord is thy shade upon thy right hand. The sun shall not smite thee by day, nor the moon by night. The Lord shall preserve thee from all evil: He shall preserve thy soul. The Lord shall preserve thy going out and thy coming in, from this time forth, and even forevermore."

===Chinese===
还有一类人说，他们怕自己不能坚持到底。这一类人为数众多，也极有盼望。我喜欢看见一个人不信任自己。能使这样的人仰望神，并记念不是他抓住了神，而是神抓住了他，这是好事。有人想要抓住基督；但要紧的，是借着祷告，使基督抓住你。这样的人当读诗篇 121：「我要向山举目，我的帮助从何而来。我的帮助从造天地的耶和华而来。他必不叫你的脚摇动；保护你的必不打盹。保护以色列的，也不打盹也不睡觉。保护你的是耶和华，耶和华在你右边荫庇你。白日，太阳必不伤你；夜间，月亮必不害你。耶和华要保护你，免受一切的灾害；他要保护你的性命。你出你入，耶和华要保护你，从今时直到永远。」

===Original===
Someone calls that the traveler’s psalm. It is a beautiful psalm for those of us who are pilgrims through this world; and one with which we should be well acquainted.

God can do what He has done before. He kept Joseph in Egypt; Moses before Pharaoh; Daniel in Babylon; and enabled Elijah to stand before Ahab in that dark day. And I am so thankful that these I have mentioned were men of like passions with ourselves. It was God who made them so great. What man wants is to look to God. Real true faith is man’s weakness leaning on God’s strength. When man has no strength, if he leans on God he becomes powerful. The trouble is that we have too much strength and confidence in ourselves.

===Chinese===
有人称这篇为「旅客之诗」。对于我们这些在世上作客旅的人，这实在是一篇极美的诗；我们应当十分熟悉它。

神能行祂从前所行过的事。祂在埃及保守了约瑟，在法老面前保守了摩西，在巴比伦保守了但以理，又使以利亚在那黑暗的日子能站在亚哈面前。我满心感恩，因为我所提的这些人，都是与我们一样性情的人。是神使他们如此伟大。人所需要的，就是仰望神。真实的信心，就是人的软弱倚靠神的大能。当人没有力量时，只要倚靠神，他就成为刚强。难处只在于我们对自己有太多的力量与自信。

===Original===
Again in Hebrews 6:17﻿–﻿18: "Wherein God, willing more abundantly to show unto the heirs of promise the immutability of His counsel, confirmed it by an oath that by two immutable things, in which it was impossible for God to lie, we might have a strong consolation, who have fled for refuge to lay hold upon the hope set before us: which hope we have as an anchor of the soul, both sure and steadfast, and which entereth into that within the vail; whither the Forerunner is for us entered, even Jesus, made an high priest forever after the order of Melchisedec."

Now these are precious verses to those who are afraid of falling, who fear that they will not hold out. It is God’s work to hold. It is the Shepherd’s business to keep the sheep. Who ever heard of the sheep going to bring back the shepherd? People have an idea that they have to keep themselves and Christ too. It is a false idea. It is the work of the Shepherd to look after them, and to take care of those who trust Him. And He has promised to do it. I once heard that when a sea captain was dying he said, "Glory to God; the anchor holds." He trusted in Christ. His anchor had taken hold of the solid rock. An Irishman said, on one occasion, that "he trembled; but the Rock never did." We want to get sure footing.

===Chinese===
再看希伯来书 6:17–18：「照样，神愿意为那承受应许的人格外显明他的旨意是不更改的，就起誓为证。借这两件不更改的事——神决不能说谎——好叫我们这逃往避难所、持定摆在我们前头指望的人可以大得勉励。我们有这指望，如同灵魂的锚，又坚固又牢靠，且通入幔内。作先锋的耶稣，既照着麦基洗德的等次成了永远的大祭司，就为我们进入了幔内。」

这些话，对于那怕跌倒、怕自己不能坚持到底的人，实在宝贵。保守，是神的工作。看顾羊群，是牧人的事。谁曾听见羊去把牧人领回来呢？人有一种想法，以为自己既要保守自己，又要保守基督。这是错的想法。看顾他们、眷护那信靠祂的人，是牧人的工作；祂也已应许要这样行。我曾听说，一位船长临死之时说：「荣耀归与神；锚抓住了。」他所信靠的是基督。他的锚已抓住了那坚固的磐石。有一位爱尔兰人某次说：「是我在发抖；磐石却从不发抖。」我们要的，正是站上稳固的根基。

===Original===
In 2 Timothy 1:12 Paul says: "I know whom I have believed, and am persuaded that He is able to keep that which I have committed unto Him against that day." That was Paul’s persuasion.

During the late war of the rebellion, one of the chaplains, going through the hospitals, came to a man who was dying. Finding that he was a Christian, he asked to what persuasion he belonged, and was told "Paul’s persuasion." "Is he a Methodist?" he asked; for the Methodists all claim Paul. "No." "Is he a Presbyterian?" for the Presbyterians lay special claim to Paul. "No," was the answer. "Does he belong to the Episcopal Church?" for all the Episcopalian brethren contend that they have a claim to the Chief Apostle. "No," he was not an Episcopalian. "Then, to what persuasion does he belong?" "I am persuaded that He is able to keep that which I have committed unto Him against that day." It is a grand persuasion; and it gave the dying soldier rest in a dying hour.

===Chinese===
在提摩太后书 1:12，保罗说：「因为知道我所信的是谁，也深信他能保全我所交付他的，直到那日。」这便是保罗所深信的。

在不久前那场南北叛乱之战中，有一位随军牧师巡过各医院，来到一个垂死的人跟前。他发现这人是个基督徒，便问他属哪一个宗派，被告知：「属保罗那一宗。」「他是卫理公会的吗？」他问；因为卫理公会的人都认保罗。「不是。」「他是长老会的吗？」因为长老会的人尤其爱认保罗。「不是，」是回答。「他属圣公会吗？」因为圣公会的弟兄们也都自称对这位最大的使徒有分。「不是，」他不是圣公会的。「那么，他属哪一宗呢？」「我深信他能保全我所交付他的，直到那日。」这是了不起的信念；它使那垂死的兵丁在临终之时得着了安息。

===Original===
Let those who fear that they will not hold out turn to the 24th verse of the Epistle of Jude: "Now unto Him that is able to keep you from falling, and to present you faultless before the presence of His glory with exceeding joy."

Then look at Isaiah 41:10: "Fear thou not; for I am with thee: be not dismayed; for I am thy God: I will strengthen thee; yea, I will help thee; yea, I will uphold thee with the right hand of My righteousness."

===Chinese===
那怕自己不能坚持到底的人，当翻到犹大书第 24 节：「那能保守你们不失脚，叫你们无瑕无疵、欢欢喜喜站在他荣耀之前的我们的救主独一的神」

再看以赛亚书 41:10：「你不要害怕，因为我与你同在；不要惊惶，因为我是你的神。我必坚固你，我必帮助你，我必用我公义的右手扶持你。」

===Original===
Then see verse 13: "For I the Lord thy God will hold thy right hand, saying unto thee, Fear not; I will help thee."

Now if God has got hold of my right hand in His, cannot He hold me and keep me? Has not God the power to keep? The great God who made heaven and earth can keep a poor sinner like you and like me if we trust Him. To refrain from feeling confidence in God for fear of falling﻿—would be like a man who refused a pardon, for fear that he should get into prison again; or a drowning man who refused to be rescued, for fear of falling into the water again.

Many men look forth at the Christian life, and fear that they will not have sufficient strength to hold out to the end. They forget the promise that "as thy days, thy strength" (Deuteronomy 33:25). It reminds me of the pendulum to the clock which grew disheartened at the thought of having to travel so many thousands of miles; but when it reflected that the distance was to be accomplished by "tick, tick, tick," it took fresh courage to go its daily journey. So it is the special privilege of the Christian to commit himself to the keeping of his heavenly Father and to trust Him day by day. It is a comforting thing to know that the Lord will not begin the good work without also finishing it.

===Chinese===
再看第 13 节：「因为我耶和华你的神必搀扶你的右手，对你说：不要害怕，我必帮助你。」

如今神既已用祂的手握住我的右手，难道祂不能托住我、保守我吗？神难道没有保守的能力吗？那创造天地的伟大的神，必能保守像你像我这样可怜的罪人——只要我们信靠祂。因为怕跌倒而不敢向神心存确信——那就好比一个人因怕再入监牢而拒绝赦免；又好比一个溺水的人因怕再落水里而拒绝被人救起。

许多人展望基督徒的一生，便怕自己没有够用的力量能坚持到底。他们忘了那应许：「你的日子如何，你的力量也必如何」（申命记 33:25）。这叫我想起钟上的钟摆，一想到要走过那么多千万英里，便沮丧了；可当它想到这路程原是靠「滴答、滴答、滴答」走成的，便重新得着勇气，去走它每日的路。照样，基督徒特有的特权，就是把自己交在天父的保守之中，日日信靠祂。主既动了善工，就必成全它——知道这一点，实在令人安慰。

===Original===
There are two kinds of sceptics﻿—one class with honest difficulties; and another class who delight only in discussion. I used to think that this latter class would always be a thorn in my flesh; but they do not prick me now. I expect to find them right along the journey. Men of this stamp used to hang around Christ to entangle Him in His talk. They come into our meetings to hold a discussion. To all such I would commend Paul’s advice to Timothy: "But foolish and unlearned questions avoid; knowing that they do gender strifes." (2 Timothy 2:23). Unlearned questions! Many young converts make a woeful mistake. They think they are to defend the whole Bible. I knew very little of the Bible when I was first converted; and I thought that I had to defend it from beginning to end against all comers; but a Boston infidel got hold of me, floored all my arguments at once, and discouraged me. But I have got over that now. There are many things in the Word of God that I do not profess to understand.

===Chinese===
怀疑者有两种——一种有诚实的难处，另一种则只喜欢争辩。我从前以为后一种人要永远作我肉中的刺；如今他们已不再刺痛我了。我预料一路上都遇见这种人。这一类人当年也曾围着基督，要在祂的话语里找把柄陷害祂。他们来到我们的聚会，为的是开一场辩论。对这一切人，我要把保罗给提摩太的劝勉推荐给他们：「惟独那愚拙无学问的辩论，你要弃绝，因为知道这等事是起争竞的。」（提摩太后书 2:23）。无学问的辩论啊！许多初信者犯了一个可悲的错误。他们以为自己必须为整本圣经辩护。我初归主时对圣经所知甚少；我以为必须从头到尾为它辩护，抵挡一切来攻的人；可是波士顿有一个不信者把我缠住了，一下子驳倒了我所有的论据，叫我灰心丧气。但如今我已不再这样了。在神的话语里，有许多事我并不自称能明白。

===Original===
When I am asked what I do with them. I say, "I don’t do anything."

"How do you explain them?" "I don’t explain them."

"What do you do with them?" "Why, I believe them."

And when I am told, "I would not believe anything that I do not understand," I simply reply that I do.

===Chinese===
人问我对这些经文怎么办。我说：「我什么也不办。」

「那你怎样解释它们呢？」「我不解释。」

「那你怎么对待它们呢？」「哎，我信它们。」

人若对我说：「凡我所不明白的，我就不信。」我只回答：我倒信。

===Original===
There are many things which were dark and mysterious five years ago, on which I have since had a flood of light; and I expect to be finding out something fresh about God throughout eternity. I make a point of not discussing disputed passages of Scripture. An old divine has said that some people, if they want to eat fish, commence by picking the bones. I leave such things till I have light on them. I am not bound to explain what I do not comprehend. "The secret things belong unto the Lord our God: but those things which are revealed belong unto us, and to our children, forever" (Deuteronomy 29:29); and these I take, and eat, and feed upon, in order to get spiritual strength.

Then there is a little sound advice in Titus 3:9. "But avoid foolish questions, and genealogies, and contentions, and strivings about the law; for they are unprofitable and vain."

===Chinese===
有许多事，五年前还是又暗又奥秘的，如今我已得了一片亮光；我期望在永恒里还要不断发现关于神的新事。我立了一个规矩，不讨论那些有争议的经文。一位古时的神仆曾说，有些人若要吃鱼，便从挑骨头开始。这类事，我搁下等有了亮光再说。我没有义务去解释我所不明白的。「隐秘的事是属耶和华我们神的；惟有明显的事是永远属我们和我们子孙的」（申命记 29:29）；这些我就拿来，吃下去，喂养自己，好得着属灵的力量。

提多书 3:9 又有一点中肯的劝戒：「要远避无知的辩论和家谱的空谈，以及分争，并因律法而起的争竞，因为这都是虚妄无益的。」

===Original===
But now here comes an honest sceptic. With him I would deal as tenderly as a mother with her sick child. I have no sympathy with those people who, because a man is sceptical, cast him off and will have nothing to do with him.

I was in an Inquiry-meeting, some time ago, and I handed over to a Christian lady, whom I had known some time, one who was sceptical. On looking round soon after I noticed the enquirer marching out of the hall. I asked, "Why have you let her go?" "Oh, she is a sceptic!" was the reply. I ran to the door and got her to stop, and introduced her to another Christian worker who spent over an hour in conversation and prayer with her. He visited her and her husband; and, in the course of a week, that intelligent lady cast off her scepticism and came out an active Christian. It took time, tact, and prayer; but if a person of this class is honest we ought to deal with such an one as the Master would have us.

===Chinese===
可是如今来了一个诚实的怀疑者。对他，我要像母亲对待自己病中的孩子那样温柔。有些人只因一个人怀疑，便把他抛开、不肯与他往来——我对这样的人毫无同情。

前些时候我在一个询道聚会里，把一位心存怀疑的人交托给一位我认识了些日子的基督徒女士。不久我环顾四周，看见那位慕道者正大步走出会堂。我问：「你怎么让她走了？」「哦，她是个怀疑者！」是那样的回答。我跑到门口把她留住，又把她介绍给另一位基督徒工作者，那人花了不止一个小时与她交谈、为她祷告。他又去探访她和她丈夫；不出一周，那位有见识的女士便抛弃了怀疑，公开站出来，成了一个热心事奉的基督徒。这是需要时间、机敏与祷告的；但这一类人若是诚实的，我们就当照主所要我们行的去待他。

===Original===
Here are a few passages for doubting enquirers:

"If any man will do His will, he shall know of the doctrine, whether it be of God, or whether I speak of myself" (John 7:17). If a man is not willing to do the will of God he will not know the doctrine. There is no class of sceptics who are ignorant of the fact that God desires them to give up sin; and if a man is willing to turn from sin and take the light and thank Him for what He does give, and not expect to have light on the whole Bible all at once, he will get more light day by day; make progress step by step; and be led right out of darkness into the clear light of heaven.

===Chinese===
这里有几处经文，给心存疑问的慕道者：

「人若立志遵着他的旨意行，就必晓得这教训或是出于神，或是我凭着自己说的」（约翰福音 7:17）。人若不愿意遵行神的旨意，就不会晓得这教训。没有任何一类怀疑者不知道这一事实：神要他们放下罪；人若愿意转离罪恶、接受亮光、为神所已经赐下的感谢祂，又不指望一下子得着整本圣经的亮光，他必日复一日得着更多的光，一步一步地长进，被领出黑暗，进入天堂清明的亮光之中。

===Original===
In Daniel 12:10 we are told: "Many shall be purified, and made white, and tried: but the wicked shall do wickedly; and none of the wicked shall understand; but the wise shall understand."

Now God will never reveal His secrets to His enemies. Never! And if a man persists in living in sin he will not know the doctrines of God.

===Chinese===
但以理书 12:10 告诉我们：「必有许多人使自己清净洁白，且被熬炼；但恶人仍必行恶，一切恶人都不明白，惟独智慧人能明白。」

神绝不肯将祂的奥秘向祂的仇敌启示。绝不！人若执意活在罪中，他就不能明白神的道理。

===Original===
"The secret of the Lord is with them that fear Him; and He will show them His covenant" (Psalm 25:14).

And in John 15:15 we read: "Henceforth I call you not servants; for the servant knoweth not what his Lord doeth: but I have called you friends; for all things that I have heard of my Father I have made known unto you." When you become friends of Christ you will know His secrets. The Lord said, "Shall I hide from Abraham the things which I do?" (Genesis 18:17).

===Chinese===
「耶和华与敬畏他的人亲密，他必将自己的约指示他们」（诗篇 25:14）。

约翰福音 15:15 又记着：「以后我不再称你们为仆人，因仆人不知道主人所做的事。我乃称你们为朋友；因我从我父所听见的，已经都告诉你们了。」你们既作了基督的朋友，就必晓得祂的奥秘。主曾说：「我所要做的事岂可瞒着亚伯拉罕呢？」（创世记 18:17）。

===Original===
Now those who resemble God are the most likely to understand God. If a man is not willing to turn from sin he will not know God’s will, nor will God reveal His secrets to him. But if a man is willing to turn from sin he will be surprised to see how the light will come in!

I remember one night when the Bible was the driest and darkest book in the universe to me. The next day it became entirely different. I thought I had the key to it. I had been born of the Spirit. But before I knew anything of the mind of God I had to give up my sin. I believe God meets every soul on the spot of self-surrender; and when they are willing to let Him guide and lead. The trouble with many sceptics is their self-conceit. They know more than the Almighty! and they do not come in a teachable spirit. But the moment a man comes in a receptive spirit he is blessed; for "If any of you lack wisdom, let him ask of God, that giveth to all men liberally, and upbraideth not; and it shall be given him" (James 1:5).

===Chinese===
凡像神的，才最有可能明白神。人若不愿转离罪恶，就不晓得神的旨意，神也不向他启示祂的奥秘。但人若愿意转离罪恶，他必要希奇，看见亮光是怎样照进来的！

我记得有一个夜晚，那时圣经对我来说是全宇宙中最枯燥、最黑暗的一本书。第二天它却全然不同了。我以为自己得了它的钥匙。我已被圣灵重生。但在我对神的心意稍有认识之前，我必须先放下我的罪。我相信，神是在人完全降服之处遇见每一个灵魂的；是在他们愿意让祂引导带领之时。许多怀疑者的难处，在于他们的自恃。他们比全能者还要懂！他们不是怀着受教的心而来的。但一个人一怀着领受的心而来，他便有福了；因为「你们中间若有缺少智慧的，应当求那厚赐与众人、也不斥责人的神，主就必赐给他」（雅各书 1:5）。

## V / V

### A Divine Saviour / 一位神圣的救主

“Thou art **the Christ**, the Son of the living God.” / 「你是**基督**，是永生神的儿子。」

> Matthew 16:1; John 6:69 / 马太福音 16:1；约翰福音 6:69

===Original===
We meet with a certain class of Enquirers who do not believe in the Divinity of Christ. There are many passages that will give light on this subject.

In 1 Corinthians 15:47, we are told: “The first man is of the earth earthy: the second man is the Lord from heaven.”

In 1 John 5:20: “We know that the Son of God is come, and hath given us an understanding, that we may know Him that is true; and we are in Him that is true, even in His Son Jesus Christ. This is the true God, and eternal life.”

Again in John 17:3: “And this is life eternal, that they might know Thee, the only true God; and Jesus Christ, whom Thou hast sent.”

===Chinese===
我们常遇到一类慕道者，不信基督的神性。关于这题目，有许多经文可以赐下亮光。

哥林多前书 15:47 告诉我们：「头一个人是出于地，乃属土；第二个人是出于天。」

约翰一书 5:20：「我们也知道神的儿子已经来到，且将智慧赐给我们，使我们认识那位真实的，我们也在那位真实的里面，就是在他儿子耶稣基督里面。这是真神，也是永生。」

约翰福音 17:3 又说：「认识你独一的真神，并且认识你所差来的耶稣基督，这就是永生。」

===Original===
And then, in Mark 14:60: “The high priest stood up in the midst, and asked Jesus, saying, Answerest Thou nothing? What is it which these witness against thee? But He held His peace, and answered nothing. Again the high priest asked Him, and said unto Him, Art Thou the Christ, the Son of the Blessed? And Jesus said, I am: and ye shall see the Son of Man sitting on the right hand of power, and coming in the clouds of heaven. Then the high priest rent his clothes, and saith, What need we any further witnesses? Ye have heard the blasphemy: what think ye? And they all condemned Him to be guilty of death.”

Now what brought me to believe in the Divinity of Christ was this: I did not know where to place Christ, or what to do with Him, if He were not divine. When I was a boy I thought that He was a good man like Moses, Joseph, or Abraham. I even thought that He was the best man who had ever lived on the earth. But I found that Christ had a higher claim. He claimed to be God-Man, to be divine; to have come from heaven. He said: “Before Abraham was I am” (John 8:58). I could not understand this; and I was driven to the conclusion﻿—and I challenge any candid man to deny the inference, or meet the argument﻿—that Jesus Christ is either an impostor or deceiver, or He is the God-Man﻿—God manifest in the flesh. And for these reasons. The first commandment is, “Thou shalt have no other gods before Me” (Exodus 20:2). Look at the millions throughout Christendom who worship Jesus Christ as God. If Christ be not God this is idolatry. We are all guilty of breaking the first commandment if Jesus Christ were mere man﻿—if He were a created being, and not what He claims to be.

===Chinese===
马可福音 14:60 又记着：「大祭司站起来，问到中间，问耶稣说：你什么都不回答吗？这些人作见证告你的是什么呢？耶稣却不言语，一句话也不答。大祭司又问他说：你是那当称颂者的儿子基督不是？耶稣说：我是。你们必看见人子坐在那权能者的右边，驾着天上的云降临。大祭司就撕开衣服，说：我们何必再用见证人呢？你们已经听见他这僭妄的话了。你们的意见如何？他们都定他该死的罪。」

至于叫我信服基督神性的，是这一点：基督若不是神，我就不知道该把祂摆在何处，也不知道该怎样对待祂。我年幼时，以为祂像摩西、约瑟、亚伯拉罕一样是个好人。我甚至以为祂是地上有史以来最好的人。可我发现基督有更高的宣告。祂宣告自己是神而人（God-Man），是神圣的，是从天上来的。祂说：「还没有亚伯拉罕，就有了我」（约翰福音 8:58）。我无法明白这话；便被推到了一个结论——我敢请任何坦白的人来否认这推论，或答覆这论证——耶稣基督要么是个骗子、是个欺哄人的，要么祂就是神而人——神在肉身显现。理由如下。第一条诫命是：「除了我以外，你不可有别的神」（出埃及记 20:2）。请看整个基督教界千千万万人敬拜耶稣基督为神。基督若不是神，这便是拜偶像。倘若耶稣基督不过是人——倘若祂只是受造之物，而不是祂自己所宣告的那位——我们便都犯了违背第一条诫命的罪。

===Original===
Some people, who do not admit His divinity, say that He was the best man who ever lived; but if He were not Divine, for that very reason He ought not to be reckoned a good man, for He laid claim to an honor and dignity to which these very people declare He had no right or title. That would rank Him as a deceiver.

Others say that He thought He was divine, but that He was deceived. As if Jesus Christ were carried away by a delusion and deception, and thought that He was more than He was! I could not conceive of a lower idea of Jesus Christ than that. This would not only make Him out an impostor; but that He was out of His mind, and that He did not know who He was, or where He came from. Now if Jesus Christ was not what He claimed to be, the Saviour of the world; and if He did not come from heaven, He was a gross deceiver.

===Chinese===
有些人不承认祂的神性，说祂是有史以来最好的人；可祂若不是神，正因这缘故祂便不该算作好人，因为祂自称拥有一种尊荣与威严，而正是这些人声称祂对此毫无权利与资格。那便要算祂为骗子了。

又有人说，祂以为自己有神性，其实是被蒙蔽了。仿佛耶稣基督被一种幻觉与欺骗所惑，自以为比祂所是的更大！我想不出比对耶稣基督更贬低的看法了。这不仅是把祂说成骗子；更是说祂神经错乱，连自己是谁、从哪里来都不晓得。倘若耶稣基督不是祂自己所宣告的那位——世人的救主；倘若祂不是从天上来的，祂便是一个彻头彻尾的骗子。

===Original===
But how can anyone read the life of Jesus Christ and make Him out a deceiver? A man has generally some motive for being an impostor. What was Christ’s motive? He knew that the course He was pursuing would conduct Him to the cross; that His name would be cast out as vile; and that many of His followers would be called upon to lay down their lives for His sake. Nearly every one of the apostles were martyrs; and they were considered as offscouring and refuse in the midst of the people. If a man is an impostor, he has a motive at the back of his hypocrisy. But what was Christ’s object? The record is that “He went about doing good.” This is not the work of an impostor. Do not let the enemy of your soul deceive you.

In John 5:21 we read: “For as the Father raiseth up the dead, and quickeneth them; even so the Son quickeneth whom He will. For the Father judgeth no man, but hath committed all judgment unto the Son: that all men should honor the Son, even as they honor the Father. He that honoureth not the Son, honoureth not the Father which hath sent Him.”

===Chinese===
然而怎能有人读过耶稣基督的生平，竟把祂说成骗子呢？一个人假冒为善，通常总有某种动机。基督的动机是什么？祂知道祂所走的路必把祂引到十字架；祂的名必被当作恶名抛弃；祂的许多跟随者也要为祂的缘故舍命。使徒几乎没有一个不是殉道者；他们被当作万物的渣滓，被人厌弃。一个人若是骗子，他的虚伪背后总有动机。可基督的目的是什么呢？圣经上记着，祂「周流四方，行善事」。这绝不是骗子所为。不要容那灵魂的仇敌欺骗你。

约翰福音 5:21 记着：「父怎样叫死人起来，使他们活着，子也照样随自己的意思使人活着。父不审判什么人，乃将审判的事全交与子，叫人都尊敬子如同尊敬父一样。不尊敬子的，就是不尊敬差子来的父。」

===Original===
Now notice: by the Jewish law if a man were a blasphemer he was to be put to death; and supposing Christ to be merely human if this be not blasphemy I do not know where you will find it. “He that honoureth not the Son, honoureth not the Father.” That is downright blasphemy if Christ be not divine. If Moses, or Elijah, or Elisha, or any other mortal had said, “You must honour me as you honor God;” and had put himself on a level with God, it would have been downright blasphemy.

The Jews put Christ to death because they said that He was not what He claimed to be. It was on that testimony He was put under oath. The high priest said: “I adjure Thee by the living God, that Thou tell us whether Thou be the Christ, the Son of God” (Matthew 26:63). And when the Jews came round Him and said, “How long dost Thou make us to doubt? If Thou be the Christ tell us plainly.” Jesus said, “I and My Father are one.” Then the Jews took up stones again to stone Him. (John 10:24﻿–﻿33). They said they did not want to hear more, for that was blasphemy. It was for declaring Himself to be the Son of God that He was condemned and put to death. (Matthew 26:63﻿–﻿66).

===Chinese===
请留意：按犹太律法，人若亵渎，必被治死；倘若基督不过是人，这若不算亵渎，我就不知道哪里还能找到亵渎了。「不尊敬子的，就是不尊敬差子来的父。」基督若不是神，这便是彻头彻尾的亵渎。摩西、以利亚、以利沙，或任何别的人，若曾说「你们必须像尊敬神那样尊敬我」，又把自己与神并列，那便是彻头彻尾的亵渎。

犹太人把基督处死，因为祂们说祂并不是祂自己所宣告的那位。正是为此祂被起誓质问。大祭司说：「我指着永生神叫你起誓告诉我们，你是神的儿子基督不是」（马太福音 26:63）。犹太人围着祂说：「你叫我们犹疑不定到几时呢？你若是基督，就明明地告诉我们。」耶稣说：「我与父原为一。」犹太人又拿起石头来要打祂（约翰福音 10:24–33）。祂们说不要再听了，因为那是亵渎。祂正是因宣告自己是神的儿子而被定罪、被处死（马太福音 26:63–66）。

===Original===
Now if Jesus Christ were mere man the Jews did right, according to their law, in putting Him to death. In Leviticus 24:16, we read: “And he that blasphemeth the name of the Lord, he shall surely be put to death, and all the congregation shall certainly stone him: as well the stranger, as he that is born in the land, when he blasphemeth the name of the Lord, shall be put to death.”

This law obliged them to put to death everyone who blasphemed. It was making the statement that He was divine that cost Him His life; and by the Mosaic law He ought to have suffered the death penalty. In John 16:15, Christ says, “All things that the Father hath are Mine: therefore said I, that He shall take of Mine, and shall show it unto you.” How could He be merely a good man and use language as that?

No doubt has ever entered my mind on the point since I was converted.

A notorious sinner was once asked how he could prove the divinity of Christ. His answer was, “Why, He has saved me; and that is a pretty good proof, is it not?”

===Chinese===
倘若耶稣基督不过是人，那么犹太人按他们的律法把祂处死，反而是对的。利未记 24:16 记着：「那亵渎耶和华名的，必被治死，全会众总要用石头打死他。不管是寄居的是本地人，他亵渎耶和华名的时候，必被治死。」

这条律法吩咐他们把一切亵渎的人治死。正是因祂说自己有神性，祂才丧了命；按摩西律法，祂该受死刑。约翰福音 16:15，基督说：「凡父所有的，都是我的；所以我说，他要将受于我的，告诉你们。」祂怎能只是个好人，却说这样的话呢？

自从我得救归主以来，这一点上从没有半点疑惑进入我心。

曾有一个声名狼藉的罪人被问到，他怎能证明基督的神性。他回答说：「哎，祂救了我；这岂不是相当好的明证吗？」

===Original===
An infidel on one occasion said to me, “I have been studying the life of John the Baptist, Mr. Moody. Why don’t you preach him? He was a greater character than Christ. You would do a greater work.” I said to him, “My friend, you preach John the Baptist; and I will follow you and preach Christ: and we will see who will do the most good.” “You will do the most good,” he said, “because the people are so superstitious.” Ah! John was beheaded; and his disciples begged his body and buried it: but Christ has risen from the dead; He has “ascended on high; He has led captivity captive; and received gifts for men.” (Psalm 68:18).

Our Christ **lives**. Many people have not found out that Christ has risen from the grave. They worship a dead Saviour, like Mary, who said, “They have taken away my Lord; and I know not where they have laid Him.” (John 20:13). That is the trouble with those who doubt the divinity of our Lord.

===Chinese===
曾有一位不信者对我说：「慕迪先生，我一直在研究施洗约翰的生平。你为何不讲他呢？他是个比基督更伟大的人物。你讲他会作成更大的事。」我对他说：「朋友，你去讲施洗约翰；我跟着你去传基督：我们看看谁作的善工更大。」他说：「你会作成更大的善工，因为百姓是这般迷信。」啊！约翰被斩首；他的门徒求取他的遗体埋葬了：可基督已经从死里复活；祂已经「升上高天的时候，掳掠了仇敌，将各样的恩赐赏给人」（诗篇 68:18）。

我们的基督**活着**。许多人尚未发现基督已经从坟墓里复活。他们敬拜的是一位死了的救主，像马利亚那样说：「他们把我主挪了去，我不知道放在哪里」（约翰福音 20:13）。这便是那些怀疑我们主神性之人难处的所在。

===Original===
Then look at Matthew 18:20. “Where two or three are gathered together in My name, there am I in the midst of them.” “**There am** I.” Well now, if He is a mere man, how can He be there? All these are strong passages.

Again in Matthew 28:18. “And Jesus came and spake unto them, saying, All power is given unto Me in heaven and in earth.” Could He be a mere man and talk in that way? “All power is given unto Me in heaven and in earth!”

Then again in Matthew 28:20. “Teaching them to observe all things whatsoever I have commanded you; and, lo, I am with you alway, even unto the end of the world.” If He were mere man, how could He be with us? Yet He says, “I am with you alway, even unto the end of the world!”

===Chinese===
再看马太福音 18:20。「因为无论在哪里，有两三个人奉我的名聚会，那里就有我在他们中间。」「那里就有我。」那么，祂若不过是人，怎能同时在那里呢？这些都是有力的经文。

马太福音 28:18 又说：「耶稣进前来，对他们说：天上地下所有的权柄都赐给我了。」祂能只是个人，却这样说吗？「天上地下所有的权柄都赐给我了！」

马太福音 28:20 又说：「凡我所吩咐你们的，都教训他们遵守，我就常与你们同在，直到世界的末了。」祂若不过是人，怎能与我们同在？然而祂说：「我就常与你们同在，直到世界的末了！」

===Original===
Then again in Mark 2:7. “Why doth this Man thus speak blasphemies? who can forgive sins but God only? And immediately when Jesus perceived in His Spirit that they reasoned within themselves, He said unto them, Why reason ye these things in your hearts? Whether is it easier to say to the sick of the palsy, Thy sins be forgiven thee, or to say, Arise and take up thy bed and walk?”

Some men will meet you and say, “Did not Elisha also raise the dead?” Notice that in the rare instances in which men have raised the dead, they did it by the power of God. They called on God to do it. But when Christ was on earth He did not call upon the Father to bring the dead to life. When He went to the house of Jairus He said, “Damsel, I say unto thee, Arise.” (Mark 5:41).

===Chinese===
马可福音 2:7 又记着：「这个人为什么这样说呢？他说僭妄的话了。除了神以外，谁能赦罪呢？耶稣心中知道他们心里这样议论，就说：你们心里为什么这样议论呢？或对瘫子说你的罪赦了，或说起来！拿你的褥子行走，哪一样容易呢？」

有人会遇见你说：「以利沙不也叫死人复活吗？」请留意，人叫死人复活的那些罕见事例中，他们都是靠着神的大能去作的。他们求神去作。可基督在地上时，并没有求父叫死人活过来。祂到了睚鲁的家，便说：「闺女，我吩咐你起来」（马可福音 5:41）。

===Original===
He had power to impart life. When they were carrying the young man out of Nain He had compassion on the widowed mother and came and touched the bier and said, “Young man, I say unto thee, Arise.” (Luke 7:14).

He spake; and the dead arose.

And when He raised Lazarus He called with a loud voice, “Lazarus, come forth!” (John 11:43). And Lazarus heard, and came forth.

Someone has said, It was a good thing that Lazarus was mentioned by name, or all the dead within the sound of Christ’s voice would immediately have risen.

===Chinese===
祂有赐人生命的大能。他们把那少年人抬出拿因城时，祂怜悯那寡居的母亲，上前按住杠，说：「少年人，我吩咐你，起来」（路加福音 7:14）。

祂一发声，死人便起来了。

祂叫拉撒路复活时，大声呼喊说：「拉撒路出来」（约翰福音 11:43）。拉撒路听见了，便出来了。

曾有人说，幸亏祂点出了拉撒路的名字，否则凡基督声音所及之处的一切死人，都要立刻复活了。

===Original===
In John 5:25, Jesus says: “Verily, verily, I say unto you, The hour is coming, and now is, when the dead shall hear the voice of the Son of God; and they that hear shall live.” What blasphemy would this have been, had He not been divine! The proof is overwhelming, if you will but examine the Word of God.

And then another thing﻿—no good man except Jesus Christ has ever allowed anybody to worship him. When this was done He never rebuked the worshiper. In John 9:38, we read that when the blind man was found by Christ he said, “Lord, I believe. And he worshiped Him.” The Lord did not rebuke him.

===Chinese===
约翰福音 5:25，耶稣说：「我实实在在地告诉你们，时候将到，如今就是了，死人要听见神儿子的声音，听见的人就要活了。」祂若不是神，这话便是何等的亵渎！你只要查考神的话语，证据便铺天盖地、无可推诿。

还有一件事——除了耶稣基督，从来没有一个好人容许任何人敬拜自己。人这样作时，祂从未责备那敬拜者。约翰福音 9:38 记着，那瞎子被基督找着时，便说：「主啊，我信。就拜耶稣。」主并未责备他。

===Original===
Then again, Revelation 22:6, runs thus: “And he said unto me, These things are faithful and true; and the Lord God of the holy prophets sent His angel to show unto His servants the things which must shortly be done. Behold, I come quickly: blessed is he that keepeth the sayings of the prophecy of this book. And I John saw these things and heard them. And when I had heard and seen, I fell down to worship before the feet of the angel which showed me these things. Then saith He unto me, See thou do it not; for I am thy fellow-servant and of thy brethren the prophets, and of them which keep the sayings of this book, *worship God*.”

We see here that even that angel would not allow John to worship him. Even an angel from heaven! And if Gabriel came down here from the presence of God it would be a sin to worship him, or any seraph, or any cherub, or Michael, or any archangel.

===Chinese===
启示录 22:6 又说：「天使又对我说：这些话是真实可信的。主就是众先知被感之灵的神，差遣他的使者，将那必要快成的事指示他仆人。看哪，我必快来！凡遵守这书上预言的有福了！这些事是我约翰所听见、所看见的。我既听见看见了，就在指示我的天使脚前俯伏要拜他。他对我说：千万不可！我与你和你的弟兄众先知，并那些守这书上言语的人，同是作仆人的。*你要敬拜神*。」

我们在此看见，连那位天使也不容约翰敬拜他。那还是从天上来的天使！加百列若从神面前降到这里，人敬拜他也是罪；无论哪个撒拉弗，哪个基路伯，米迦勒，或哪位天使长，都是一样。

===Original===
“**Worship God**!” And if Jesus Christ were not God manifest in the flesh we are guilty of idolatry in worshiping Him. In Matthew 14:33, we read: “Then they that were in the ship came and *worshiped* Him, saying, Of a truth Thou art the Son of God.” He did not rebuke them.

And in Matthew 8:2, we also read: “And, behold, there came a leper and *worshiped* Him, saying, Lord, if Thou wilt, Thou canst make me clean.”

In Matthew 15:25: “Then came she, and *worshiped* Him, saying, Lord, help me!”

There are many other passages; but I give these as sufficient in my opinion to prove beyond any doubt the Divinity of our Lord.

===Chinese===
「*你要敬拜神*！」倘若耶稣基督不是神在肉身显现，我们敬拜祂便是犯了拜偶像的罪。马太福音 14:33 记着：「在船上的人都拜他，说：你真是神的儿子了。」祂并未责备他们。

马太福音 8:2 又记着：「有一个长大痲疯的来拜他，说：主若肯，必能叫我洁净了。」

马太福音 15:25：「那妇人来拜他，说：主啊，帮助我！」

类似的经文还有许多；但我所引的这些，在我看来已足以毫无疑问地证明我们主的神性。

===Original===
In the 14th chapter of Acts we are told the heathen at Lystra came with garlands and would have done sacrifice to Paul and Barnabas because they had cured an impotent man; but the evangelists rent their clothes and told these Lystrans that they were but men, and not to be worshipped; as if it were a great sin. And if Jesus Christ is a mere man, we are all guilty of a great sin in worshipping Him.

But if He is, as we believe, the only-begotten and well-beloved Son of God, let us yield to His claims upon us; let us rest on His all-atoning work, and go forth to serve Him all the days of our life.

===Chinese===
使徒行传第 14 章告诉我们，路司得的异教徒拿着花圈，要向保罗和巴拿巴献祭，因为他们治好了一个瘫子；可这两位传道人撕开衣服，告诉这些路司得人，他们不过是人，不配受人敬拜；仿佛那是大罪一般。倘若耶稣基督只是个人，我们敬拜祂便都犯了大罪。

可祂若正如我们所信，是神独生的、蒙爱的儿子，那么让我们顺服祂对我们所宣告的权；让我们安息于祂那全然赎罪之功上，尽我们一生的日子出去事奉祂。

## VI / VI

### Repentance and Restitution / 悔改与赔偿

「神……如今却吩咐各处的人都要悔改。」

> 使徒行传 17:30

===Original===
Repentance is one of the fundamental doctrines of the Bible. Yet I believe it is one of those truths that many people little understand at the present day. There are more people today in the mist and darkness about Repentance, Regeneration, the Atonement, and suchlike fundamental truths, than perhaps on any other doctrines. Yet from our earliest years we have heard about them. If I were to ask for a definition of Repentance, a great many would give a very strange and false idea of it.

A man is not prepared to believe or to receive the Gospel, unless he is ready to repent of his sins and turn from them. Until John the Baptist met Christ, he had but one text, “Repent ye; for the kingdom of heaven is at hand” (Matthew 3:2). But if he had continued to say this, and had stopped there without pointing the people to Christ the Lamb of God, he would not have accomplished much.

When Christ came, He took up the same wilderness cry, “Repent; for the kingdom of heaven is at hand” (Matthew 4:17). And when our Lord sent out His disciples, it was with the same message, “that men should repent” (Mark 6:12). After He had been glorified, and when the Holy Ghost came down, we find Peter on the day of Pentecost raising the same cry, “Repent!” It was this preaching—Repent, and believe the Gospel—that wrought such marvellous results then. (Acts 2:38–47). And we find that, when Paul went to Athens, he uttered the same cry, “*Now* God commandeth *all men, everywhere*, to repent” (Acts 17:30).

===Chinese===
悔改（Repentance）是圣经的根本教义之一。然而我相信，时至今日，这条真理是许多人所不大明白的。今日在悔改、重生、赎罪等根本真理上落在云里雾里、黑暗之中的人，恐怕比在几乎任何别的教义上的人都多。然而这些事，我们从小便已听闻。我若请人给悔改下个定义，许许多多人会给出极其古怪错谬的说法。

一个人若不愿为他的罪悔改、转离它们，他便没有预备好去相信、去领受福音。施洗约翰在遇见基督之前，只有一句话：「天国近了，你们应当悔改」（马太福音 3:2）。可他若只是一直这样说下去，却停在这里，不把百姓指向基督——神的羔羊——他所作成的也就有限。

基督来了，祂接过了这旷野的呼喊：「天国近了，你们应当悔改」（马太福音 4:17）。我们的主差遣门徒出去时，所带的也是同一信息：「人应当悔改」（马可福音 6:12）。祂得了荣耀之后，圣灵降下来时，我们看见彼得在五旬节那天发出同样的呼喊：「你们要悔改！」正是这样的讲道——你们当悔改，信福音——在那时作成了奇妙的结果（使徒行传 2:38–47）。我们又看见，保罗到了雅典，也发出同样的呼喊：「*如今*神却吩咐*各处的人*都要悔改」（使徒行传 17:30）。

===Original===
Before I speak of what Repentance *is*, let me briefly say what it *is not*. Repentance is not *fear*. Many people have confounded the two. They think they have to be alarmed and terrified; and they are waiting for some kind of fear to come down upon them. But multitudes become alarmed who do not really repent. You have heard of men at sea during a terrible storm. Perhaps they have been very profane men; but when the danger came they suddenly grew quiet, and began to cry to God for mercy. Yet you would not say they repented. When the storm had passed away, they went on swearing the same as before. You might think that the king of Egypt repented when God sent the terrible plagues upon him and his land. But it was not repentance at all. The moment God’s hand was removed Pharaoh’s heart was harder than ever. He did not turn from a single sin; he was the same man. So that there was no true repentance there.

Often, when death comes into a family, it looks as if the event would be sanctified to the conversion of all who are in the house. Yet in six months’ time all may be forgotten. Some who read this have perhaps passed through that experience. When God’s hand was heavy upon them it looked as if they were going to repent; but the trial has been removed—and lo and behold, the impression has all gone.

===Chinese===
在我论到悔改*是什么*之前，容我简略说一下它*不是什么*。悔改不是*恐惧*。许多人把二者混为一谈。他们以为必须受惊吓、被吓倒；他们正等着某种恐惧降临到自己身上。可是受惊吓的人成千上万，真正悔改的却无几。你曾听过海上遭遇可怕风暴之人的故事。他们也许是极其亵渎的人；可危险一来，他们忽然静下来，开始呼求神施怜悯。你总不能说他们悔改了。风暴一过，他们又照旧起誓发咒。你也许以为，神把可怕的灾祸降在埃及王和他全地之时，他是悔改了。可那根本不是悔改。神的手一挪开，法老的心比从前更硬。他一样罪也没有转离；他仍是同一个人。可见那里头并无真正的悔改。

死亡进入一家人时，事情往往看起来会因这事件而使全家所有人都归主得救。可过不了半年，这一切也许便全被遗忘了。读这篇文字的人中，或许有人曾经历过这样的事。当神的手重重压在他们身上时，他们似乎要悔改了；可试炼一挪去——你瞧，那感动已全消散了。

===Original===
Then again, Repentance is not *feeling*. I find a great many people are waiting for a certain kind of feeling to come. They would like to turn to God; but think they cannot do it until this feeling comes. When I was in Baltimore I used to preach every Sunday in the Penitentiary to nine hundred convicts. There was hardly a man there who did not feel miserable enough: they had plenty of feeling. For the first week or ten days of their imprisonment many of them cried half the time. Yet, when they were released, most of them would go right back to their old ways. The truth was, that they felt very bad because they had got caught; that was all. So you have seen a man in the time of trial show a good deal of feeling: but very often it is only because he has got into trouble; not because he has committed sin, or because his conscience tells him he has done evil in the sight of God. It seems as if the trial were going to result in true repentance; but the feeling too often passes away.

Once again, Repentance is not *fasting and afflicting the body*. A man may fast for weeks and months and years, and yet not repent of one sin. Neither is it *remorse*. Judas had terrible remorse—enough to make him go and hang himself; but that was not repentance. I believe if he had gone to his Lord, fallen on his face, and confessed his sin, he would have been forgiven. Instead of this he went to the priests, and then put an end to his life. A man may do all sorts of penance—but there is no true repentance in that. Put that down in your mind. You cannot meet the claims of God by offering the fruit of your body for the sin of your soul. Away with such a delusion!

===Chinese===
再者，悔改不是*感觉*。我看见许许多多的人在等某种感觉来到。他们想要归向神；却以为非等这感觉来到便作不到。我在巴尔的摩（Baltimore）时，每个主日都到州立监狱去向九百个囚犯讲道。那里几乎没有一个人不是够悲惨的了：他们的感觉够多。入狱的头一两个礼拜，许多人一半的时间都在哭。可他们一旦放出来，大多数人又径直回到老路上去了。其实情是，他们之所以难受，是因为被抓住了；仅此而已。照样，你曾见过一个人在受试炼时大有感觉：可这往往只因他遭了难；并非因为他犯了罪，也并非因为他的良心告诉他自己在神面前作了恶。情形似乎要引出真正的悔改；可那感觉往往也随风而逝。

再说一遍，悔改不是*禁食与苦待己身*。一个人可以禁食几周、几月、几年，却一样罪也不悔改。它也不是*懊悔*。犹大有可怕的懊悔——足以叫他出去吊死自己；可那不是悔改。我相信，他若到主那里去，俯伏在地，认了他的罪，他必蒙赦免。他非但没有这样作，反而去见祭司，然后了结了自己的性命。一个人也许会作种种苦行赎罪——其中却无真正的悔改。请你把这记在心里。你不能用身体的果子来抵偿灵魂的罪，以此应付神的要求。扔掉这种错觉吧！

===Original===
Repentance is not *conviction of sin*. That may sound strange to some. I have seen men under such deep conviction of sin that they could not sleep at night; they could not enjoy a single meal. They went on for months in this state; and yet they were not converted; they did not truly repent. Do not confound conviction of sin with Repentance.

Neither is *praying*—Repentance. That too may sound strange. Many people, when they become anxious about their soul’s salvation, say, “I will pray, and read the Bible;” and they think that will bring about the desired effect. But it will not do it. You may read the Bible and cry to God a great deal, and yet never repent. Many people cry loudly to God, and yet do not repent.

Another thing: it is not *breaking off some one sin*. A great many people make that mistake. A man who has been a drunkard signs the pledge, and stops drinking. Breaking off one sin is not Repentance. Forsaking one vice is like breaking off one limb of a tree, when the whole tree has to come down. A profane man stops swearing; very good: but if he does not break off *from every sin* it is not Repentance—it is not the work of God in the soul. When God works He hews down the whole tree. He wants to have a man turn from every sin. Supposing I am in a vessel out at sea, and I find the ship leaks in three or four places. I may go and stop up one hole; yet down goes the vessel. Or suppose I am wounded in three or four places, and I get a remedy for one wound: if the other two or three wounds are neglected, my life will soon be gone. True Repentance is not merely breaking off this or that particular sin.

===Chinese===
悔改不是*为罪自责*。这话在有些人听来也许奇怪。我曾见过人处在极深的为罪自责之中，夜间无法入睡；一顿饭也吃不出滋味。他们一连数月处在这种光景里；却仍未归主；并未真正悔改。不要把为罪自责与悔改混为一谈。

*祷告*也不是悔改。这话也许同样奇怪。许多人一旦为自己灵魂的救恩焦急起来，便说：「我要祷告，要读圣经；」他们以为这样便能产生所要的效果。可这作不到。你也许读了许多圣经、向神呼求了许多，却从未悔改。许多人向神大声呼求，却并不悔改。

还有一件事：悔改不是*戒掉某一个罪*。许许多多人都犯了这错误。一个素来酗酒的人在誓约上签了字，不再喝酒。戒掉一样罪并不是悔改。撇弃一项恶习，就像一棵本该整棵伐倒的树，只砍掉它一根枝子。一个亵渎的人不再起誓发咒了；很好：但他若没有*从每一样罪*上转离，那便不是悔改——那不是神在人心里所作的工。神作工时，是把整棵树伐倒。祂要人从各样罪上转离。设想我在海上一艘船里，发现船底有三四处漏水。我可以去堵住一个洞；可船还是要沉。又设想我身上有三四处伤，我只为一处伤寻得治法：其余两三处若置之不理，我的命不久便没了。真正的悔改，不仅仅是戒掉这一样或那一样具体的罪。

===Original===
Well then, you will ask, what is Repentance? I will give you a good definition: it is “right about face!” In the Irish language the word “Repentance” means even more than “right about face!” It implies that a man who has been walking in one direction has not only faced about, but is actually walking in an exactly contrary direction. “Turn ye, turn ye; for why will ye die?” A man may have little feeling or much feeling; but if he does not turn away from sin, God will not have mercy on him. Repentance has also been described as “a change of mind.” For instance, there is the parable told by Christ: “A certain man had two sons; and he came to the first, and said, Son, go work today in my vineyard. He answered and said, I will not” (Matthew 21:28–29). After he had said “I will not” he thought over it, and changed his mind. Perhaps he may have said to himself, “I did not speak very respectfully to my father. He asked me to go and work, and I told him I would not go. I think I was wrong.” But suppose he had only said this, and still had not gone, he would not have repented. He was not only convinced that he was wrong; but he went off into the fields, hoeing, or mowing or whatever it was. That is Christ’s definition of repentance. If a man says, “By the grace of God I will forsake my sin, and do His will,” that is Repentance—a turning right about.

Someone has said, man is born with his face turned away from God. When he truly repents he is turned right around towards God; he leaves his old life.

===Chinese===
那么你要问，悔改是什么？我给你一个好定义：就是「向后转！」在爱尔兰语里，「悔改」一词的意思比「向后转」还要重！它意味着，一个朝某个方向走的人，不仅转过了脸，而且实际上正朝完全相反的方向走。「你们转回吧，转回吧；你们何必死亡呢？」一个人也许感觉少，也许感觉多；但他若不转离罪，神必不向他施怜悯。悔改也被形容为「心思的转变」。例如，基督所讲的那比喻：「一个人有两个儿子；他来对大儿子说，我儿，你今天到葡萄园里去作工。他回答说，我不去」（马太福音 21:28–29）。他说了「我不去」之后，心里一想，就改了主意。他也许对自己说：「我对父亲说话不够恭敬。他叫我去作工，我对他说我不去。我想我是错了。」可设想他只是这样说了，却仍旧没有去，他便没有悔改。他不仅被说服自己是错的；而且下到田里去锄地、割草，或作那该作的工。这便是基督对悔改所下的定义。一个人若说：「靠着神的恩典，我要撇弃我的罪，遵行祂的旨意」——这便是悔改——一次彻底的向后转。

曾有人说，人生来便是背着脸对着神的。当他真正悔改时，他便整个转过身来向着神；他撇下了旧有的生命。

===Original===
Can a man at once repent? Certainly he can. It does not take a long while to turn around. It does not take a man six months to change his mind. There was a vessel that went down some time ago on the Newfoundland coast. As she was bearing towards the shore, there was a moment when the captain could have given orders to reverse the engines and turn back. If the engines had been reversed then, the ship would have been saved. But there was a moment when it was too late. So there is a moment, I believe, in every man’s life when he can halt and say, “By the grace of God I will go no further towards death and ruin. I repent of my sins and turn from them.” You may say you have not got feeling enough; but if you are convinced that you are on the wrong road, turn right about, and say, “I will no longer go on in the way of rebellion and sin as I have done.”

Just then, when you are willing to turn towards God, salvation may be yours.

===Chinese===
一个人能立刻悔改吗？当然能。转个身并不需要多久。一个人改主意，也不需要半年。不久前有一艘船在纽芬兰（Newfoundland）海岸沉了。当它朝岸边冲去时，有那么一刻，船长本可以下令让引擎倒转、掉头回去。倘若那时引擎倒转，船就得救了。可总有那么一刻，已经太迟了。照样，我相信在每个人的生命中，都有那么一刻，他能停下来说：「靠着神的恩典，我不再朝死亡与毁灭走下去了。我为我的罪悔改，转离它们。」你也许说你感觉不够；但你若已被说服自己走在错路上，那就向后转，说：「我不再像从前那样，行在悖逆与罪之中了。」

就在那一刻，当你愿意转向神时，救恩便可归于你。

===Original===
I find that every case of conversion recorded in the Bible was instantaneous. Repentance and faith came very suddenly. The moment a man made up his mind, God gave him the power.
