God does not ask any man to do what he has not the power to do. He would not command “all men everywhere to repent” (Acts 17:30) if they were not able to do so. Man has no one to blame but himself if he does not repent and believe the Gospel. One of the leading ministers of the Gospel in Ohio wrote me a letter some time ago describing his conversion; it very forcibly illustrates this point of instantaneous decision. He said:

“I was nineteen years old, and was reading law with a Christian lawyer in Vermont. One afternoon when he was away from home, his good wife said to me as I came into the house, ‘I want you to go to class-meeting with me tonight and become a Christian, so that you can conduct family worship while my husband is away.’ ‘Well, I’ll do it,’ I said, without any thought. When I came into the house again she asked me if I was honest in what I had said. I replied, ‘Yes, so far as going to meeting with you is concerned; that is only courteous.’

===Chinese===
我发现圣经所记每一桩归主的事都是顷刻之间发生的。悔改与信心都来得极快。一个人一下了决心，神就赐他能力。神从不叫任何人去作他没有能力作的事。祂若不是知道人能作到，便不会吩咐「各处的人都要悔改」（使徒行传 17:30）。人若不悔改、不信福音，只能怪自己，无可推诿。俄亥俄州一位领头的福音牧师前些时候写信给我，述说他归主的经过；这封信很有力地说明了顷刻决志这一点。他说：

「我十九岁，在佛蒙特一位信主的律师手下读法律。有一天下午他不在家，他那位贤德的妻子见我进屋，便对我说：『我要你今晚跟我去班会，作个基督徒，这样我丈夫不在家时你就可以带领家庭礼拜。』『好，我去，』我说，并未多加思索。等我再进屋时，她问我刚才说的话是否出于真心。我答道：『是的，就跟你去聚会这件事而言是真心；那不过是出于礼貌。』

===Original===
“I went with her to the class-meeting, as I had often done before. About a dozen persons were present in a little schoolhouse. The leader had spoken to all in the room but myself and two others. He was speaking to the person next me, when the thought occurred to me: he will ask me if I have anything to say. I said to myself: I have decided to be a Christian sometime; why not begin now? In less time than a minute after these thoughts had passed through my mind he said, speaking to me familiarly—for he knew me very well—‘Brother Charles, have you anything to say?’ I replied, with perfect coolness, ‘Yes, sir. I have just decided, within the last thirty seconds, that I will begin a Christian life, and would like to have you pray for me.’

“My coolness staggered him; I think he almost doubted my sincerity. He said very little, but passed on and spoke to the other two. After a few general remarks, he turned to me and said, ‘Brother Charles, will you close the meeting with prayer?’ He knew I had never prayed in public. Up to this moment I had no feeling. It was purely a business transaction. My first thought was: I cannot pray, and I will ask him to excuse me. My second was: I have said I will begin a Christian life; and this is a part of it. So I said, ‘Let us pray.’ And somewhere between the time I started to kneel and the time my knees struck the floor the Lord converted my soul.

===Chinese===
「我像往常一样跟她去班会。大约十二个人聚在一所小小的校舍里。领会的已经跟屋里除了我和另外两人之外的所有人都谈过话了。他正跟坐我旁边的那位说话，这时我心里忽然冒出一个念头：他等一下要问我有没有什么话要说。我对自己说：我已经决定将来某时要作个基督徒了；为何不现在就开始呢？这些念头闪过我心之后不到一分钟，他便开口了——他对我说话很亲切，因为他跟我熟得很——『查理弟兄，你有什么话要说吗？』我极其镇定地答道：『有，先生。在过去三十秒内，我刚下了决心，要开始过基督徒的生活，想请你为我祷告。』

「我的镇定使他吃了一惊；我想他几乎怀疑我的诚意。他没说什么，便过去跟另外那两个人谈了。说了几句寻常的话之后，他转向我说：『查理弟兄，你来作结束祷告好吗？』他知道我从未在公开场合祷告过。直到这一刻，我毫无感觉。这纯粹是一桩交易。我的头一个念头是：我不会祷告，我要请他免了我。第二个念头是：我已经说过要开始过基督徒生活；这便是其中的一部分。于是我说：『我们来祷告。』而在我开始跪下与我双膝触地之间的某一刻，主把我的灵魂翻转了。

===Original===
“The first words I said were, ‘Glory to God!’ What I said after that I do not know, and it does not matter, for my soul was too full to say much but Glory! From that hour the devil has never dared to challenge my conversion. To Christ be all the praise.”

Many people are waiting, they cannot exactly tell for what, but for some sort of miraculous feeling to come stealing over them—some mysterious kind of faith. I was speaking to a man some years ago, and he always had one answer to give me. For five years I tried to win him to Christ, and every year he said, “It has not ‘struck me’ yet.” “Man, what do you mean? What has not struck you?” “Well,” he said, “I am not going to become a Christian until it strikes me; and it has not struck me yet. I do not see it in the way you see it.” “But don’t you know you are a sinner?” “Yes, I know I am a sinner.” “Well, don’t you know that God wants to have mercy on you—that there is forgiveness with God? He wants you to repent and come to Him.” “Yes, I know that; but—it has not struck me yet.” He always fell back on that. Poor man! he went down to his grave in a state of indecision. Sixty long years God gave him to repent; and all he had to say at the end of those years was that it “had not struck him yet.”

===Chinese===
「我说的头一句话是：『荣耀归给神！』之后我说了什么，我自己也不知道，也不要紧，因为我的灵魂满了，除了说『荣耀』之外说不出多少。从那时起，魔鬼再不敢质疑我的归主。愿一切赞美归给基督。」

许多人在等候，他们也说不清到底等什么，只是等某种神奇的感觉悄悄临到他们——某种神秘的信心。前些年我对一个人讲道，他对我始终只有一个答复。五年之久我设法领他归向基督，他每年都说：「这事还没有『打动』我。」「朋友，你什么意思？什么事没有打动你？」「嗯，」他说，「在它打动我之前，我不打算作基督徒；可它至今还没有打动我。我看这事不像你看见的那样。」「可你不知道自己是个罪人吗？」「是的，我知道我是个罪人。」「那么，你不知道神要向你施怜悯——神那里有赦免之恩吗？祂要你悔改，到祂那里去。」「是的，我知道；可是——这事还没有打动我。」他总是退回到这一句上。可怜的人！他就在犹豫不决之中进了坟墓。神给了他整整六十年去悔改；到了那些年日的末了，他所能说的只是：这事「至今还没有打动他」。

===Original===
Is any reader waiting for some strange feeling—you do not know what? Nowhere in the Bible is a man told to wait; God is commanding you now to repent.

Do you think God can forgive a man when he does not want to be forgiven? Would he be happy if God forgave him in this state of mind? Why, if a man went into the kingdom of God without repentance, heaven would be hell to him. Heaven is a prepared place for a prepared people. If your boy has done wrong, and will not repent, you cannot forgive him. You would be doing him an injustice. Suppose he goes to your desk, and steals $10, and squanders it. When you come home your servant tells you what your boy has done. You ask if it is true, and he denies it. But at last you have certain proof. Even when he finds he cannot deny it any longer, he will not confess the sin, but says he will do it again the first chance he gets. Would you say to him, “Well, I forgive you,” and leave the matter there? No! Yet people say that God is going to save all men, whether they repent or not—drunkards, thieves, harlots, whoremongers, it makes no difference. “God is so merciful,” they say. Dear friend, do not be deceived by the god of this world. Where there is true repentance and a turning from sin unto God, He will meet and bless you; but He never blesses until there is sincere repentance.

===Chinese===
读者中可有谁在等着某种奇怪的感觉——你自己也不知道是什么？圣经里没有一处叫人去等；神如今正吩咐你悔改。

你以为一个人不愿意被赦免时，神还能赦免他吗？神若在他这种心境之下赦免了他，他会快乐吗？哎，一个人若不悔改便进了神的国，天堂对他便是地狱。天堂是为有预备之人所预备的地方。你的孩子若作了错事，又不愿悔改，你便无法赦免他。你若赦免他，便是亏待了他。设想他到你书桌前偷了 $10，挥霍掉了。你回家，仆人告诉你孩子作了什么。你问他是否属实，他矢口否认。可你终于有了确凿的证据。即便等他发现再也无法否认了，他仍不肯认这罪，反说一有机会还要再作。你会对他说：「好啦，我赦免你，」便把这事搁在那里吗？不！然而人们却说，神要拯救众人，不管他们悔改不悔改——醉鬼、窃贼、淫妇、嫖客，全都一样。「神是这么慈悲，」他们说。亲爱的朋友，不要被这世界的神所欺骗。哪里有真正的悔改、有从罪转向神的转回，祂便在那里迎见你、赐福给你；但在有真诚悔改之前，祂从不赐福。

===Original===
David made a woeful mistake in this respect with his rebellious son, Absalom. He could not have done his son a greater injustice than to forgive him when his heart was unchanged. There could be no true reconciliation between them when there was no repentance. But God does not make these mistakes. David got into trouble on account of his error of judgment. His son soon drove his father from the throne.

Speaking on repentance, Dr. Brooks, of St. Louis, well remarks: “Repentance, strictly speaking, means a ‘change of mind or purpose;’ consequently it is the judgment which the sinner pronounces upon himself, in view of the love of God displayed in the death of Christ, connected with the abandonment of all confidence in himself and with trust in the only Saviour of sinners. Saving repentance and saving faith always go together; and you need not be worried about repentance if you will believe.”

===Chinese===
大卫在他悖逆的儿子押沙龙（Absalom）身上，就在这事上犯了一个悲惨的错误。他儿子心既未改，大卫若赦免他，便是给他行了最大的不义。没有悔改，父子之间便没有真正的和好。可神并不犯这些错误。大卫因这判断上的失误而招来祸患。他的儿子不久便把父亲赶下了王位。

论到悔改，圣路易斯（St. Louis）的布鲁克斯博士（Dr. Brooks）说得好：「严格说来，悔改意即『心思或旨意的转变』；因此，它是罪人因着看见基督之死所显明的神的爱，而对自己所作的宣判，与放弃一切对自己的信靠、转而信靠罪人唯一的救主，是相连的。能救人的悔改与能救人的信心总是并行的；你只要肯相信，便不必为悔改忧虑。」

===Original===
“Some people are not sure that they have ‘repented enough.’ If you mean by this that you must repent in order to incline God to be merciful to you, the sooner you give over such repentance the better. God is already merciful, as He has fully shown at the Cross of Calvary; and it is a grievous dishonor to His heart of love if you think that your tears and anguish will move Him, not knowing that ‘the goodness of God leadeth thee to repentance.’ It is not your badness, therefore, but His goodness that leads to repentance; hence the true way to repent is to believe on the Lord Jesus Christ, ‘who was delivered for our offences, and was raised again for our justification.’”

Another thing. If there is true repentance it will bring forth fruit. If we have done wrong to anyone we should never ask God to forgive us, until we are willing to make restitution. If I have done any man a great injustice and can make it good, I need not ask God to forgive me until I am willing to make it good. Suppose I have taken something that does not belong to me. I have no right to expect forgiveness until I make restitution.

===Chinese===
「有些人不确定自己是否『悔改得够了』。你若以此表示，必须借悔改来使神倾向于向你施怜悯，那么你越早放弃这种悔改越好。神早已满有怜悯，祂在各各他的十字架上已把这完全显明出来了；你若以为你的眼泪与痛苦能打动祂，便是严重地羞辱了祂那慈爱的心——你竟不知道『神的恩慈是领你悔改的』。所以领人悔改的，不是你的坏，而是祂的善；因此，悔改的正路乃是信靠主耶稣基督，『他被交给人，是为我们的过犯；复活，是为叫我们称义。』」

还有一件事。若有真正的悔改，它就必结出果子来。我们若亏负了什么人，在我们愿意作出赔偿（restitution）之前，绝不应求神赦免我们。我若大大地亏负了什么人，又能加以补偿，那么在我愿意补偿之前，我不必求神赦免我。设想我拿了不属于我的东西。在我作出赔偿之前，我无权指望得着赦免。

===Original===
I remember preaching in one of our large cities, when a fine-looking man came up to me at the close. He was in great distress of mind. “The fact is,” he said, “I am a defaulter. I have taken money that belonged to my employers. How can I become a Christian without restoring it?” “Have you got the money?” He told me he had not got it all. He had taken about $1,500, and he still had about $900. He said “Could I not take that money and go into business, and make enough to pay them back?” I told him that was a delusion of Satan; that he could not expect to prosper on stolen money; that he should restore all he had, and go and ask his employers to have mercy upon him and forgive him. “But they will put me in prison,” he said: “cannot you give me any help?” “No, you must restore the money before you can expect to get any help from God.” “It is pretty hard,” he said. “Yes, it is hard; but the great mistake was in doing the wrong at first.”

His burden became so heavy that it got to be insupportable. He handed me the money—950 dollars and some cents—and asked me to take it back to his employers. The next evening the two employers and myself met in a side room of the church. I laid the money down, and informed them it was from one of their employees. I told them the story, and said he wanted mercy from them, not justice. The tears trickled down the cheeks of these two men, and they said, “Forgive him! Yes, we will be glad to forgive him.” I went downstairs and brought him up. After he had confessed his guilt and been forgiven, we all got down on our knees and had a blessed prayer-meeting. God met us and blessed us there.

===Chinese===
我记得在一座大城市讲道，散会时一个相貌体面的人走到我跟前。他心里极其痛苦。「实情是，」他说，「我是个亏空公款的人。我拿了属于我雇主的钱。我若不归还，怎能作基督徒呢？」「那笔钱你还在吗？」他告诉我，不全在了。他拿了约 $1,500，手头还剩约 $900。他说：「我能不能拿那笔钱去做生意，赚够了再还他们？」我告诉他，那是撒但的欺哄；他休想靠偷来的钱兴旺；他应当把所有的都归还，再去求他的雇主向他施怜悯、赦免他。「可他们要把我下在监里，」他说：「你不能帮帮我吗？」「不能，你必须先把钱还清，才能指望从神那里得着任何帮助。」「这太难了，」他说。「是的，这难；可最大的错误，是一开始作了那错事。」

他的担子越来越重，重到他承受不住了。他把那笔钱——$950 零几角几分——交给我，请我送还他的雇主。第二天晚上，那两位雇主和我在教堂的一间侧室相会。我把钱放下，告诉他们这是他们一个雇员送来的。我把经过告诉他们，说他要的是他们的怜悯，而不是公道。泪珠顺着这两个人的面颊滚下来，他们说：「赦免他！是的，我们乐意赦免他。」我下楼把他领上来。他认了罪、得了赦免之后，我们大家都跪下来，有了一次有福的祷告会。神在那里遇见我们、赐福给我们。

===Original===
There was a friend of mine who some time ago had come to Christ and wished to consecrate himself and his wealth to God. He had formerly had transactions with the government, and had taken advantage of them. This thing came up when he was converted, and his conscience troubled him. He said, “I want to consecrate my wealth, but it seems as if God will not take it.” He had a terrible struggle; his conscience kept rising up and smiting him. At last he drew a check for $1,500 and sent it to the United States Treasury. He told me he received such a blessing when he had done it. That was bringing forth “fruits meet for repentance.” I believe a great many men are crying to God for light; and they are not getting it because they are not honest.

I was once preaching, and a man came to me who was only thirty-two years old, but whose hair was very grey. He said, “I want you to notice that my hair is grey, and I am only thirty-two years old. For twelve years I have carried a great burden.” “Well,” I said, “what is it?” He looked around as if afraid someone would hear him. “Well,” he answered, “my father died and left my mother with the county newspaper, and left her only that: that was all she had. After he died the paper begun to waste away; and I saw my mother was fast sinking into a state of need. The building and the paper were insured for a thousand dollars, and when I was twenty years old I set fire to the building, and obtained the thousand dollars, and gave it to my mother. For twelve years that sin has been haunting me. I have tried to drown it by indulgence in pleasure and sin; I have cursed God; I have gone into infidelity; I have tried to make out that the Bible is not true; I have done everything I could: but all these years I have been tormented.” I said, “There is a way out of that.” He inquired “How?” I said, “Make restitution. Let us sit down and calculate the interest, and then you pay the Company the money.” It would have done you good to see that man’s face light up when he found there was mercy for him. He said he would be glad to pay back the money and interest if he could only be forgiven.

===Chinese===
我有一个朋友，前些时候归向了基督，愿意把自己和自己的财物都奉献给神。他从前与政府有过生意往来，曾占了政府的便宜。他归主之后，这件事浮上心头，他的良心责备他。他说：「我想要奉献我的财物，可神似乎不收。」他挣扎得极其厉害；他的良心不断起来击打他。末了他开了一张 $1,500 的支票，寄到美国财政部。他告诉我，他这样作之后，所得的福分何等大。那便是结出「与悔改相称的果子」来。我相信许许多多的人正向神呼求亮光；他们得不着，是因为他们不诚实。

有一次我讲道，一个人来见我，他只有三十二岁，头发却已很白。他说：「我要你注意，我的头发白了，我才三十二岁。十二年来我背着一个重担。」「哦，」我说，「是什么？」他东张西望，仿佛怕有人听见。「嗯，」他答道，「我父亲死了，留给我母亲一份县里的报纸，留下的就只有这个：那是她全部的所有。父亲死后，那报纸日渐衰微；我看见母亲正急速陷入困乏之中。房屋和报纸保了一千元险，我二十岁那年便放火烧了那房屋，领了一千元，给了我母亲。十二年来这罪一直缠着我。我试图借着放纵情欲与罪中之乐把它淹没掉；我曾咒骂神；我曾陷入不信；我曾设法证明圣经不真实；我作了所能作的一切：可这些年我一直在受折磨。」我说：「这事有出路。」他问：「怎么作？」我说：「作出赔偿。我们坐下来算一算利息，然后你把钱还给那保险公司。」你若看见那人发现仍有怜悯为他存留时脸上亮起来的样子，你也会受益。他说，只要他能蒙赦免，他乐意偿还那笔钱和利息。

===Original===
There are men today who are in darkness and bondage because they are not willing to turn from their sins and confess them; and I do not know how a man can hope to be forgiven if he is not willing to confess his sins.

Bear in mind that *now* is the only day of mercy you will ever have. You can repent now, and have the awful record blotted out. God waits to forgive you; He is seeking to bring you to Himself. But I think the Bible teaches clearly that there is *no repentance after this life*. There are some who tell you of the possibility of repentance in the grave; but I do not find that in Scripture. I have looked my Bible over very carefully, and I cannot find that a man will have another opportunity of being saved.

*Why should he ask for any more time?* You have time enough to repent now. You can turn from your sins this moment if you will. God says: “I have no pleasure in the death of him that dieth; wherefore turn, and live ye” (Ezekiel 18:32).

===Chinese===
今日有许多人落在黑暗与捆绑之中，因为他们不愿从罪里转回、不愿认罪；一个人若不愿认自己的罪，我不知道他怎能指望得着赦免。

请记住，*如今*是你仅有的蒙恩之日。你如今可以悔改，把那可怕的记录一笔涂抹。神等着赦免你；祂正寻求把你带到祂自己面前。但我想圣经清楚地教导，今生之后*再没有悔改*。有人告诉你，在坟墓里还有悔改的可能；我在圣经里找不到这话。我曾极其仔细地把我的圣经从头到尾查考过，我找不到人还有另一次得救的机会。

*他为何还要再求时间呢？*你如今已有够多的时间可以悔改。你若愿意，此刻便能转离你的罪。神说：「我不喜悦那死人之死，所以你们当回头而存活」（以西结书 18:32）。

===Original===
Christ said, He “came not to call the righteous, but sinners to repentance.” Are you a sinner? Then the call to repent is addressed to you. Take your place in the dust at the Saviour’s feet, and acknowledge your guilt. Say, like the publican of old, “God be merciful to me a sinner!” and see how quickly He will pardon and bless you. He will even justify you and reckon you as righteous, by virtue of the righteousness of Him who bore your sins in His own body on the Cross.

There are some perhaps who think themselves righteous; and that, therefore, there is no need for them to repent and believe the Gospel. They are like the Pharisee in the parable, who thanked God that he was not as other men—“extortioners, unjust, adulterers, or even as this publican;” and who went on to say, “I fast twice a week; I give tithes of all I possess.” What is the judgment about such self-righteous persons? “I tell you this man [the poor, contrite, repenting publican] went down to his house justified rather than the other” (Luke 18:11–14). “There is none righteous; no, not one.” “All have sinned, and come short of the glory of God” (Romans 3:10, 23). Let no one say *he* does not need to repent. Let each one take his true place—that of a sinner; then God will lift him up to the place of forgiveness and justification. “Whosoever exalteth himself shall be abased: and he that humbleth himself shall be exalted” (Luke 14:11).

Wherever God sees true repentance in the heart He meets that soul.

===Chinese===
基督说，祂「来，本不是召义人，乃是召罪人悔改」。你是个罪人吗？那么，悔改的呼召正是向你发出的。你当在尘土中俯伏在救主的脚前，承认你的罪。像古时的税吏那样说：「神啊，开恩可怜我这个罪人！」看祂是多么快地赦免你、赐福给你。祂甚至要称你为义，把你算为义人——靠着那位在十字架上亲身担当了你罪孽之人的义。

或许有些人自以为义；因此以为自己不必悔改、不必信福音。他们正像比喻里的那个法利赛人，感谢神说自己不像别人——「勒索、不义、奸淫，也不像这个税吏；」又接着说：「我一个礼拜禁食两次，凡我所得的都捐上十分之一。」对这样自义的人，判断是什么呢？「我告诉你们，这人[那可怜、痛悔、悔改的税吏]回家去，比那人倒算为义了」（路加福音 18:11–14）。「没有义人，连一个也没有。」「世人都犯了罪，亏缺了神的荣耀」（罗马书 3:10, 23）。不要让*任何人*说他不需要悔改。每个人都要站到自己真正的地位上——罪人的地位上；这样，神必把他举到赦免与称义的地位上。「凡自高的必降为卑；自卑的必升为高」（路加福音 14:11）。

神在哪里看见人心里有真正的悔改，祂便在那里迎见那灵魂。

===Original===
I was in Colorado, preaching the gospel some time ago, and I heard something that touched my heart very much. The governor of the State was passing through the prison, and in one cell he found a boy who had his window full of flowers, that seemed to have been watched with very tender care. The governor looked at the prisoner, and then at the flowers, and asked whose they were, “These are my flowers,” said the poor convict. “Are you fond of flowers?” “Yes, sir.” “How long have you been here?” He told him so many years: he was in for a long sentence. The governor was surprised to find him so fond of the flowers, and he said, “Can you tell me why you like these flowers so much?” With much emotion he replied, “While my mother was alive she thought a good deal of flowers; and when I came here I thought if I had these they would remind me of mother.” The governor was so pleased that he said, “Well, young man, if you think so much of your mother I think you will appreciate your liberty,” and he pardoned him then and there.

When God finds that beautiful flower of true repentance springing up in a man’s heart, then salvation comes to that man.

===Chinese===
前些时候我在科罗拉多（Colorado）传福音，听见了一件事，深深触动了我的心。那州的州长正巡视监狱，在一间牢房里发现一个少年，窗上摆满了花，像是受过极其细心的照料。州长看看那囚犯，又看看那些花，问是谁的。「这些是我的花，」那可怜的囚犯说。「你喜欢花吗？」「是的，先生。」「你在这里多久了？」他告诉他有多少年了：他判的是长刑期。州长见他这样爱花，很觉惊奇，便说：「你能告诉我，你为什么这样喜欢这些花吗？」他满怀激动地答道：「我母亲在世时，极爱花；我来到这里之后，想我若有这些花，它们便能让我想起母亲。」州长十分喜悦，便说：「好，年轻人，你既然这样重看你的母亲，我想你也必宝贝你的自由，」便当场赦免了他。

当神发现人心里有那朵真正悔改的美花生长起来时，救恩便临到那人。

## VII — Assurance of Salvation / VII —— 得救的确据

> 「我将这些话写给你们信奉神儿子之名的人，要叫你们知道自己有永生，也叫你们信奉神儿子之名。」
> ——约翰一书 5:13

===Original===
There are two classes who ought not to have Assurance. First: those who are in the Church, but who are not converted, having never been born of the Spirit. Second: those not willing to do God’s will; who are not ready to take the place that God has mapped out for them, but want to fill some other place.

Someone will ask “Have all God’s people Assurance?” No; I think a good many of God’s dear people have no Assurance; but it is the privilege of every child of God to have beyond doubt a knowledge of his own salvation. No man is fit for God’s service who is filled with doubts. If a man is not sure of his own salvation, how can he help anyone else into the kingdom of God? If I seem in danger of drowning and do not know whether I shall ever reach the shore, I cannot assist another. I must first get on the solid rock myself; and then I can lend my brother a helping hand. If being myself blind I were to tell another blind man how to get sight, he might reply, “First get healed yourself; and then you can tell me.” I recently met with a young man who was a Christian: but he had not attained to victory over sin. He was in terrible darkness. Such an one is not fit to work for God, because he has besetting sins; and he has not the victory over his doubts, because he has not the victory over his sins.

None will have time or heart to work for God, who are not assured as to their own salvation. They have as much as they can attend to; and being themselves burdened with doubts, they cannot help others to carry their burdens. There is no rest, joy, or peace﻿—no liberty, nor power﻿—where doubts and uncertainty exist.

===Chinese===
有两类人是不该有确据的。第一类：那些在教会里、却未曾归主、从未由圣灵而生的人。第二类：那些不愿意遵行神旨意的人；他们不愿站在神为他们安排的位置上，却想充任别的位置。

有人会问：「神的所有百姓都有确据吗？」没有；我想神的许多宝贝儿女并没有确据；但每一个神的儿女都有特权，能毫无疑虑地确知自己已经得救。一个人若满心疑惑，就不配事奉神。他若连自己的得救都不确定，又怎能领别人进神的国呢？我若自己也像要溺水、不知能否到达彼岸，便无法搭救别人。我得自己先站到坚固的磐石上，然后才能向弟兄伸出援手。我若自己瞎了眼，却去告诉另一个瞎子怎样能看见，他也许会回答：「先治好你自己，然后你才能告诉我。」我近来遇见一位年轻基督徒：他却未尝胜过罪。他陷在极深的黑暗里。这样的人不配为神作工，因为他有缠累他的罪；他胜不过自己的疑惑，是因为他胜不过自己的罪。

那些对自己得救没有确据的人，既无时间、也无心力为神作工。他们自己的事已够他们应付；既被疑惑重担所压，便不能帮助别人担当重担。哪里有疑惑与不确定，那里就没有安息、喜乐、平安——没有自由，也没有能力。

===Original===
Now it seems as if there are three wiles of Satan against which we ought to be on our guard. In the first place he moves all his kingdom to keep us away from Christ; then he devotes himself to get us into “Doubting Castle:” but if we have, in spite of him, a clear ringing witness for the Son of God, he will do all he can to blacken our characters and belie our testimony.

Some seem to think that it is presumption not to have doubts; but doubt is very dishonoring to God. If anyone were to say that they had known a person for thirty years and yet doubted him, it would not be very creditable; and when we have known God for ten, twenty or thirty years does it not reflect on His veracity to doubt Him.

Could Paul and the early Christians and martyrs have gone through what they did if they had been filled with doubts, and had not known whether they were going to heaven or to perdition after they had been burned at the stake? They must have had **Assurance**.

===Chinese===
如今看来，撒但有三种诡计，是我们当防备的。第一，它倾动它整个国度来拦阻我们到基督面前；然后它又竭力把我们送进「疑惑堡」（Doubting Castle）；但若我们不顾它，仍对神之子有清楚响亮的见证，它便要竭力抹黑我们的品格，作假见证败坏我们的见证。

有人似乎以为不存疑惑便是僭越；但疑惑对神是极不荣耀的。若有人说自己认识一个人三十年，却仍怀疑他，这并不光彩；我们既已认识神十年、二十年或三十年，仍怀疑祂，岂不有损祂的真实可信吗？

保罗和早期的基督徒与殉道者，若满心疑惑、不知自己被火烧死之后究竟要上天堂还是下地狱，他们能经受得住那些事吗？他们必定有**确据**。

===Original===
Mr. Spurgeon says: “I never heard of a stork that when it met with a fir tree demurred as to its right to build its nest there; and I never heard of a coney yet that questioned whether it had a permit to run into the rock. Why, these creatures would soon perish if they were always doubting and fearing as to whether they had a right to use providential provisions.

“The stork says to himself, ‘Ah, here is a fir tree:’ he consults with his mate, ‘Will this do for the nest in which we may rear our young?’ ‘Aye,’ says she; and they gather the materials, and arrange them. There is never any deliberation, ‘May we build here?’ but they bring their sticks and make their nest.

“The wild goat on the crag does not say, ‘Have I a right here?’ No, he must be somewhere: and there is a crag which exactly suits him; and he springs upon it.

===Chinese===
司布真先生（Mr. Spurgeon）说：「我从未听说有鹳鸟遇见一棵杉树，还犹疑自己有没有权利在那里筑巢；我也从未听说有沙番质疑自己究竟获准不获准跑进磐石里。哎呀，这些受造之物若老是怀疑、害怕自己有没有权使用神护理中所赐的预备，它们早就要灭绝了。

「鹳鸟自语道：『啊，这里有一棵杉树。』它与配偶商量：『这里可作我们养育幼雏的窝吗？』『可以，』她说；它们便拾取材料，一一铺排。它们从不斟酌『我们可以在这里筑巢吗？』而是衔来树枝，搭起窝来。

「悬崖上的野山羊并不说：『我在这里有权利吗？』不，它总得在某个地方；恰好有一处悬崖正合它用；它便纵身跃上。

===Original===
“Yet, though these dumb creatures know the provision of their God, the sinner does not recognize the provision of his Saviour. He quibbles and questions, ‘May I?’ and ‘I am afraid it is not for me;’ and ‘I think it cannot be meant for me;’ and ‘I am afraid it is too good to be true.’

“And yet nobody ever said to the stork, ‘Whosoever buildeth on this fir tree shall never have his nest pulled down.’ No inspired word has ever said to the coney, ‘Whosoever runs into this rock cleft shall never be driven out of it.’ If it had been so it would make assurance doubly sure.”

“And yet here is Christ provided for sinners, just the sort of a Saviour sinners need; and the encouragement is added, ‘Him that cometh unto Me I will in no wise cast out;’ ‘Whosoever will, let him take the water of life freely.’ ”

===Chinese===
「然而这些哑巴受造之物虽晓得它们神所赐的预备，罪人却不认识他救主所赐的预备。他支吾推诿，反反复复：『我可以吗？』『我怕这不是为我预备的』；『我想这不可能是指着我说的』；『我怕这好得难以成真。』

「然而从来没有人对鹳鸟说过：『凡在这棵杉树上筑巢的，他的窝永不被拆毁。』也从来没有默示的话对沙番说：『凡跑进这磐石缝里的，永不被赶出来。』若曾有这样的话，那便叫确据加倍牢靠。」

「然而这里有基督为罪人预备，正合罪人之所需；并且加上了鼓励：『到我这里来的，我总不丢弃他』；『愿意的，都可以白白取生命的水喝。』」

===Original===
Now let us come to the Word. John tells us in his Gospel what Christ did for us on earth. In his Epistle He tells us what He is doing for us in heaven as our Advocate. In his Gospel there are only two chapters in which the word “believe” does not occur. With these two exceptions, every chapter in John is “Believe! *Believe!!* **Believe!!!**” He tells us in 20:31, “But these are written, that ye might believe that Jesus is the Christ, the son of God, and that, believing, ye might have life through His name.” That is the purpose for which he wrote the Gospel﻿—“that we might believe that Jesus is the Christ, the Son of God: and that, believing, we might have life through His name” (John 20:31).

Turn to 1 John 5:13, he there tells us why he wrote this Epistle: “These things have I written unto you that believe on the name of the Son of God.” Notice to whom he writes it “You that believe on the name of the Son of God; that ye may know that ye have eternal life, and that ye may believe on the name of the Son of God.” There are only five short chapters in this first Epistle, and the word “know” occurs over forty times. It is “*Know!* **Know!!** **Know!!!**” The Key to it is **Know**! and all through the Epistle there rings out the refrain﻿—“that we might know that we have eternal life.”

I went twelve hundred miles down the Mississippi in the spring some years ago; and every evening, just as the sun went down, you might have seen men, and sometimes women, riding up to the banks of the river on either side on mules or horses, and sometimes coming on foot, for the purpose of lighting up the Government lights; and all down that mighty river there were landmarks which guided the pilots in their dangerous navigation. Now God has given us lights or landmarks to tell us whether we are His children or not; and what we need to do is to examine the tokens He has given us.

===Chinese===
如今让我们回到神的话语。约翰在他的福音书里告诉我们基督在地上为我们做了什么。在他的书信里，他告诉我们基督在天上作我们的中保，正为我们做什么。在他的福音书里，只有两章没有「信」这个字。除此两章之外，约翰福音每一章都是「信！*信！* **信！**」他在 20:31 告诉我们：「但记这些事，要叫你们信耶稣是基督，是神的儿子，并且叫你们信了他，就可以因他的名得生命。」这便是他写福音书的目的——「要叫我们信耶稣是基督，是神的儿子；并且叫我们信了他，就可以因他的名得生命」（约翰福音 20:31）。

翻到约翰一书 5:13，他在那里告诉我们他为何写这封书信：「我将这些话写给你们信奉神儿子之名的人。」请留意他写给谁——「你们这些信奉神儿子之名的人；要叫你们知道自己有永生，也叫你们信奉神儿子之名。」这第一封书信只有短短五章，而「知道」一词出现了四十多次。整卷书都是「*知道！* **知道！** **知道！**」它的钥匙便是**知道**！整卷书信都回荡着这一叠句——「要叫我们知道自己有永生」。

几年前的一个春天，我沿密西西比河顺流而下了一千二百英里；每到傍晚日落时分，你都能看见人，有时也有妇女，骑着骡子或马，或徒步，赶到河两岸的岸边来点燃政府所设的航标灯；在那条大河一路下去，都有引导领航员在危险航行中的地标。神也给了我们航标灯或地标，好告诉我们自己是不是祂的儿女；我们所当作的，便是查验祂所赐给我们的这些凭据。

===Original===
In the third chapter of John’s first Epistle there are five things worth knowing.

In the fifth verse we read the first: “And ye *know* that He was manifested to take away our sins; and in Him is no sin.” Not what I have done, but what **He** has done. Has He failed in His mission? Is He not able to do what He came for? Did ever any heaven-sent man fail yet? and could God’s own Son fail? **He was manifested to take away our sins**.

Again, in the nineteenth verse, the second thing worth knowing: “And hereby *we know* that we are of the truth, and shall *assure* our hearts before Him.” **We know** that we are of **the truth**. And if the truth make us free, we shall be free indeed. “If the Son therefore shall make you free, ye shall be free indeed.” (John 8:36).

===Chinese===
在约翰一书第三章里，有五件值得知道的事。

第五节里我们读到第一件：「你们*知道*主曾显现，是要除掉人的罪；在他并没有罪。」不是我所做的，而是**祂**所做的。祂的使命失败了吗？祂不能成就祂来的目的吗？天上所差来的人，有谁曾失败过吗？神的独生子会失败吗？**祂曾显现，是要除掉人的罪**。

再者，第十九节，第二件值得知道的事：「从此我们就*知道*我们是属真理的，并且我们的心在神面前可以*安稳*。」**我们知道**自己是属**真理**的。真理若叫我们自由，我们就真自由了。「所以天父的儿子若叫你们自由，你们就真自由了。」（约翰福音 8:36）。

===Original===
The third thing worth knowing is in the fourteenth verse, “*We know* that we have passed from death unto life, because we love the brethren.” The natural man does not like godly people, nor does he care to be in their company. “He that loveth not his brother abideth in death.” He has no spiritual life.

The fourth thing worth knowing we find in verse twenty-four: “And he that keepeth His commandments dwelleth in Him, and He in him. And hereby *we know* that He abideth in us, by the Spirit which He hath given us.” We can tell what kind of Spirit we have if we possess the Spirit of Christ﻿—a Christ-like spirit﻿—not the same in degree, but the same in kind. If I am meek, gentle, and forgiving; if I have a spirit filled with peace and joy; if I am long-suffering and gentle, like the Son of God﻿—that is a test: and in that way we are to tell whether we have eternal life or not.

The fifth thing worth knowing, and the best of all, is “Beloved, *now*.” Notice the word “**Now**.” It does not say when you come to die. “Beloved, *now* are we the sons of God; and it doth not yet appear what we shall be: but *we know* that, when He shall appear; we shall be like Him; for we shall see Him as He is” (1 John 3:2).

===Chinese===
第三件值得知道的事在第十四节：「我们*知道*我们已经出死入生，因为我们爱弟兄。」属血气的人不喜欢属神的人，也不愿与之为伍。「不爱弟兄的，仍住在死中。」他没有属灵的生命。

第四件值得知道的事，我们见于第二十四节：「遵守神命令的，就住在神里面，神也住在他里面。我们所以*知道*神住在我们里面，是因他所赐给我们的圣灵。」我们若有了基督的灵——就是基督那样的灵——便能分辨自己所得的是怎样的灵；不是程度上相同，而是种类上相同。我若是温柔、和蔼、肯饶恕人的；我若是充满平安与喜乐的灵；我若是长久忍耐、和蔼可亲，像神的儿子一样——那便是一个试验：我们便是这样察验自己到底有没有永生。

第五件值得知道的事，也是最好的，便是「亲爱的弟兄啊，*如今*」。请留意「**如今**」二字。它不是说等你临死之时。「亲爱的弟兄啊，我们*如今*是神的儿女，将来如何，还未显明；但我们*知道*，主若显现，我们必要像他，因为我们必得见他的真体」（约翰一书 3:2）。

===Original===
But some will say, “Well, I believe all that; but then I have sinned since I became a Christian.” Is there a man or a woman on the face of the earth who has not sinned since becoming a Christian? Not one! There never has been, and never will be, a soul on this earth who has not sinned, or who will not sin, at some time of their Christian experience. But God has made provision for believers’ sins. *We* are not to make provision for them; but God has. Bear that in mind.

Turn to 1 John 2:1: “My little children, these things write I unto you, that ye sin not. And if any man sin, we have an Advocate with the Father, Jesus Christ the righteous.” He is here writing to the righteous. “If any man sin, *we*”﻿—John put himself in﻿—“we have an Advocate with the Father, Jesus Christ the righteous.” What an Advocate! He attends to our interests at the very best place﻿—the throne of God. He said, “Nevertheless, I tell you the truth; it is expedient for you that I go away” (John 16:7). He went away to become our High Priest, and also our Advocate. He has had some hard cases to plead; but he has never lost one: and if you entrust your immortal interests to Him, He will “present you faultless before the presence of His glory with exceeding joy” (Jude 24).

===Chinese===
但有人会说：「这些我都信；可是自从我成了基督徒以后，我又犯了罪。」地上可有哪一个男人或女人，自成基督徒以后未曾犯罪的？一个也没有！在这地上，从来未曾有过、将来也不会有一个灵魂，在其基督徒经历中某个时候未曾犯罪、或不会犯罪。但神已为信徒的罪作了预备。*我们*不该为罪作预备；神却作了。要把这话记在心里。

翻到约翰一书 2:1：「我小子们哪，我将这些话写给你们，是要叫你们不犯罪。若有人犯罪，在父那里我们有一位中保，就是那义者耶稣基督。」他这里是写信给义人的。「若有人犯罪，*我们*」——约翰把自己也算在里面——「在父那里我们有一位中保，就是那义者耶稣基督。」何等的中保！他在最好的地方——神的宝座那里——照管我们的益处。他说：「然而我将真情告诉你们，我去是与你们有益的」（约翰福音 16:7）。他去，是要作我们的大祭司，也作我们的中保。他有些极难的案子要辩护；但他从未输过一件：你若将自己不朽的益处交托给他，他必「能保守你们不失脚，叫你们无瑕无疵、欢欢喜喜站在他荣耀之前」（犹大书 24）。

===Original===
The past sins of Christians are all forgiven as soon as they are confessed; and they are never to be mentioned. That is a question which is not to be opened up again. If our sins have been put away, that is the end of them. They are not to be remembered; and God will not mention them any more. This is very plain. Suppose I have a son who, while I am from home, does wrong. When I go home he throws his arms around my neck and says, “Papa, I did what you told me not to do. I am very sorry. Do forgive me.” I say: “Yes, my son,” and kiss him. He wipes away his tears, and goes off rejoicing.

But the next day he says: “Papa, I wish you would forgive me for the wrong I did yesterday.” I should say: “Why, my son, that thing is settled; and I don’t want it mentioned again.” “But I wish you would forgive me: it would help me to hear you say, ‘I forgive you.’ ” Would that be honoring me? Would it not grieve me to have my boy doubt me? But to gratify him I say again, “I forgive you, my son.”

And if, the next day, he were again to bring up that old sin, and ask forgiveness, would not that grieve me to the heart? And so, my dear reader, if God has forgiven us, never let us mention the past. Let us forget those things which are behind, and reach forth unto those which are before, and press toward the mark for the prize of the high calling of God in Christ Jesus. Let the sins of the past go; for “If we confess our sins, He is faithful and just to forgive us our sins, and to cleanse us from all unrighteousness” (1 John 1:9).

===Chinese===
基督徒已往的罪，一认便得着赦免；并且永不再提。这事不可再开启。我们的罪既已除掉，便告了结。它们不再被记念；神也不再提说。这是十分明白的。设想我有一个儿子，我不在家时他作了错事。我回家时，他搂着我的脖子说：「爸爸，我做了您不叫我做的事。我很对不起。求您赦免我。」我说：「好的，我的儿子，」便亲他。他擦干眼泪，欢欢喜喜地走开了。

可第二天他说：「爸爸，我希望您赦免我昨天所作的错事。」我便会说：「哎呀，我的儿子，那事已经了结；我不想再提。」「可是我希望您赦免我；听您说一句『我赦免你』，对我有帮助。」这是荣耀我吗？我的孩子疑惑我，岂不叫我伤心？但为迁就他，我又说一遍：「我赦免你，我的儿子。」

若第三天他又把那旧罪翻出来，再求赦免，那岂不叫我伤心透顶？照样，亲爱的读者，神既已赦免了我们，断不可再提过去。让我们忘记背后，努力面前，向着标竿直跑，要得神在基督耶稣里从上面召我来得的奖赏。让过去的罪过去吧；因为「我们若认自己的罪，神是信实的，是公义的，必要赦免我们的罪，洗净我们一切的不义」（约翰一书 1:9）。

===Original===
And let me say that this principle is recognized in courts of justice. A case came up in the courts of a country﻿—I won’t say where﻿—in which a man had had trouble with his wife; but he forgave her, and then afterwards brought her into court. And, when it was known that he had forgiven her, the judge said that the thing was settled. The judge recognized the soundness of the principle, that if a sin were once forgiven there was an end of it. And do you think the Judge of all the earth will forgive you and me, and open the question again? Our sins are gone for time and eternity, if God forgives: and what we have to do is to confess and forsake our sins.

Again in 2 Corinthians 13:5: “Examine yourselves whether ye be in the faith; prove your own selves. Know ye not your own selves, how that Jesus Christ is in you, except ye be reprobates?” Now examine yourselves. Try your religion. Put it to the test. Can you forgive an enemy? That is a good way to know if you are a child of God. Can you forgive an injury, or take an affront, as Christ did? Can you be censured for doing well, and not murmur? Can you be misjudged and misrepresented, and yet keep a Christ-like spirit?

Another good test is to read Galatians 5, and notice the fruits of the Spirit; and see if you have them. “The fruit of the Spirit is love, joy, peace, long-suffering, gentleness, goodness, faith, meekness, temperance: against such there is no law.” If I have the fruits of the Spirit I must have the Spirit. I could not have the fruits without the Spirit any more than there could be an orange without the tree. And Christ says “Ye shall know them by their fruits;” “for the tree is known by his fruits.” Make the tree good, and the fruit will be good. The only way to get the fruit is to have the Spirit. That is the way to examine ourselves whether we are the children of God.

===Chinese===
容我说，这一原则在法庭上也是公认的。某国——我不说在哪里——的法庭上有过一桩案子：一个人曾与妻子不和；但他赦免了她，后来却又把她告上法庭。既知他已赦免了她，法官便说事情已经了结。法官承认这条原则站得住：罪一经赦免，便无可再问。你想全地的审判者既赦免了你我，还会把这事再翻出来吗？神若赦免，我们的罪便在时间与永恒里都去了；我们所当作的，便是认罪、离罪。

又见哥林多后书 13:5：「你们总要自己省察有信心没有，也要自己试验。岂不知你们若不是可弃绝的，就有耶稣基督在你们心里吗？」请省察自己。试试你的信仰。把它拿来检验。你能赦免仇敌吗？这是知道自己是否神儿女的好法子。你能像基督那样赦免一种伤害、忍受一种冒犯吗？你能在行善而被责难时，不发怨言吗？你能在被误解、被曲解时，仍存基督那样的灵吗？

另一个好试验，是读加拉太书第 5 章，留意圣灵的果子；看你有没有。「圣灵所结的果子，就是仁爱、喜乐、和平、忍耐、恩慈、良善、信实、温柔、节制；这样的事，没有律法禁止。」我若有圣灵的果子，便必有圣灵。没有圣灵便不能有果子，正如没有橘树便不能有橘子。基督说：「所以，凭着他们的果子就可以认出他们来」；「因为看果子就可以知道树」。把树弄好了，果子也就好了。得果子的惟一法子，就是有圣灵。这便是省察自己是否神儿女之法。

===Original===
Then there is another very striking passage. In Romans 8:9, Paul says: “Now, if any man have not the Spirit of Christ, he is none of His.” That ought to settle the question, even though one may have gone through all the external forms that are considered necessary by some to constitute a member of a Church. Read Paul’s life, and put yours alongside of it. If your life resembles his, it is a proof that you are born again﻿—that you are a new creature in Christ Jesus.

But although you may be born again, it will require time to become a full-grown Christian. Justification is instantaneous; but sanctification is a lifework. We are to grow in wisdom. Peter says “Grow in grace, and in the knowledge of our Lord and Saviour Jesus Christ” (2 Peter 3:18); and in the first chapter of his Second Epistle, “Add to your faith virtue; and to virtue knowledge; and to knowledge temperance; and to temperance patience; and to patience godliness; and to godliness brotherly kindness; and to brotherly kindness charity. For if these things be in you and abound they make you that ye shall neither be barren nor unfruitful in the knowledge of our Lord Jesus Christ.” So that we are to add grace to grace. A tree may be perfect in its first year of growth; but it does not attain its maturity. So with the Christian: he may be a true child of God, but not a matured Christian. The eighth of Romans is very important, and we should be very familiar with it. In the fourteenth verse the apostle says: “For as many as are led by the Spirit of God they are the sons of God.” Just as the soldier is led by his captain, the pupil by his teacher, or the traveller by his guide; so the Holy Spirit will be the guide of every true child of God.

===Chinese===
此外还有一段非常发人深省的经文。罗马书 8:9，保罗说：「如果神的灵住在你们心里，你们就不属肉体，乃属圣灵了。人若没有基督的灵，就不是属基督的。」这话便该了结一切争论，即便一个人也许已经行过种种外面形式，而有人认为那些形式是构成教会成员所必需的。请读保罗的生平，再把你自己的放在旁边。你的生命若与他相似，便是你已经重生——你在基督耶稣里成了新造之物的明证。

但你虽然已经重生，要长成一个完全长成的基督徒，仍需时日。称义是顷刻之间的事；成圣却是一生的功夫。我们当在智慧上有长进。彼得说：「要在我们主救主耶稣基督的恩典和知识上有长进」（彼得后书 3:18）；又在他的第二封书信第一章里说：「有了信心，又要加上德行；有了德行，又要加上知识；有了知识，又要加上节制；有了节制，又要加上忍耐；有了忍耐，又要加上虔敬；有了虔敬，又要加上爱弟兄的心；有了爱弟兄的心，又要加上爱众人的心。你们若充充足足地有这几样，就不至于在认识我们的主耶稣基督上闲懒不结果子了。」所以我们当恩上加恩。一棵树在生长的头一年也许已经完全；却未到成熟之时。基督徒也是这样：他也许已是神真正的儿女，却还不是长成的基督徒。罗马书第八章十分重要，我们当十分熟悉。第十四节，使徒说：「因为凡被神的灵引导的，都是神的儿子。」正如士兵被他的将帅引导，学生被他的老师引导，旅客被他的向导引导；照样，圣灵要作每一个神真儿女的向导。

===Original===
Then let me call your attention to another fact. All Paul’s teaching in nearly every Epistle rings out the doctrine of assurance. He says in 2 Corinthians 5:1: “For we *know* that if our earthly house of this tabernacle were dissolved, we have a building of God, a house not made with hands, eternal in the heavens.” He had a title to the mansions above, and he says﻿—*I know it*. He was not living in uncertainty. He said: “I have a desire to depart and be with Christ” (Philippians 1:23); and if he had been uncertain he would not have said that. Then in Colossians 3:4, he says: “When Christ, who is our life, shall appear, then shall ye also appear with Him in glory.” I am told that Dr. Watts’ tombstone bears this same passage of Scripture. There is no doubt there.

Then turn to Colossians 1:12: “Giving thanks unto the Father, which **hath** made us meet to be partakers of the inheritance of the saints in light; who *hath* delivered us from the power of darkness, and *hath* translated us into the kingdom of His dear Son.”

===Chinese===
再者，请让我提醒你们注意另一件事。保罗在几乎每一封书信里的教导，都响彻确据之道。他在哥林多后书 5:1 说：「我们原*知道*，我们这地上的帐棚若拆毁了，必得神所造，不是人手所造，在天上永存的房屋。」他对天上的居所拥有所有权，并且他说——*我知道*。他不是活在不确定之中。他说：「情愿离世与基督同在」（腓立比书 1:23）；他若不确定，便不会这样说。又在歌罗西书 3:4，他说：「基督是我们的生命，他显现的时候，你们也要与他一同显现在荣耀里。」我听说华兹博士（Dr. Watts）的墓碑上刻的正是这节圣经。那里没有疑惑。

再翻到歌罗西书 1:12：「又感谢父，叫我们能与众圣徒在光明中同得基业。他救了我们脱离黑暗的权势，把我们迁到他爱子的国里。」

===Original===
Three *haths*: “**hath** made us meet;” “**hath** delivered us;” and “**hath** translated us.” It does not say that He is going to make us meet; that He is going to deliver; that He is going to translate.

Then again in verse 14th: “In whom we have redemption through His blood, even the forgiveness of sins.” We are either forgiven or we are not, we should not give ourselves any rest until we get into the kingdom of God; nor until we can each look up and say, “I know that if my earthly house of this tabernacle were dissolved, I have a building of God, a house not made with hands, eternal in the heavens” (2 Corinthians 5:1).

Look at Romans 8:32: “He that spared not His own Son, but delivered Him up for us all, how shall He not with Him also freely give us all things?” If He gave us His Son, will He not give us the certainty that He is ours. I have heard this illustration. There was a man who owed $10,000, and would have been made a bankrupt, but a friend came forward and paid the sum. It was found afterwards that he owed a few dollars more; but he did not for a moment entertain a doubt that, as his friend had paid the larger amount, he would also pay the smaller. And we have high warrant for saying that if God has given us His Son He will with Him also freely give us all things; and if we want to realize our salvation beyond controversy He will not leave us in darkness.

===Chinese===
三个「已」字：「**已**叫我们能与……同得基业」；「**已**救了我们脱离黑暗的权势」；「**已**把我们迁到他爱子的国里」。它不是说祂将要叫我们合格；不是说祂将要救；不是说祂将要迁。

再者在第十四节：「我们在爱子里得蒙救赎，罪过得以赦免。」我们要么已蒙赦免，要么未蒙赦免；我们不可给自己安息，直到进入神的国；直到各人能抬头说：「我知道，我们这地上的帐棚若拆毁了，必得神所造，不是人手所造，在天上永存的房屋」（哥林多后书 5:1）。

请看罗马书 8:32：「神既不爱惜自己的儿子，为我们众人舍了，岂不也把万物和他一同白白地赐给我们吗？」祂既把祂的儿子赐给我们，岂不也把「祂属于我们」的确据赐给我们吗？我听过这样一个比方。有一个人欠了一万元，本要破产，但一位朋友出来替他付了那笔款。后来发现他还欠了几块钱；他却毫不怀疑：朋友既已付了那大数，也必付这小数。我们有崇高的根据可以说，神既已将祂的儿子赐给我们，也必把他一同白白地赐给我们万物；我们若要无可争议地确知自己的救恩，祂必不把我们留在黑暗中。

===Original===
Again in the 33rd verse: “Who shall lay anything to the charge of God’s elect? It is God that justifieth. Who is he that condemneth? It is Christ that died, yea rather, that is risen again, who is even at the right hand of God, who also maketh intercession for us. Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? shall tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword? As it is written, For Thy sake we are killed all the day long; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter. Nay, in all these things we are more than conquerors through Him that loved us. For I am persuaded that neither death, nor life, nor angels, nor principalities, nor powers, nor things present, nor things to come, nor height, nor depth, nor any other creature, shall be able to separate us from the love of God, which is in Christ Jesus our Lord.”

===Chinese===
又见第三十三节：「谁能控告神所拣选的人呢？有神称他们为义了。谁能定他们的罪呢？有基督耶稣已经死了，而且从死里复活，现今在神的右边，也替我们祈求。谁能使我们与基督的爱隔绝呢？难道是患难吗？是困苦吗？是逼迫吗？是饥饿吗？是赤身露体吗？是危险吗？是刀剑吗？如经上所记：『我们为你的缘故终日被杀，人看我们如将宰的羊。』然而，靠着爱我们的主，在这一切的事上已经得胜有余了。因为我深信无论是死，是生，是天使，是掌权的，是有能的，是现在的事，是将来的事，是高处的，是低处的，是别的受造之物，都不能叫我们与神的爱隔绝；这爱是在我们的主基督耶稣里的。」

===Original===
That has the right ring in it. There is Assurance for you. “I **Know**.” Do you think that the God who has justified me will condemn me? That is quite an absurdity. God is going to save us so that neither men, angels, nor devils, can bring any charge against us or Him. He will have the work complete.

Job lived in a darker day than we do; but we read in Job 19:25: “I *know* that my Redeemer liveth, and that He shall stand in the latter day upon the earth.”

The same confidence breathes through Paul’s last words to Timothy: “For the which cause I also suffer these things: nevertheless I am not ashamed; for I *know* whom I have believed, and am persuaded that He is able to keep that which I have committed unto Him against that day.” It is not a matter of doubt, but of knowledge. “I know.” “I am persuaded.” The word “Hope,” is not used in the Scripture to express doubt. It is used in regard to the second coming of Christ, or to the resurrection of the body. We do not say that we “hope” we are Christians. I do not say that I “hope” I am an American, or that I “hope” I am a married man. These are settled things. I may say that I “hope” to go back to my home, or I hope to attend such a meeting. I do not say that I “hope” to come to this country, for I am here. And so, if we are born of God we know it; and He will not leave us in darkness if we search the Scriptures.

===Chinese===
这话里有当有的响亮气概。这便是你的确据。「我**知道**。」你想那称我为义的神，还会定我的罪吗？那简直是荒谬。神要这样救我们，叫人、天使、魔鬼都不能对任何人——或对祂自己——提出任何控告。祂要把这工做全。

约伯所住的时代比我们更黑暗；但我们在约伯记 19:25 读到：「我*知道*我的救赎主活着，末了必站立在地上。」

同样的把握也贯串在保罗对提摩太的临别之言里：「为这缘故，我也受这些苦难；然而我不以为耻；因为*知道*我所信的是谁，也深信他能保全我所交付他的，直到那日。」这不是疑惑的事，而是知道的事。「我知道。」「我深信。」圣经里「望」字并不是用来表达疑惑的。它用于论到基督的再来，或身体的复活。我们不说我们「盼望」自己是基督徒。我不会说我「盼望」自己是美国人，或「盼望」自己是已婚之人。这些都是已定的事。我可以说我「盼望」回家，或我盼望参加某次聚会。我不会说我「盼望」来到这个国家，因为我已经在这里。照样，我们若已从神而生，便知道；我们若查考圣经，祂必不把我们留在黑暗中。

===Original===
Christ taught this doctrine to His seventy disciples when they returned elated with their success, saying, “Lord, even the devils are subject unto us through Thy name.” The Lord seemed to check them, and said that He would give them something to rejoice in. “Notwithstanding in this rejoice not, that the spirits are subject unto you; but rather rejoice because your names are written in heaven.” (Luke 10:20).

It is the privilege of every one of us to know, beyond a doubt, that our salvation is sure. Then we can work for others. But if we are doubtful of our own salvation, we are not fit for the service of God.

Another passage is John 5:24: “Verily, verily I say unto you: He that heareth my word, and believeth on Him that sent Me, hath everlasting life, and shall not come into ‘*judgment*,’ ” (the new translation has it so), “but is passed from death unto life.”

===Chinese===
基督把这教训教导祂的七十个门徒，是在他们因事工成功而兴高采烈地回来时——他们说：「主啊，因你的名，就是鬼也服了我们。」主似乎要纠正他们，说祂要给他们一件值得喜乐的事。「然而不要因鬼服了你们就欢喜，要因你们的名记录在天上欢喜。」（路加福音 10:20）。

我们每一个人都有特权，毫无疑虑地知道自己的救恩是稳当的。这样我们才能为别人作工。但我们若对自己得救疑惑，便不配事奉神。

还有一节经文是约翰福音 5:24：「我实实在在地告诉你们：那听我话、又信差我来者的，就有永生，不至于*定罪*」（新译本如此），「是已经出死入生了」。

===Original===
Some people say that you never can tell till you are before the great white throne of Judgment whether you are saved or not. Why, my dear friend, if your life is hid with Christ in God, you are not coming into judgment for your sins. We may come into judgment for reward. This is clearly taught where the lord reckoned with the servant to whom five talents had been given, and who brought other five talents saying, “Lord, thou deliveredst unto me five talents; behold, I have gained beside them five talents more. His lord said unto him, Well done, thou good and faithful servant: thou hast been faithful over a few things; I will make thee ruler over many things; enter thou into the joy of thy lord.” (Matthew 25:20﻿–﻿21). We shall be judged for our stewardship. That is one thing; but salvation﻿—eternal life﻿—is another.

Will God demand payment twice of the debt which Christ has paid for us? If Christ bear my sins in His own body on the tree, am I to answer for them as well?

Isaiah tells us that, “He was wounded for our transgressions; He was bruised for our iniquities; the chastisement of our peace was upon Him: and with His stripes we are healed.” In Romans 4:25, we read: He “was delivered for our offences, and was raised again for our justification.” Let us believe, and get the benefit of His finished work.

===Chinese===
有人说，直到你站在白色大宝座前，你才能知道自己得救了没有。哎，亲爱的朋友，你的生命若与基督一同藏在神里面，你就不会为你的罪受审判。我们也许会为赏赐受审判。这一点在那仆人的事上教导得清楚：那仆人受了五千银子，又带着另外五千来，说：『主啊，你交给我五千；看哪，我又赚了五千。』主人说：『好，你这又良善又忠心的仆人：你在不多的事上有忠心，我要把许多事派你管理；可以进来享受你主人的快乐。』（马太福音 25:20–21）。我们将照管家的职分受审。那是一件事；救恩——永生——却是另一件事。

基督既已替我们偿清了债，神还要向我们追讨两遍吗？基督既在自己的身体上担当了我的罪，我还要为这些罪交账吗？

以赛亚告诉我们：「哪知他为我们的过犯受害，为我们的罪孽压伤；因他受的刑罚我们得平安，因他受的鞭伤我们得医治。」罗马书 4:25，我们读到：他被「交给人，是为我们的过犯；复活，是为叫我们称义」。让我们相信，得他那已成之工的益处。

===Original===
Then again in John 10:9: “I am the door: by Me if any man enter in he shall be saved, and shall go in and out, and find pasture.” That is the promise. Then the 27th verse, “My sheep hear my voice; and I know them, and they follow Me. And I give unto them eternal life; and they shall never perish, neither shall any man pluck them out of my hand. My father which gave them is greater than all; and no man is able to pluck them out of my Father’s hand.” Think of that! The Father, the Son, and the Holy Ghost, are pledged to keep us. You see that it is not only the Father, not only the Son, but the three persons of the Triune God.

Now, a great many people want some token outside of God’s word. That habit always brings doubt. If I made a promise to meet a man at a certain hour and place tomorrow, and he were to ask me for my watch as a token of my sincerity, it would be a slur on my truthfulness. We must not question what God has said: He has made statement after statement, and multiplied figure upon figure. Christ says: “I am the door; by Me if any man enter in he shall be saved.” “I am the Good Shepherd, and know My sheep, and am known of Mine.” “I am the light of the world; he that followeth Me shall not walk in darkness, but shall have the light of life.” “I am the truth;” receive Me, and you will have the truth; for I am the embodiment of truth. Do you want to know the way? “I am the way:” follow Me, and I will lead you into the kingdom. Are you hungering after righteousness? “I am the Bread of life:” if you eat of Me you shall never hunger. “I am the Water of life:” if you drink of this water it shall be within you “a well of water springing up unto everlasting life.” “I am the resurrection and the life: he that believeth in Me, though he were dead, yet shall he live; and whosoever liveth and believeth in Me shall never die.” (John 11:2﻿–﻿26).

===Chinese===
又在约翰福音 10:9：「我就是门，凡从我进来的，必然得救，并且出入得草吃。」这是应许。再到第二十七节：「我的羊听我的声音，我也认识他们，他们也跟着我。我又赐给他们永生，他们永不灭亡，谁也不能从我手里把他们夺去。我父把羊赐给我，他比万有都大，谁也不能从我父手里把他们夺去。」请想想这话！父、子、圣灵，都保证要保守我们。你看，这不仅是父，不仅是子，而是三位一体（Triune God）之神三个位格。

如今，许多人要在神的话语之外另求一个凭据。这习惯总带来疑惑。我若应许某人在明日的某时某地相会，他却向我要我的手表作为我诚意的凭据，那便有辱我的诚实。我们不可质疑神所说的话：祂已经一项又一项地陈述，一个比方又一个比方地增加。基督说：「我就是门，凡从我进来的，必然得救。」「我是好牧人，我认识我的羊，我的羊也认识我。」「我是世界的光；跟从我的，就不在黑暗里走，必要得着生命的光。」「我就是真理」；接受我，你就有了真理；因为我是真理的化身。你想要知道道路吗？「我就是道路」；跟从我，我就领你进神的国。你饥渴慕义吗？「我是生命的粮」；你若吃我就永不饿。「我是生命的活水」；你若喝这水，它便在你里头「成为泉源，直涌到永生」。「复活在我，生命也在我；信我的人虽然死了，也必复活；凡活着信我的人必永远不死。」（约翰福音 11:2–26）。

===Original===
Let me remind you where our doubts come from. A good many of God’s dear people never get beyond knowing themselves servants. He calls us “friends.” If you go into a house you will soon see the difference between the servant and the son. The son walks at perfect liberty all over the house; he is at home. But the servant takes a subordinate place. What we want is to get beyond servants. We ought to realize our standing with God as sons and daughters. He will not “un-child” His children. God has not only adopted us, but we are His by birth: we have been born into His kingdom. My little boy was as much mine when he was a day old as now that he is fourteen. He was *my son*; although it did not appear what he would be when he attained manhood. He is mine; although he may have to undergo probation under tutors and governors. The children of God are not perfect; but we are perfectly His children.

Another origin of doubts is looking at ourselves. If you want to be wretched and miserable, filled with doubts from morning till night, look at yourselves. “Thou wilt keep him in perfect peace whose mind is stayed on Thee.” (Isaiah 26:3). Many of God’s dear children are robbed of joy because they keep looking at themselves.

Someone has said: “There are three ways to look. If you want to be wretched, look within; if you wish to be distracted, look around; but if you would have peace, look up.” Peter looked away from Christ, and he immediately began to sink. The Master said to him: “O thou of little faith! Wherefore didst thou doubt?” (Matthew 14:31). He had God’s eternal word, which was sure footing, and better than either marble, granite or iron; but the moment he took his eyes off Christ down he went. Those who look around cannot see how unstable and dishonoring is their walk. We want to look straight at the “Author and Finisher of our faith.”

===Chinese===
让我提醒你，我们的疑惑从何而来。神许多宝贝的儿女从不越过「知道自己只是仆人」这一步。祂却称我们为「朋友」。你进一户人家，很快便看出仆人与儿子的分别。儿子在家中各处行走，完全自由；他自在无拘。仆人却居于从属地位。我们所要的，是越过仆人的地步。我们当认识自己作为儿女与神所处的地位。祂不会把祂的儿女「取消作儿女」。神不仅收纳了我们，我们更是因生而属祂：我们已经生在祂的国里。我的小儿子在他出生的那一天是我的儿子，如今他十四岁了，同样是我的儿子。他*是我的儿子*；尽管他成年后会成为怎样的人，那时还未显明。他是我的；尽管他也许要在师傅和管家之下经历试炼。神的儿女并不完全；但我们是完全属于祂的儿女。

疑惑的另一个来源，是看自己。你若要愁苦悲惨，从早到晚满心疑惑，便看你自己。「坚心倚赖你的，你必保守他十分平安。」（以赛亚书 26:3）。神许多宝贝的儿女被夺去喜乐，是因为他们一直看自己。

有人说过：「看，有三种看法。你若要愁苦，向内看；你若要心烦意乱，向周围看；你若要得平安，向上看。」彼得把目光从基督身上移开，便立刻往下沉。主对他说：「你这小信的人哪，为什么疑惑呢？」（马太福音 14:31）。他原有神永恒的话语作立足之地，那比大理石、花岗岩、铁都稳固；可他一移开眼看基督，便沉了下去。那些向周围看的人，看不出自己的脚步何等不稳、何等不荣耀神。我们要直直地仰望「我们信心的创始成终者」。

===Original===
When I was a boy I could only make a straight track in the snow, by keeping my eyes fixed upon a tree or some object before me. The moment I took my eye off the mark set in front of me, I walked crooked. It is only when we look fixedly on Christ that we find perfect peace. After He rose from the dead He showed His disciples His hands and His feet. (Luke 24:40). That was the ground of their peace. If you want to scatter your doubts, look at the blood; and if you want to increase your doubts, look at yourself. You will get doubts enough for years by being occupied with yourself for a few days.

Then again: look at what He is, and at what He has done; not at what you are, and what you have done. That is the way to get peace and rest.

Abraham Lincoln issued a proclamation declaring the emancipation of three millions of slaves. On a certain day their chains were to fall off, and they were to be free. The proclamation was put up on the trees and fences wherever the Northern Army marched. A good many slaves could not read: but others read the proclamation, and most of them believed it; and on a certain day a glad shout went up, “We are free!” Some did not believe it, and stayed with their old masters; but it did not alter the fact that they were free. Christ, the Captain of our salvation, has proclaimed freedom to all who have faith in Him. Let us take Him at His word. Their feelings would not have made the slaves free. The power must come from the outside. Looking at ourselves will not make us free, but it is looking to Christ with the eye of faith.

===Chinese===
我小时候，在雪地里要走成一条直线，惟有把眼睛盯住前面一棵树或某个目标。我一移开盯在前面目标上的眼，便走歪了。惟有定睛仰望基督，我们才得完全的平安。他从死里复活以后，向门徒展示他的手和脚。（路加福音 24:40）。那便是他们平安的根基。你若要驱散疑惑，便看那宝血；你若要增加疑惑，便看你自己。你只消几天专注于自己，便够你受用多年的疑惑了。

再者：看祂是谁，看祂所作的；不要看你是谁，看你所作的。这便是得平安与安息之法。

亚伯拉罕·林肯（Abraham Lincoln）曾颁布一道宣言，宣告三百万奴隶获得解放。到了某一日，他们的锁链要脱落，他们要成为自由人。宣言张贴在北军所到之处的树上和栅栏上。许多奴隶不识字；但别人把宣言读给他们听，他们多半都信了；到了那某一日，一声欢呼响起：「我们自由了！」有些人不信，仍留在旧主人那里；但这并不改变他们已经自由这一事实。基督，我们救恩的元帅，已向一切信他的人宣告了自由。让我们信他的话。奴隶们的情感不会使他们自由。那能力必须从外面来。看我们自己不会使我们自由，使我们自由的是用信心之眼仰望基督。

===Original===
Bishop Ryle has strikingly said: “Faith is the root, and Assurance the flower.” Doubtless you can never have the flower without the root; but it is no less certain you may have the root, and not the flower.

“Faith is that poor trembling woman who came behind Jesus in the press, and touched the hem of His garment. (Mark 5:27). Assurance is Stephen standing calmly in the midst of his murderers, and saying, ‘I see the heavens opened, and the Son of Man standing on the right hand of God’ ” (Acts 7:56).

===Chinese===
莱尔主教（Bishop Ryle）曾精辟地说：「信心是根，确据是花。」你当然不能有花而无根；但同样确实的是，你可以有根而无花。

「信心，是那个患了血漏、在人群中挤到耶稣背后、摸他衣裳繸子的战战兢兢的妇人。（马可福音 5:27）。确据，是司提反从容地站在杀人者中间，说：『我看见天开了，人子站在神的右边』」（使徒行传 7:56）。

===Original===
“Faith is the penitent thief, crying, ‘Lord, remember me’ (Luke 23:42). Assurance is Job sitting in the dust, covered with sores, and saying, ‘I know that my Redeemer liveth;’ ‘Though He slay me, yet will I trust in Him’ ” (Job 19:25; 13:15).

“Faith is Peter’s drowning cry, as he began to sink, ‘Lord, save me!’ (Matthew 14:30). Assurance is that same Peter declaring before the Council, in after-times, ‘This is the stone which was set at nought of you builders, which is become the head of the corner: neither is there salvation in any other; for there is none other name under heaven given among men whereby we must be saved’ ” (Acts 4:11﻿–﻿12).

===Chinese===
「信心，是那个悔改的强盗，喊着说：『耶稣啊，你得国降临的时候，求你记念我。』（路加福音 23:42）。确据，是约伯坐在尘土中，浑身生疮，说：『我知道我的救赎主活着』；『他虽杀我，我仍要信靠他』」（约伯记 19:25; 13:15）。

「信心，是彼得开始往下沉时那呼救的声音：『主啊，救我！』（马太福音 14:30）。确据，是后来同一位彼得在公会面前宣告说：『他是你们匠人所弃的石头，已成了房角的头块石头。除他以外，别无拯救；因为在天下人间，没有赐下别的名，我们可以靠着得救』」（使徒行传 4:11–12）。

===Original===
“Faith is the anxious, trembling voice, ‘Lord, I believe; help Thou mine unbelief!’ (Mark 9:24). Assurance is the confident challenge, ‘Who shall lay anything to the charge of God’s elect? Who is he that condemneth?’ ” (Romans 8:33﻿–﻿34).

“Faith is Saul praying in the house of Judas at Damascus, sorrowful, blind, and alone. (Acts 9:11). Assurance is Paul, the aged prisoner, looking calmly into the grave, and saying, ‘I know whom I have believed.’ ‘There is a crown laid up for me’ ” (2 Timothy 1:12; 4:8).

===Chinese===
「信心，是那焦急战抖的声音：『主啊，我信，但我信不足，求主帮助！』（马可福音 9:24）。确据，是那满有把握的挑战：『谁能控告神所拣选的人呢？谁是定人罪的呢？』」（罗马书 8:33–34）。

「信心，是扫罗在大马士革犹大家里祷告，忧愁、瞎眼、孤单。（使徒行传 9:11）。确据，是年老的囚犯保罗，从容地凝视坟墓，说：『我知道我所信的是谁。』『有公义的冠冕为我存留』」（提摩太后书 1:12; 4:8）。

===Original===
“Faith is **Life**. How great the blessing! Who can tell the gulf between life and death? And yet life may be weak, sickly, unhealthy, painful, trying, anxious, worn, burdensome, joyless, smileless, to the very end.

“Assurance is *more than life*. It is health, strength, power, vigor, activity, energy, manliness, beauty.”

===Chinese===
「信心是**生命**。这福分何等大！谁能说清生与死之间的鸿沟？然而生命可能是软弱的、病态的、不健康的、痛苦的、难熬的、焦虑的、疲惫的、沉重的、没有喜乐、没有笑容，一直到尽头。

「确据*比生命更多*。它是健康、力量、能力、活力、活泼、精力、刚强、美丽。」

===Original===
A minister once pronounced the benediction in this way: “The heart of God to make us welcome; the blood of Christ to make us clean, and the Holy Spirit to make us certain.” The security of the believer is the result of the operation of the Spirit of God.

Another writer says: “I have seen shrubs and trees grow out of the rocks, and overhang fearful precipices, roaring cataracts, and deep running waters; but they maintained their position, and threw out their foliage and branches as much as if they had been in the midst of a dense forest.” It was their hold on the rock that made them secure; and the influences of nature that sustained their life. So believers are oftentimes exposed to the most horrible dangers in their journey to heaven; but, so long as they are “rooted and grounded” in the Rock of Ages, they are perfectly secure. Their hold of Him is their guarantee; and the blessings of His grace give them life and sustain them in life. And as the tree must die, or the rock fall, before a dissolution can be effected between *them*, so either the believer must lose his spiritual life, or the Rock must crumble, ere their union can be dissolved.

Speaking of the Lord Jesus, Isaiah says: “I will fasten Him as a nail in a sure place; and He shall be for a glorious throne to His Father’s house: and they shall hang upon Him all the glory of His father’s house, the offspring and the issue, all vessels of small quantity, from the vessels of cups, even to all the vessels of flagons” (22:23﻿–﻿24).

===Chinese===
有一次，一位牧师这样祝福：「愿神的心肠使你们受欢迎；愿基督的宝血使你们洁净；愿圣灵使你们笃定。」信徒的稳妥，是神的灵运行的结果。

另一位作者说：「我见过灌木和树木从岩石里长出来，悬在可怖的悬崖、轰鸣的瀑布、奔流的深水之上；但它们稳住自己的位置，舒展枝叶，一如它们身在密林之中。」抓住磐石，使它们稳妥；而大自然的滋养，维持它们的生命。照样，信徒在去天国的路上常遭遇最可怕的危险；但只要他们「扎根」在万古磐石之上，便十分稳妥。抓住祂便是他们的保证；祂恩典的福分赐他们生命，又在生命中托住他们。正如树非死、磐石非塌，二者之间的结合便不能断裂；照样，除非信徒失去他属灵的生命，或磐石崩塌，他们与祂的联合便不能解散。

论到主耶稣，以赛亚说：「我必将他安放在坚固处；他必作为他父家的荣耀宝座：他父家所有的荣耀，连儿女带后裔，一切小器皿，从杯器到一切盛酒的器皿，都挂在他身上」（22:23–24）。

===Original===
There is **one nail**, fastened in a sure place; and on it hang all the flagons and all the cups. “Oh,” says one little cup, “I am so small and so black, suppose I were to drop!” “Oh,” says a flagon, “there is no fear of you; but I am so heavy, so very weighty, suppose I were to drop!” And a little cup says, “Oh, if I were only like the gold cup there, I should never fear falling.” But the gold cup answers, “It is not because I am a gold cup that I keep up; but because I hang upon the nail.” If the nail gives way we all come down, gold cups, china cups, pewter cups, and all; but as long as the nail keeps up, all that hang on Him hang safely.

I once read these words on a tombstone: “Born, died, kept.” Let us pray God to keep us in perfect peace, and assured of salvation.

===Chinese===
这里有**一根钉子**，钉在坚固处；其上挂着一切盛酒的器和一切杯。「哎呀，」一个小杯说，「我又小又黑，万一掉下去怎么办！」「哎呀，」一个酒瓶说，「你倒不用怕；我可重得很，沉得很，万一我掉下去怎么办！」一个小杯说，「哎呀，我要是像那只金杯就好了，我就永远不怕掉下去了。」金杯却回答说：「我能挂住，并非因为我是金杯；而是因为我挂在钉子上。」钉子若松，我们全都要掉下来——金杯、瓷杯、锡杯，无一例外；但只要钉子挂得住，凡挂在祂身上的，都挂得安稳。

我曾在一块墓碑上读到这几个字：「生，死，蒙保守。」愿我们求神保守我们十分平安，又确知自己已经得救。

## VIII — Christ All and in All / VIII ——「基督是一切，又住在一切之内」

> Colossians 3:11 / 歌罗西书 3:11

===Original===
Christ is *all* to us that we make Him to be. I want to emphasize that word “**all**.” Some men make Him to be “a root out of a dry ground,” “without form or comeliness.” He is nothing to them; they do not want Him. Some Christians have a very small Saviour, for they are not willing to receive Him fully, and let Him do great and mighty things for them. Others have a mighty Saviour, because they make Him to be great and mighty.

===Chinese===
基督对我们所是的，就是我们让祂所成为的*一切*。我要强调这一个词——「**一切**」。有人把祂看为「出于干地的根」、「无佳形美容」。在他们祂算不得什么；他们也不要祂。有些基督徒只有一位很小的救主，因为他们不愿完全地接受祂，不让祂为他们成就伟大奇妙的事。另一些人却有一位大能的救主，因为他们把祂当作伟大而大能的。

===Original===
If we would know what Christ wants to be to us, we must first of all know Him as our Saviour from sin. When the angel came down from heaven to proclaim that He was to be born into the world, you remember he gave His name, “He shall be called **Jesus**,2 for He shall save His people from their sins.” **Have we been delivered from sin**? He did not come to save us *in* our sins, but *from* our sins. Now, there are three ways of knowing a man. Some men you know only by hearsay; others you merely know by having been once introduced to them, you know them very slightly; others again you know by having been acquainted with them for years, you know them intimately. So I believe there are three classes of people today in the Christian Church and out of it: those who know Christ only by reading or by hearsay, those who have a historical Christ; those who have a slight personal acquaintance with Him; and, those who thirst, as Paul did, to “know Him and the power of His resurrection.” The more we know of Christ the more we shall love Him, and the better we shall serve Him.

Let us look at Him as He hangs upon the Cross, and see how He has put away sin. He was manifested that He might take away our sins; and if we really know Him we must first of all see Him as our Saviour from sin. You remember how the angels said to the shepherds on the plains of Bethlehem, “Behold, I bring you good tidings of great joy, which shall be to all people: for unto you is born this day, in the city of David, a Saviour, which is Christ the Lord.” (Luke 2:10–11). Then if you go clear back to Isaiah, seven hundred years before Christ’s birth, you will find these words: “I, even I, am the Lord; and beside me there is no Saviour” (43:11).

===Chinese===
我们若想知道基督愿做什么样的主给我们，就必须先认识祂是我们脱离罪的救主。当天使从天上降下来，宣告祂将要降生到世上的时候，你记得他给祂起的名：「祂要被称为**耶稣**，因祂要将自己的百姓从罪恶里救出来。」**我们已经脱离了罪吗**？祂来，不是要救我们*留在*罪中，而是要救我们*脱离*罪。要知道，认识一个人有三种途径。有的人你只是风闻其名；有的人你不过经人介绍见过一面，所知甚浅；还有的人你与之相交多年，便认识得透彻。照样，我相信今日无论在基督教会之内还是之外，都有三类人：一类只凭阅读或传闻认识基督，他们所有的只是一位历史上的基督；一类与祂只有浮浅的个人相识；还有一类，像保罗那样切慕「认识祂和祂复活的大能」。我们越认识基督，便越爱祂，也越能更好地事奉祂。

让我们仰望那悬挂在十字架上的祂，看祂如何除去了罪。祂显现，为要除掉我们的罪；我们若真认识祂，就必须首先看见祂是我们脱离罪的救主。你记得天使怎样对伯利恒之野的牧羊人说：「看哪，我报给你们大喜的信息，是关乎万民的：因今天在大卫的城里，为你们生了救主，就是主基督。」（路加福音 2:10–11）。再者，你若一直回溯到基督降生前七百年的以赛亚书，便会读到这样的话：「惟有我是耶和华；除我以外没有救主」（43:11）。

===Original===
Again, in the First Epistle of John (4:14) we read: “We have seen, and do testify, that the Father sent the Son to be the Saviour of the world.” All the heathen religions, we read, teach men to work their way up to God; but the religion of Jesus Christ is God coming down to men to save them, to lift them up out of the pit of sin. In Luke 19:10, we read that Christ Himself told the people what He had come for: “The Son of Man is come to seek and to save that which was lost.” So we start from the Cross, not from the cradle. Christ has opened up a new and living way to the Father; He has taken all the stumbling-blocks out of the way, so that every man who accepts of Christ as his Saviour can have salvation.

But Christ is not only a Saviour. I might save a man from drowning and rescue him from an untimely grave; but I might probably not be able to do any more for him. Christ is something more than a Saviour. When the children of Israel were placed behind the blood, that blood was their salvation; but they would still have heard the crack of the slave-driver’s whip if they had not been delivered from the Egyptian yoke of bondage: then it was that God delivered them from the hand of the king of Egypt. I have little sympathy with the idea that God comes down to save us, and then leaves us in prison, the slaves of our besetting sins. No; He has come to deliver us, and to give us victory over our evil tempers, our passions, and our lusts. Are you a professed Christian but one who is a slave to some besetting sin? If you want to get victory over that temper or that lust, go on to know Christ more intimately. He brings deliverance for the past, the present, and the future. “Who delivered; who doth deliver; who will yet deliver.” (2 Corinthians 1:10).

===Chinese===
再者，约翰一书（4:14）又记着：「父差子作世人的救主；这是我们所看见且作见证的。」我们所读到的一切异教，都教导人靠自己向上爬到神那里；而耶稣基督的信仰，却是神亲自降下来寻找人、拯救人，把他们从罪恶的坑中拉上来。路加福音 19:10 记着，基督亲自告诉众人祂来是为了什么：「人子来，为要寻找、拯救失丧的人。」所以我们是始于十字架，不是始于马槽。基督为我们开辟了一条又新又活的道路通向父；祂已把路上一切绊脚石都挪去，叫凡接受基督为救主的，都可以得着救恩。

但基督不仅仅是一位救主。我或许能救一个溺水的人，把他从夭折的坟里抢救出来；但我也许不能为他再做什么了。基督远不止于一位救主。当以色列人被安置在血的后面时，那血便是他们的拯救；可他们若未蒙拯救脱离埃及人为奴的轭，便仍要听见督工鞭子的响声：于是那时神把他们从埃及王的手中救了出来。神降下来救了我们，却把我们留在监牢里、作我们那容易缠累之罪的奴仆——我对此等说法不太以为然。不；祂来，是要救我们，要叫我们胜过自己的坏脾气、血气与私欲。你是一个口称基督徒、却作某样容易缠累之罪的奴仆的人吗？你若想胜过那脾气或那私欲，就要进一步更亲密地认识基督。祂带来的拯救，是为过去、为现在、也为将来的。「祂曾救；现在救；将来还要救。」（哥林多后书 1:10）。

===Original===
How often, like the children of Israel when they came to the Red Sea, have we become discouraged because everything looked dark before us, behind us, and around us, and we knew not which way to turn. Like Peter we have said, “To whom shall we go?” But God has appeared for our deliverance. He has brought us through the Red Sea right out into the wilderness, and opened up the way into the Promised Land. But Christ is not only our Deliverer; He is our Redeemer. That is something more than being our Saviour. He has brought us back. “Ye have sold yourselves for nought; and ye shall be redeemed without money.” (Isaiah 52:3). “We were not redeemed with corruptible things, as silver and gold.” (1 Peter 1:18). If gold could have redeemed us, could He not have created ten thousand worlds full of gold?

When God had redeemed the children of Israel from the bondage of Egypt, and brought them through the Red Sea, they struck out for the wilderness; and then God became to them their Way. I am so thankful the Lord has not left us in darkness as to the right way. There is no living man who has been groping in the darkness but may know the way. “I am the Way,” says Christ. If we follow Christ we shall be in the right way, and have the right doctrine. Who could lead the children of Israel through the wilderness like the Almighty God Himself? He knew the pitfalls and dangers of the way, and guided the people through all their wilderness journey right into the promised land. It is true that if it had not been for their accursed unbelief they might have crossed into the land at Kadesh Barnea, and taken possession of it, but they desired something besides God’s word; so they were turned back, and had to wander in the desert for forty years. I believe there are thousands of God’s children wandering in the wilderness still. The Lord has delivered them from the hand of the Egyptian, and would at once take them through the wilderness right into the Promised Land, if they were only willing to follow Christ. Christ has been down here, and has made the rough places smooth, and the dark places light, and the crooked places straight. If we will only be led by Him, and will follow Him, all will be peace, and joy, and rest.

===Chinese===
像以色列人到了红海那样，我们有多少次因前、后、四周尽都黑暗、不知何去何从而灰心丧胆。我们也像彼得那样说过：「我们还归从谁呢？」但神已显现，为我们施行拯救。祂带我们过了红海，一直出到旷野，又为我们开通了进入应许之地的道路。但基督不仅仅是我们的拯救者；祂也是我们的救赎主。这又胜过单作我们的救主。祂把我们带了回来。「你们是无价被卖的，也必无银被赎。」（以赛亚书 52:3）。「知道你们得赎……不是凭着能坏的金银等物。」（彼得前书 1:18）。若是金子能赎我们，祂岂不能造出一万个装满金子的世界吗？

当神把以色列人从埃及的奴役中赎出来、带他们过红海之后，他们便动身进入旷野；那时神就成为他们的道路。我何等感谢主，祂没有把我们留在黑暗里不知正路。凡是曾在黑暗中摸索的活人，没有不能知道这条路的。「我就是道路，」基督说。我们若跟随基督，便走在了正路上，也有了正道。除了全能的神自己，谁能这样领以色列人走过旷野呢？祂知道路上的陷阱与险阻，便引导百姓经过全程的旷野旅程，直入应许之地。诚然，若不是因他们那可咒诅的不信，他们原可以在加低斯·巴尼亚进入那地、得地为业；但他们要的是神的话语之外的东西；于是他们被折回，不得不在沙漠中飘流四十年。我相信，至今仍有成千上万神的儿女在旷野里飘流。主已经救他们脱离了埃及人的手，只要他们肯跟随基督，祂愿立时领他们穿过旷野、直入应许之地。基督曾下到这里来，把崎岖之处变为平坦，黑暗之处变为光明，弯曲之处变为正直。我们只要肯受祂引导、跟随祂，便一切都是平安、喜乐、安息。

===Original===
In the frontier, when a man goes out hunting he takes a hatchet with him, and cuts off pieces from the bark of the trees as he goes along through the forest: this is called “blazing the way.” He does it that he may know the way back, as there is no pathway through these thick forests. Christ has come down to this earth; He has “blazed the Way:” and now that He has gone up on high, if we will but follow him, we shall be kept in the right path. I will tell you how you may know if you are following Christ or not. If someone has slandered you, or misjudged you, do you treat them as your master would have done? If you do not bear these things in a loving and forgiving spirit, all the churches and ministers in the world cannot make you right. “If any man have not the Spirit of Christ, he is none of His.” (Romans 8:9). “If any man be in Christ Jesus he is a new creature: old things are passed away; behold, all things are become new.” (2 Corinthians 5:17).

===Chinese===
在边疆，一个人出去打猎时，随身带一把短斧，穿过森林时一路在树皮上砍下记号：这叫作「开辟路标」（blazing the way）。他这样作，是为了能认得回来的路，因为这些茂密的森林里本无路径可循。基督曾降在这地上；祂已经「开辟了那条路」；如今祂既已升上高天，我们只要跟随祂，便能行在正路之中。我来告诉你，你怎样可以知道自己是否在跟随基督。若有人毁谤你、错待你，你是否像你的主那样待他们？你若不用爱与赦免的灵来忍受这些事，普天下所有的教会和牧师都不能叫你走在正路上。「人若没有基督的灵，就不是属基督的。」（罗马书 8:9）。「若有人在基督里，他就是新造的人：旧事已过，都变成新的了。」（哥林多后书 5:17）。

===Original===
Christ is not only our way; He is the Light upon the way. He says, “I am the Light of the world.” (John 8:12; 9:5; 12:46). He goes on to say, “He that followeth Me shall not walk in darkness, but shall have the light of life.” It is impossible for any man or woman who is following Christ to walk in darkness. If your soul is in the darkness, groping around in the fog and mist of earth, let me tell you it is because you have got away from the true light. There is nothing but light that will dispel darkness. So let those who are walking in spiritual darkness admit Christ into their hearts: He is the Light. I call to mind a picture of which I used at one time to think a good deal; but now I have come to look more closely, I would not put it up in my house except I turned the face to the wall. It represents Christ as standing at a door, knocking, and having a big lantern in His hand. Why, you might as well hang up a lantern to the sun as put one into Christ’s hand. He is the Sun of Righteousness; and it is our privilege to walk in the light of an unclouded sun.

Many people are hunting after light, and peace, and joy. We are nowhere told to seek after these things. If we admit Christ into our hearts these will all come of themselves. I remember, when a boy, I used to try in vain to catch my shadow. One day I was walking with my face to the sun; and as I happened to look around I saw that my shadow was following me. The faster I went the faster my shadow followed; I could not get away from it. So when our faces are directed to the Sun of Righteousness, the peace and joy are sure to come. A man said to me some time ago, “Moody, how do you feel?” It was so long since I had thought about my feelings I had to stop and consider awhile, in order to find out. Some Christians are all the time thinking about their feelings; and because they do not feel just right they think their joy is all gone. If we keep our faces towards Christ, and are occupied with Him, we shall be lifted out of the darkness and the trouble that may have gathered round our path.

===Chinese===
基督不仅是我们的路；祂更是路上的光。祂说：「我是世界的光。」（约翰福音 8:12；9:5；12:46）。祂接着又说：「跟从我的，就不在黑暗里走，必要得着生命的光。」任何跟随基督的人，都不可能行在黑暗中。你的魂若处在黑暗里，在世上的雾与阴霾中摸索，容我告诉你，那是因为你离开了真光。除了光，没有什么能驱散黑暗。所以那些行在属灵黑暗中的人，当把基督迎进他们心里：祂就是光。我记得有一幅画，从前我对它颇为欣赏；可如今我看得更仔细了，除非把画面朝墙挂着，否则我不会把它挂在自己家里。那画把基督画成站在一扇门前叩门，手里还提着一盏大提灯。哎，你把一盏灯挂到太阳旁边，与把灯放到基督手里，同样都是多此一举。祂是公义的日头；行在毫无云翳的日头之光中，正是我们的特权。

许多人追寻光、平安与喜乐。圣经从没有一处叫我们去寻求这些东西。我们若把基督迎进心里，这一切自然都会随之而来。我记得小时候，常试着要抓住自己的影子，总是抓不着。有一天我面向太阳走着；偶然回头一看，竟见我的影子在后面跟着我。我走得越快，影子跟得越快；我甩不掉它。照样，当我们的脸朝向公义的日头时，平安与喜乐就必定临到。前些时有人对我说：「慕迪，你感觉如何？」我已经许久没有想到自己的感觉了，竟不得不停下来想了一会儿，才弄清楚。有些基督徒时刻都在想着自己的感觉；因为他们觉得不太对劲，便以为自己的喜乐都失去了。我们若把脸朝向基督，专注在祂身上，便能从那笼罩在路上的黑暗与烦扰中被提升出来。

===Original===
I remember being in a meeting after the war of the great rebellion broke out. The war had been going on for about six months. The army of the North had been defeated at Bull Run, in fact, we had nothing but defeat, and it looked as though the republic was going to pieces. So we were much cast down and discouraged. At this meeting every speaker for awhile seemed as if he had hung his harp upon the willow; and it was one of the gloomiest meetings I ever attended. Finally an old man with beautiful white hair got up to speak, and his face literally shone. “Young men,” he said, “you do not talk like sons of the King. Though it is dark just here, remember it is light somewhere else.” Then he went on to say that if it were dark all over the world, it was light up around the Throne.

He told us he had come from the east, where a friend had described to him how he had been up a mountain to spend the night and see the sun rise. As the party were climbing up the mountain, and before they had reached the summit, a storm came on. This friend said to the guide, “I will give this up; take me back.” The guide smiled, and replied, “I think we shall get above the storm soon.” On they went; and it was not long before they got up to where it was as calm as any summer evening. Down in the valley a terrible storm raged; they could hear the thunder rolling, and see the lightning’s flash; but all was serene on the mountain top. “And so, my young friends,” continued the old man, “though all is dark around you, come a little higher and the darkness will flee away.” Often when I have been inclined to get discouraged, I have thought of what he said. Now if you are down in the valley amidst the thick fog and the darkness, get a little higher; get nearer to Christ, and know more of Him.

===Chinese===
我记得大叛乱之战爆发后，我曾参加一次聚会。那时战争已进行了大约半年。北军在牛奔河一役吃了败仗，事实上，我们除了失败一无所有，看上去共和国就要分崩离析了。所以我们十分沮丧灰心。在那次聚会上，每一个讲员一时都像把琴挂在柳树上一般；那是我所参加过最阴沉的聚会之一。末了，一位满头美丽白发的老人站起来说话，他的脸简直在发光。「年轻人们，」他说，「你们说话不像君王的儿子。这里虽然黑暗，但别忘了别处却是光明的。」接着他又说，即便全世界都黑暗，宝座四周仍是光明的。

他告诉我们，他从东部来，那里有一位朋友曾向他描绘，自己怎样登上一座山过夜、看日出。一行人正爬山、还没到顶之时，忽起了暴风雨。那位朋友对向导说：「我放弃了；带我回去吧。」向导微微一笑，回答说：「我想我们很快就要到暴风雨的上头了。」他们继续前行；不久便到了一个地方，那里平静得像任何一个夏夜。下面的山谷里狂风暴雨肆虐；他们能听见雷声轰鸣、看见电光闪烁；可山顶上却一片宁静。「所以，年轻的朋友们，」老人接着说，「尽管你们四周一片黑暗，再上高一点，黑暗就会逃遁。」每逢我倾向灰心之时，常常想起他这番话。如今你若身在山谷之中、困于浓雾与黑暗，那就再上高一点；更靠近基督，更多地认识祂。

===Original===
You remember the Bible says, that when Christ expired on the cross, the light of the world was put out. God sent His Son to be the light of the world; but men did not love the light because it reproved them of their sins. When they were about to put out this light, what did Christ say to His disciples? “Ye shall be witnesses unto Me.” (Acts 1:8). He has gone up yonder to intercede for us; but He wants us to shine for Him down here. “Ye are the light of the world.” (Matthew 5:14). So our work is to shine; not to blow our own trumpet so that people may look at us. What we want to do is to show forth Christ. If we have any light at all it is borrowed light. Someone said to a young Christian: “Converted! it is all moonshine!” Said he: “I thank you for the illustration; the moon borrows its light from the sun; and we borrow ours from the Sun of Righteousness.” If we are Christ’s, we are here to shine for Him: by and by he will call us home to our reward.

I remember hearing of a blind man who sat by the wayside with a lantern near him. When he was asked what he had a lantern for, as he could not see the light, he said it was that people should not stumble over him. I believe more people stumble over the inconsistencies of professed Christians than from any other cause. What is doing more harm to the cause of Christ than all the scepticism in the world is this cold, dead formalism, this conformity to the world, this professing what we do not possess. The eyes of the world are upon us. I think it was George Fox who said every Quaker ought to light up the country for ten miles around him. If we were all brightly shining for the Master, those about us would soon be reached, and there would be a shout of praise going to heaven.

===Chinese===
你记得圣经上说，基督在十字架上断气之时，世界的光就被熄灭了。神差祂的儿子来作世上的光；人却不爱这光，因为它定他们的罪。当他们要熄灭这光之时，基督对门徒说了什么？「你们……要作我的见证。」（使徒行传 1:8）。祂已升到上面去为我们代求；但祂要我们在这里为祂发光。「你们是世上的光。」（马太福音 5:14）。所以我们的工作就是发光；不是吹响自己的号角，好叫人看我们。我们当作的，是把基督彰显出来。我们若有任何一点光，那都是借来的光。有人对一个年轻的基督徒说：「归主了！那全是瞎扯（moonshine）！」他说：「我谢谢你这个比方；月亮是从太阳借光的；我们则是从公义的日头借光。」我们若是属基督的，我们就是在这里为祂发光：不久祂便要召我们回家得赏赐。

我记得听说过一个盲人，坐在路旁，身边放着一盏提灯。人问他既然看不见光，为何还要一盏灯，他说，那是叫人不要被他绊倒。我相信，因着口称基督徒前后不一的行为而绊倒的人，比因任何其他原因绊倒的都多。对基督的事业造成比世上一切怀疑更大损害的，正是这种又冷、又死的形式主义，这种与世俗同流，这种口里承认自己其实没有的东西。世人的眼睛正看着我们。我想是乔治·福克斯说过，每一个贵格会信徒都当照亮他周围十里的乡野。我们若都为主明光照耀，周围的人不久便能被触及，便会有欢呼赞美的声音升上天堂。

===Original===
People say: “I want to know what is the truth.” Listen: “**I am the truth**,” says Christ. (John 14:5). If you want to know what the truth is, get acquainted with Christ. People also complain that they have not life. Many are trying to give themselves spiritual life. You may galvanize yourselves and put electricity into yourselves, so to speak; but the effect will not last very long. Christ alone is the author of life. If you would have real spiritual life, get to know Christ. Many try to stir up spiritual life by going to meetings. That may be well enough; but it will be of no use, unless they get into contact with the living Christ. Then their spiritual life will not be a spasmodic thing, but will be perpetual; flowing on and on, and bringing forth fruit to God.

===Chinese===
人说：「我想知道真理是什么。」请听：「**我就是真理**，」基督说。（约翰福音 14:5）。你若想知道真理是什么，就去认识基督。也有人抱怨自己没有生命。许多人试图给自己制造属灵的生命。你可以说给自己通电、给自己打强心针；可那效果不会持久。唯有基督是生命的赐予者。你若想要真正的属灵生命，就要去认识基督。许多人试图借着参加聚会来激发属灵的生命。那固然不错；却毫无用处，除非他们与这位又活着的基督接触上。这样，他们的属灵生命便不会是断断续续的，而是恒久不息的；源源不断地涌流，并且结出果子归给神。

===Original===
Then Christ is our **Keeper**. A great many young disciples are afraid they will not hold out. “He that keepeth Israel shall neither slumber nor sleep.” (Psalm 121:4). It is the work of Christ to keep us; and if He keeps us there will be no danger of our falling. I suppose if Queen Victoria had to take care of the Crown of England, some thief might attempt to get access to it; but it is put away in the Tower of London, and guarded night and day by soldiers.
