The whole English army would, if necessary, be called out to protect it. And we have no strength in ourselves. We are no match for Satan; he has had six thousand years’ experience. But then we remember that the One who neither slumbers nor sleeps is our keeper. In Isaiah 41:10, we read, “Fear thou not, for I am with thee; be not dismayed, for I am thy God; I will strengthen thee; yea, I will help thee; yea, I will uphold thee with the right hand of My righteousness.” In Jude also, verse 24, we are told that He is “able to keep us from falling.” “We have an Advocate with the Father, Jesus Christ the righteous.” (1 John 2:1).

===Chinese===
再者，基督是我们的**保守者**。许多年轻的门徒害怕自己不能坚持到底。「保护以色列的，也不打盹也不睡觉。」（诗篇 121:4）。保守我们，正是基督的工作；祂既保守我们，我们便没有跌倒的危险。我想，若是维多利亚女王得亲自看管英国的王冠，也许就会有贼想设法弄到手；可那王冠被收在伦敦塔里，日夜有士兵把守。必要时，全英国的军队都会被调出来护卫它。而我们在自己里面毫无力量。我们不是撒但的对手；它已有六千年的经验。可那时我们便记得，那位不打盹也不睡觉的，正是我们的保守者。以赛亚书 41:10 记着：「你不要害怕，因为我与你同在；不要惊惶，因为我是你的神。我必坚固你，我必帮助你，我必用我公义的右手扶持你。」犹大书第 24 节也告诉我们，祂「能保守你们不失脚」。「在父那里我们有一位中保，就是那义者耶稣基督。」（约翰一书 2:1）。

===Original===
But Christ is something more. He is our **Shepherd**. It is the work of the shepherd to care for the sheep, to feed them and protect them. “I am the Good Shepherd;” “My sheep hear My voice.” “I lay down My life for the sheep.” In that wonderful tenth chapter of John, Christ uses the personal pronoun no less than twenty-eight times, in declaring what He is and what He will do. In verse 28 He says, “They shall never perish; neither shall any [*man*] pluck them out of My hand.” But notice the word “man” is in italics. See how the verse really reads: “Neither shall **any** pluck them out of My hand”—no devil or man shall be able to do it. In another place the Scripture declares, “Your life is hid with Christ in God.” (Colossians 3:3). How safe and how secure!

===Chinese===
但基督不仅如此。祂是我们的**牧者**。牧者的工作，就是眷顾羊群、喂养它们、保护它们。「我是好牧人；」「我的羊听我的声音。」「我为羊舍命。」在约翰福音那奇妙的第十章里，基督为要宣告祂是什么、祂要作什么，不下二十八次用了第一人称代词。第 28 节祂说：「他们永不灭亡，谁也不能从我手里把他们夺去。」但请注意中文里的「谁」（英文作 any *man*）这个「人」字在英文钦定本里是斜体（即原文无对应字、系译者补加）。请看这一节本当怎么读：「谁也不能……把我手里的他们夺去」——任何人都不能，任何鬼魔也不能。圣经在另一处又宣告：「你们的生命与基督一同藏在神里面。」（歌罗西书 3:3）。何等安稳，何等牢靠！

===Original===
Christ says, “My sheep hear My voice… and they follow Me.” (John 10:27). A gentleman in the East heard of a shepherd who could call all his sheep to him by name. He went and asked if this was true. The shepherd took him to the pasture where they were, and called one of them by some name. One sheep looked up and answered the call, while the others went on feeding and paid no attention. In the same way he called about a dozen of the sheep around him. The stranger said, “How do you know one from the other? They all look perfectly alike.” “Well,” said he, “you see that sheep toes in a little; that other one has a squint; one has a little piece of wool off; another has a black spot; and another has a piece out of its ear.” The man knew all his sheep by their failings, for he had not a perfect one in the whole flock. I suppose our Shepherd knows us in the same way.

An Eastern shepherd was once telling a gentleman that his sheep knew his voice, and that no stranger could deceive them. The gentleman thought he would like to put the statement to the test. So he put on the shepherd’s frock and turban, and took his staff and went to the flock. He disguised his voice, and tried to speak as much like the shepherd as he could; but he could not get a single sheep in the flock to follow him. He asked the shepherd if his sheep never followed a stranger. He was obliged to admit that if a sheep got sickly it would follow anyone. So it is with a good many professed Christians; when they get sickly and weak in the faith, they will follow any teacher that comes along; but when the soul is in health, a man will not be carried away by errors and heresies. He will know whether the “voice” speaks the truth or not. He can soon tell that, if he is really in communion with God. When God sends a true messenger his words will find a ready response in the Christian heart.

===Chinese===
基督说：「我的羊听我的声音……它们也跟着我。」（约翰福音 10:27）。东方有一位绅士听说有个牧人，能把所有的羊一只只叫到跟前。他便去问这是否属实。那牧人带他到羊群所在的草场，叫了其中一只的名字。一只羊抬起头来回应了，其余的却只管吃草，毫不理会。他又照样叫了大约十二只到自己身边。那陌生人说：「你怎么能一只一只分辨出来？它们看上去全都一模一样。」「哦，」他说，「你看，那只羊走路脚有点朝里；那一只有点斜眼；这一只少了一小块羊毛；另一只有一个黑斑；还有一只耳朵缺了一块。」这人凭羊的缺点认得自己所有的羊，因为他整群羊里没有一只完美的。我想我们的牧者也是这样认识我们。

有一位东方的牧人曾对一位绅士说，他的羊认得他的声音，任何陌生人都骗不了它们。那绅士想试一试这话。于是他穿上牧人的外袍、裹上头巾，拿起他的杖，走到羊群跟前。他装作嗓音，尽量学得像那牧人；可他无法叫羊群中有任何一只跟随他。他问那牧人，他的羊是否从不跟随陌生人。他不得不承认，一只羊若生了病，便会跟任何人走。许多口称基督徒的人也是如此；当他们灵命虚弱、信心软弱之时，便什么老师来了都跟随；可当灵魂健壮之时，便不会被错谬与异端冲走。他能辨别那「声音」说的是不是真理。他若真与神相交，便能很快辨别出来。当神差遣一位真正的使者，他的话必在基督徒心里得着迅速的响应。

===Original===
Christ is a tender Shepherd. You may some time think He has not been a very tender Shepherd to you; you are passing under the rod. It is written, “Whom the Lord loveth He chasteneth, and scourgeth every son whom He receiveth.” (Hebrews 12:6). That you are passing under the rod is no proof that Christ does not love you. A friend of mine lost all his children. No man could ever have loved his family more; but the scarlet fever took one by one away; and so the whole four or five, one after another, died. The poor stricken parents went over to great Britain, and wandered from one place to another, there and on the continent. At length they found their way to Syria. One day they saw an Eastern shepherd come down to a stream, and call his flock to cross. The sheep came down to the brink, and looked at the water; but they seemed to shrink from it, and he could not get them to respond to his call. He then took a little lamb, put it under one arm; he took another lamb and put it under the other arm, and thus passed into the stream. The old sheep no longer stood looking at the water: they plunged in after the shepherd; and in a few minutes the whole flock was on the other side; and he led them away to newer and fresher pastures. The bereaved father and mother, as they looked on the scene, felt that it taught them a lesson. They no longer murmured because the Great Shepherd had taken their lambs one by one into yonder world; and they began to look up and look forward to the time when they would follow the loved ones they had lost. If you have loved ones gone before, remember that your Shepherd is calling you to “set your affection on things above.” (Colossians 3:2). Let us be faithful to Him, and follow Him, while we remain in this world. And if you have not taken Him for your Shepherd, do so this very day.

===Chinese===
基督是一位慈爱的牧者。你有时或会想，祂对你并不太慈爱；你正经过杖下。圣经上记着：「主所爱的，他必管教，又鞭打凡所收纳的儿子。」（希伯来书 12:6）。你经过杖下，并不能证明基督不爱你。我有一位朋友，失去了他所有的孩子。没有人比他更爱自己的家；可是猩红热把那些孩子一个接一个地夺走了；于是那四五个孩子，一个跟着一个，都死了。这一对可怜、心碎的父母远赴大不列颠，在那里和欧洲大陆上，从一处漂泊到另一处。末了他们到了叙利亚。一天，他们看见一位东方的牧人下到溪边，呼唤他的羊群过河。羊下到岸边，看着水；却似要退缩，他唤不动它们回应。于是他抱起一只小羊，夹在一臂之下；又抱起另一只小羊，夹在另一臂之下，就这样过了溪。老羊不再站着看水：它们跟着牧人跳了进去；不消几分钟，整群羊都到了对岸；他便领它们到又新又青翠的牧场去。那一对丧失了孩子的父母，看着这一幕，觉得其中有一个功课。他们不再因大牧者把他们的小羊一只一只接到那边的世界里而发怨言；他们开始仰望，盼着那日他们要跟从所失去的亲人而去。你若有亲人已经先去了，请记着，你的牧者正呼召你「要思念上面的事」（歌罗西书 3:2）。让我们在这世上存留之日，对祂忠心，跟随祂。你若还没有以祂为你的牧者，就在今天就这样作吧。

===Original===
Christ is not only all these things that I have mentioned: He is also our Mediator, our Sanctifier, our Justifier; in fact, it would take volumes to tell what He desires to be to every individual soul. While looking through some papers I once read this wonderful description of Christ. I do not know where it originally came from; but it was so fresh to my soul that I should like to give it to you:—

===Chinese===
基督不仅是我在上文提到的那一切：祂也是我们的中保、使我们成圣者、称我们为义者；其实，要把祂愿作每一个灵魂的什么，一一述说出来，非得写成几卷书不可。有一次我在翻阅一些文稿时，读到了下面这一段对基督奇妙的描述。我不知道它最初出自何处；但它对我的灵魂如此清新，我愿意把它送给你：——

===Original===
“Christ is our Way; we walk in Him. He is our Truth; we embrace Him. He is our Life; we live in Him. He is our Lord; we choose Him to rule over us. He is our Master; we serve Him. He is our Teacher, instructing us in the way of salvation. He is our Prophet, pointing out the future. He is our Priest, having atoned for us. He is our Advocate, ever living to make intercession for us. He is our Saviour, saving to the uttermost. He is our Root; we grow from Him. He is our Bread; we feed upon Him. He is our Shepherd, leading us into green pastures. He is our true Vine; we abide in Him. He is the Water of Life; we slake our thirst from Him. He is the fairest among ten thousand: we admire Him above all others. He is ‘the brightness of the Father’s glory, and the express image of His person;’ we strive to reflect His likeness. He is the upholder of all things; we rest upon Him. He is our wisdom; we are guided by Him. He is our Righteousness; we cast all our imperfections upon Him. He is our Sanctification; we draw all our power for holy life from Him. He is our Redemption, redeeming us from all iniquity. He is our Healer, curing all our diseases. He is our Friend, relieving us in all our necessities. He is our Brother, cheering us in our difficulties.”

===Chinese===
「基督是我们的道路；我们行在祂里面。祂是我们的真理；我们拥抱祂。祂是我们的生命；我们活在祂里面。祂是我们的主；我们拣选祂来治理我们。祂是我们的夫子；我们事奉祂。祂是我们的教师，把救恩之路指教我们。祂是我们的先知，指明未来。祂是我们的祭司，已经为我们赎了罪。祂是我们的中保，永远活着为我们代求。祂是我们的救主，拯救到底。祂是我们的根；我们从祂生长。祂是我们的粮；我们以祂为食。祂是我们的牧者，领我们到青草地。祂是真葡萄树；我们住在祂里面。祂是生命的活水；我们解渴于祂。祂是万人中最荣美的；我们爱慕祂胜过一切。祂是『神荣耀所发的光辉，是神本体的真像』；我们竭力反照祂的形像。祂是托住万有的；我们安息于祂。祂是我们的智慧；我们受祂引导。祂是我们的义；我们将自己一切的不完全都卸在祂身上。祂是我们的成圣；我们一切圣洁生活的能力都从祂而得。祂是我们的救赎，救我们脱离一切不法的事。祂是我们的医治者，医治我们一切的疾病。祂是我们的朋友，在我们一切需用上解我们的困。祂是我们的兄长，在我们一切艰难中鼓舞我们。」

===Original===
Here is another beautiful extract: it is from Gotthold:

“For my part, my soul is like a hungry and thirsty child; and I need His love and consolation for my refreshment. I am a wandering and lost sheep; and I need Him as a good and faithful shepherd. My soul is like a frightened dove pursued by the hawk; and I need His wounds for a refuge. I am a feeble vine; and I need His cross to lay hold of, and to wind myself about. I am a sinner; and I need His righteousness. I am naked and bare; and I need His holiness and innocence for a covering. I am ignorant; and I need His teaching: simple and foolish; and I need the guidance of His Holy Spirit. In no situation, and at no time, can I do without Him. Do I pray? He must prompt, and intercede for me. Am I arraigned by Satan at the Divine tribunal? He must be my Advocate. Am I in affliction? He must be my Helper. Am I persecuted by the world? He must defend me. When I am forsaken, He must be my Support; when I am dying, my life: when mouldering in the grave, my Resurrection. Well, then, I will rather part with all the world, and all that it contains, than with Thee, my Saviour. And, God be thanked! I know that Thou, too, art neither able nor willing to do without me. Thou art rich; and I am poor. Thou hast abundance; and I am needy. Thou hast righteousness; and I sins. Thou hast wine and oil; and I wounds. Thou hast cordials and refreshments; and I hunger and thirst.

===Chinese===
这里还有另一段佳美的摘录，出自戈特霍尔德（Gotthold）：

「就我而言，我的灵魂像一个又饥又渴的孩子；我需要祂的爱与安慰来使我苏醒。我是一只走迷了、失丧的羊；我需要祂作一位又良善又忠心的牧者。我的灵魂像一只被苍鹰追赶的惊鸽；我需要祂的伤痕作我的避难所。我是一棵软弱的藤蔓；我需要祂的十字架来抓住、来缠绕。我是一个罪人；我需要祂的义。我赤身露体；我需要祂的圣洁与无辜作我的遮盖。我无知；我需要祂的教导：我单纯而愚拙；我需要祂圣灵的引导。无论在何种境地、无论在何时，我都不能没有祂。我祷告吗？必须是祂在我里面发动、为我代求。撒但在神的法庭上控告我吗？祂必须作我的中保。我遭遇患难吗？祂必须作我的帮助。我受世界逼迫吗？祂必须为我辩护。我被撇弃之时，祂必须作我的扶持；我临终之时，作我的生命：我在坟墓中朽坏之时，作我的复活。那么，我宁愿舍弃全世界和其中所有的一切，也不愿舍弃你，我的救主。感谢神！我知道你也不能、也不愿没有我。你是富足的；我是贫穷的。你丰盛；我缺乏。你有义；我有罪。你有酒和油；我有伤口。你有强心剂与滋养；我有饥有渴。

===Original===
“Use me then, my Saviour, for whatever purpose, and in whatever way, Thou mayest require. Here is my poor heart, an empty vessel; fill it with Thy grace. Here is my sinful and troubled soul; quicken and refresh it with Thy love. Take my heart for Thine abode; my mouth to spread the glory of Thy name; my love and all my powers, for the advancement of Thy believing people; and never suffer the steadfastness and confidence of my faith to abate—that so at all times I may be enabled from the heart to say. ‘Jesus needs me, and I Him; and so we suit each other.’ ”

===Chinese===
「那么，我的救主啊，无论为何目的、用何方式，只要你所需用，就请使用我。这是我贫穷的心，一只空器皿；求用你的恩典充满它。这是我犯罪而烦扰的灵魂；求用你的爱使它苏醒、使它畅快。求收下我的心作你的居所；收下我的口来传扬你名的荣耀；收下我的爱和我一切的力量，用以造就你信实的百姓；并永不容我信心的坚定与笃信衰减——好叫我随时都能从心底里说：『耶稣需要我，我也需要祂；我们如此彼此相配。』」

## IX — Backsliding / IX ——「退后」

> 「我必医治他们的退后，甘心爱他们，因为我的怒气向他们转消。」
> ——何西阿书 14:4

===Original===
There are two kinds of backsliders. Some have never been converted: they have gone through the form of joining a Christian community and claim to be backsliders; but they never have, if I may use the expression, “slid forward.” They may talk of backsliding; but they have never really been born again. They need to be treated differently from real backsliders—those who have been born of the incorruptible seed, but who have turned aside. We want to bring the latter back the same road by which they left their first love.

Turn to Psalm 85:5. There you read: “Wilt Thou be angry with us forever? wilt Thou draw out Thine anger to all generations? wilt Thou not revive us again: that Thy people may rejoice in Thee? Show us Thy mercy, O Lord; and grant us Thy salvation.” Now look again: “*I will hear what God the Lord will speak:* for He will speak peace unto His people, and to His saints; but let them not turn again to folly” (verse 8).

===Chinese===
退后之人有两种。一种是从未归主的：他们走完了加入基督徒群体的形式，自称是退后之人；可是他们若容我用这个说法——从来就未曾「向前滑」过。他们可以谈论退后；但他们从未真正重生。他们所需的对待，与真正的退后之人不同——后者是藉着不能朽坏的种子而生，却偏离了正路。我们要把后者带回来，循着他们当初离开起初爱心时所走的那条路。

请翻到诗篇 85:5。那里记着：「你要永远向我们发怒吗？你的怒气要延及万代吗？你不再将我们救活，使你的百姓靠你欢喜吗？耶和华啊，求你向我们显出你的慈爱，将你的救恩赐给我们。」再看下面：「*我要听神耶和华所说的话*，因为他必应许将平安赐给他的百姓、他的圣民；他们却不可再转去受愚妄」（8 节）。

===Original===
There is nothing that will do backsliders so much good as to come in contact with the Word of God; and for them the Old Testament is as full of help as the New. The book of Jeremiah has some wonderful passages for wanderers. What we want to do is to get backsliders to hear what God the Lord will say.

Look for a moment at Jeremiah 6:10. “To whom shall I speak, and give warning, that they may hear? behold, their ear is uncircumcised, and they cannot hearken: behold, the word of the Lord is unto them a reproach; they have no delight in it.” That is the condition of backsliders. They have no delight whatever in the word of God. But we want to bring them back, and let God get their ear. Read from the 14th verse: “They have healed also the hurt of the daughter of My people slightly, saying, Peace, peace; when there is no peace. Were they ashamed when they had committed abomination? nay, they were not at all ashamed, neither could they blush: therefore they shall fall among them that fall: at the time that I visit them they shall be cast down, saith the Lord. Thus saith the Lord, Stand ye in the ways, and see, and ask for the old paths, where is the good way, and walk therein; and ye shall find rest for your souls. But they said, We will not walk therein. Also I set watchmen over you, saying, Hearken to the sound of the trumpet. But they said, We will not hearken.”

===Chinese===
没有什么比接触神的话语更能叫退后之人得益处的了；对退后之人而言，旧约与新约一样充满帮助。耶利米书里有一些为流荡者写的奇妙经文。我们所要做的，就是叫退后之人听见神耶和华所要说的话。

请稍看耶利米书 6:10。「我应当对谁说话，应当警告谁呢？看哪，他们的耳朵未受割礼，不能听见；看哪，耶和华的话他们以为是羞辱，不以为喜悦。」这正是退后之人的光景。他们对神的话语毫无喜悦可言。但我们要把他们带回来，让神得着他们的耳朵。从第 14 节读起：「他们轻轻忽忽地医治我百姓的损伤，说：平安了！平安了！其实没有平安。他们行可憎的事觉得惭愧吗？不然，他们毫不惭愧，也不知羞耻。因此，他们必在仆倒的人中仆倒；我向他们察看的时候，他们必跌倒。这是耶和华说的。耶和华如此说：你们当站在路上察看，访问古道，哪是善道，便行在其间；这样，你们心里必得安息。他们却说：我们不行在其间。我设立守望的人警戒他们，说：要听角声。他们却说：我们不听。」

===Original===
That was the condition of the Jews when they had backslidden. They had turned away from the old paths. And that is the condition of backsliders. They have got away from the good old book. Adam and Eve fell by not hearkening to the word of God. They did not believe God’s word; but they believed the tempter. That is the way backsliders fall—by turning away from the word of God.

In Jeremiah 2 we find God pleading with them as a father would plead with a son. “Thus saith the Lord, What iniquity have your fathers found in Me, that they are gone from Me, and have walked after vanity, and are become vain?… Wherefore I will yet plead with you, saith the Lord; and with your children’s children will I plead… For my people have committed two evils: they have forsaken Me, the Fountain of living waters, and hewed them out cisterns, broken cisterns, that can hold no water.”

===Chinese===
犹太人退后之时，正是这光景。他们偏离了古道。退后之人的光景也是如此。他们远离了那本宝贵的古老书卷。亚当和夏娃之所以堕落，是因为不听神的话语。他们不信神的话；反倒信了那诱惑人的。退后之人堕落的方式正是如此——因转离神的话语。

在耶利米书第 2 章，我们看到神像父亲恳求儿子那样恳求他们。「耶和华如此说：你们列祖见我有什么不义，竟远离我，随从虚无的神，自己成为虚妄的呢？……耶和华说：我因此必与你们争辩，也必与你们的子孙争辩……因为我的百姓做了两件恶事，就是离弃我这活水的泉源，为自己凿出池子，是破裂不能存水的池子。」

===Original===
Now there is one thing to which we wish to call the attention of backsliders; and that is, that the Lord never forsook them; but that they forsook Him! The Lord never left them; but they left Him! And this, too, without any cause! He says, “What iniquity have your fathers found in Me, that they are gone far from Me?” Is not God the same today as when you came to Him first? Has God changed? Men are apt to think that God has changed; but the fault is with them. Backslider, I would ask you, “What iniquity is there in God, that you have left Him and gone far from Him?” You have, He says, hewed out to yourselves broken cisterns that hold no water. The world cannot satisfy the new nature. No earthly well can satisfy the soul that has become a partaker of the heavenly nature. Honor, wealth and the pleasures of this world will not satisfy those who, having tasted the water of life, have gone astray, seeking refreshment at the world’s fountains. Earthly wells will get dry. They cannot quench spiritual thirst.

Again in the 32nd verse: “Can a maid forget her ornaments, or a bride her attire? yet My people have forgotten Me, days without number.” That is the charge which God brings against the backslider. They “have forgotten Me, days without number.”

===Chinese===
如今有一件事，我们要请退后之人留意：主从未离弃他们；是他们离弃了祂！主从未离开他们；是他们离开了祂！而且，这是毫无缘由的！祂说：「你们列祖见我有什么不义，竟远离我呢？」神今日岂不与你初次到祂面前时一样吗？神改变了吗？人总以为神改变了；过错却在于他们自己。退后之人啊，我要问你：「神有什么不义，使你离开祂、远远地远离祂？」祂说，你们为自己凿出了破裂不能存水的池子。这世界不能满足那新性情。没有任何属地的井，能满足那有分于属天性情的灵魂。尊荣、财富和这世界的种种享乐，都不能满足那些已经尝过生命之水、却走迷了路、到世界的泉源那里寻求畅快的人。属地的井终必干涸。它们解不了属灵的渴。

再看第 32 节：「处女岂能忘记她的妆饰呢？新妇岂能忘记她的美衣呢？我的百姓却忘记了我无数的日子！」这正是神对退后之人所提出的控告。他们「忘记了我无数的日子」。

===Original===
I have often startled young ladies when I have said to them, “My friend, you think more of your earrings than of the Lord.” The reply has been, “No, I do not.” But when I have asked, “Would you not be troubled if you lost one; and would you not set about seeking for it?” the answer has been, “Well, yes, I think I should.” But though they had turned from the Lord, it did not give them any trouble; nor did they seek after Him that they might find Him.

How many once in fellowship and in daily communion with the Lord now think more of their dresses and ornaments than of their precious souls! Love does not like to be forgotten. Mothers would have broken hearts if their children left them and never wrote a word or sent any memento of their affection; and God pleads over backsliders as a parent over loved ones who have gone astray. He tries to woo them back. He asks: “What have I done that you should have forsaken Me?”

===Chinese===
我常对年轻的女子说：「朋友，你把你的耳环看得比主还重。」这话常叫她们一惊。她们回答说：「不，我没有。」可等我再问：「你若丢了一只，岂不是会着急吗？岂不是会马上设法去找吗？」她们的回答是：「嗯，是的，我想我会的。」然而她们虽然离开了主，心里却一点不觉得为难；她们也不去寻找祂，好寻见祂。

曾有多少人一度与主相交、日日与主亲密，如今却把自己的衣裳饰物看得比自己宝贵的灵魂还重！爱不愿被人遗忘。作母亲的，若儿女离开她们，从不写一句话、也不捎一点记念爱心的物件，她们的心都要碎了；神为退后之人恳切，正如父母为走迷的儿女恳切。祂设法把他们吸引回来。祂问：「我做了什么，使你竟离弃我？」

===Original===
The most tender and loving words to be found in the whole of the Bible are from Jehovah to those who have left Him without a cause. Jeremiah 2:19.

Hear how He argues with such: (Jeremiah 2:19). “Thine own wickedness shall correct thee, and thy backslidings shall reprove thee; know, therefore, and see, that it is an evil thing and bitter, that thou hast forsaken the Lord thy God, and that My fear is not in thee, saith the Lord God of hosts.”

===Chinese===
整本圣经里所能找到的最温柔、最充满爱意的话，正是耶和华对那些无故离开祂之人所说的。耶利米书 2:19。

请听祂怎样与这样的人理论：（耶利米书 2:19）。「你自己的恶必惩治你；你退后的事必责备你。由此可知可见，你离弃耶和华你的神，不存敬畏我的心，乃为恶事，为苦事。这是万军之主耶和华说的。」

===Original===
I do not exaggerate when I say that I have seen hundreds of backsliders come back; and I have asked them if they have not found it an evil and a bitter thing to leave the Lord. You cannot find a real backslider, who has known the Lord, but will admit that it is an evil and a bitter thing to turn away from Him; and I do not know of any one verse more used to bring back wanderers than that very one. May it bring you back if you have wandered into the far country.

Look at Lot. Did not he find it an evil and a bitter thing? He was twenty years in Sodom, and never made a convert. He got on well in the sight of the world. Men would have told you that he was one of the most influential and worthy men in all Sodom. But alas! alas! he ruined his family. And it is a pitiful sight to see that old backslider going through the streets of Sodom at midnight, after he has warned his children, and they have turned a deaf ear.

===Chinese===
我毫不夸张地说，我曾见过成百的退后之人归回；我问他们，离开主是不是一件又恶又苦的事。你找不到一个真正认识主的退后之人，不承认远离祂是又恶又苦的事；据我所知，再没有哪一节经文比这一节更常被用来领流荡者归回。你若已流荡到了远方，愿这节经文把你领回来。

你看罗得。他岂不觉得这是一件又恶又苦的事吗？他在所多玛住了二十年，从未领过一个人归主。他在世人眼前混得不错。人们会告诉你，他是全所多玛最有势力、最体面的人之一。可是哀哉！哀哉！他毁了自己的家。想想那位年迈的退后之人，午夜走在所多玛的街上——他刚刚警告过他的孩子们，他们却充耳不闻——那是一幅多么可悲的景象。

===Original===
I have never known a man and his wife backslide, without its proving utter ruin to their children. They will make a mockery of religion and will deride their parents: “Thine own wickedness shall correct thee; and thy backsliding shall reprove thee!” Did not David find it so? Mark him, crying, “O my son Absalom, my son, my son Absalom! would God I had died for thee; O Absalom, my son, my son!” I think it was the ruin, rather than the death of his son that caused this anguish.

I remember being engaged in conversation some years ago, till past midnight, with an old man. He had been for years wandering on the barren mountains of sin. That night he wanted to get back. We prayed, and prayed, and prayed, till light broke in upon him; and he went away rejoicing. The next night he sat in front of me when I was preaching, and I think that I never saw anyone look so sad and wretched in all my life. He followed me into the enquiry-room. “What is the trouble?” I asked. “Is your eye off the Saviour? Have your doubts come back?” “No; it is not that,” he said. “I did not go to business, but spent all this day in visiting my children. They are all married and in this city. I went from house to house, but there was not one but mocked me. It is the darkest day of my life. I have awoke up to what I have done. I have taken my children into the world; and now I cannot get them out.” The Lord had restored unto him the joy of His salvation; yet there was the bitter consequence of his transgression. You can run through your experience; and you can find just such instances repeated again and again. Many who came to your city years ago serving God, in their prosperity have forgotten Him: and where are their sons and daughters? Show me the father and mother who have deserted the Lord and gone back to the beggarly elements of the world; and I am mistaken if their children are not on the high road to ruin.

===Chinese===
我从未见过一对夫妇退后，而不给他们的儿女带来彻底的毁灭。他们必把信仰当作笑柄，并讥诮父母：「你自己的恶必惩治你；你退后的事必责备你！」大卫岂不也经历了这事吗？请听他喊着说：「我儿押沙龙啊！我儿，我儿押沙龙啊！我恨不得替你死，押沙龙啊，我儿，我儿！」我想，使他如此痛苦的，是儿子的毁灭，而非儿子的死。

我记得几年前，曾与一位老人交谈，直到过了半夜。他多年流荡在罪的无草之山上。那夜他想回来。我们祷告、祷告、再祷告，直到光射进他心里；他便欢欢喜喜地走了。第二天夜里，我讲道时他坐在我面前，我想我一生从未见过谁面容那么悲伤、那么凄惨。他随我进了询道室。「难处在哪里？」我问。「你的眼睛离开救主了吗？疑惑又回来了吗？」「不；不是这个，」他说。「我今天没有去做生意，却花了一整天去探望我的儿女。他们都成了家，住在这城里。我挨家挨户地走，却没有一家不讥诮我。这是我一生中最黑暗的一天。我如今醒悟到我所做的了。我把儿女带进了世界；如今我却无法把他们领出来。」主已将祂救恩之乐恢复给了他；然而他犯罪的苦果仍摆在那里。你可以追溯自己的经历；你能发现这样的实例一遍又一遍地重演。许多年前来到你们城里事奉神的人，在发达之时便忘记了祂：他们的儿女如今在哪里呢？你指给我看一对撇弃了主、回到世界中那懦弱无用的小学之父的父母；若他们的儿女不是正走在大步迈向毁灭的路上，便是我的判断错了。

===Original===
As we desire to be faithful we warn these backsliders. It is a sign of love to warn of danger. We may be looked upon as enemies for a while; but the truest friends are those who lift up the voice of warning. Israel had no truer friend than Moses. In Jeremiah God gave His people a weeping prophet to bring them back to Him; but they cast off God. They forgot the God who brought them out of Egypt, and who led them through the desert into the promised land. In their prosperity they forget Him and turned away. The Lord had told them what would happen. (Deuteronomy 28). And see what did happen. The king who made light of the word of God was taken captive by Nebuchadnezzar, and his children brought up in front of him and every one slain: his eyes were put out of his head; and he was bound in fetters of brass and cast into a dungeon in Babylon. (2 Kings 25:7). That is the way he reaped what he had sown. Surely it is an evil and a bitter thing to backslide, but the Lord would win you back with the message of His Work.

===Chinese===
我们既要忠心，便警告这些退后之人。警告人脱离危险，正是爱的标记。我们一时也许被看作仇敌；但最真实的朋友，正是那些发出警告之声的人。以色列再没有比摩西更真实的朋友了。在耶利米书里，神赐给祂百姓一位流泪的先知，要领他们归向祂；他们却弃绝了神。他们忘记了那领他们出埃及、经过旷野领他们进入应许之地的神。在发达之时，他们便忘记了祂，转离而去。主早已告诉他们将会发生何事。（申命记第 28 章。）看看后来发生了什么。那位轻看神话语的王被尼布甲尼撒掳去，他的众子被带到他面前，一一被杀；他的双眼被剜出；他被铜链锁着，下在巴比伦的监牢里。（列王纪下 25:7。）这便是他自食其果的下场。退后实在是一件又恶又苦的事，但主愿用祂之工的信息把你赢回来。

===Original===
In Jeremiah 8:5, we read: “Why then is this people of Jerusalem slidden by a perpetual backsliding? They hold fast deceit; *They refuse to return*.” That is what the Lord brings against them. “**They refuse to return**.” “I hearkened and heard; but they spake not aright: no man repented him of his wickedness, saying, What have I done? Every one turned to his course, as the horse rusheth into the battle. Yea, the stork in the heaven knoweth her appointed times; and the turtle and the crane and the swallow observe the time of their coming; but My people know not the judgment of the Lord.”

Now look: “I hearkened and heard; but they spake not aright.” No family altar! No reading the Bible! No closet devotion! God stoops to hear; but His people have turned away! If there be a penitent backslider, one who is anxious for pardon and restoration, you will find no words more tender than are to be found in Jeremiah 3:12: “Go, and proclaim these words toward the north, and say, Return, thou backsliding Israel, saith the Lord; and I will not cause Mine anger to fall upon you: for I am merciful, saith the Lord, and I will not keep anger forever.” Now notice: “Only acknowledge thine iniquity, that thou hast transgressed against the Lord thy God, and hast scattered thy ways to the stranger under every green tree, and ye have not obeyed My voice, saith the Lord. Turn, O backsliding children, saith the Lord; for I am married unto you”—think of God coming and saying, “I am married unto you!”—and I will take you one of a city, and two of a family, and I will bring you to Zion.”

===Chinese===
在耶利米书 8:5，我们读到：「这耶路撒冷的百姓，为何长久退后呢？他们持定诡诈，*不肯回头*。」这正是主所控告他们的。「**他们不肯回头**。」「我留心听，听见他们不正直地说：没有人悔改自己的恶行，说：我做了什么呢？他们各人转奔己路，如马直闯战场。空中的鹳鸟知道来的时候；斑鸠、燕子与白鹤也守候当来的时令；我的百姓却不知道耶和华的法则。」

请看：「我留心听，听见他们不正直地说。」——没有家庭祭坛！没有读经！没有内室的灵修！神俯首垂听；祂的百姓却转离而去！若有一个悔悟的退后之人，渴望得着赦免与复兴，你会发现再没有比耶利米书 3:12 更温柔的话了：「你去向北方宣告说：耶和华说：退后的以色列啊，回来吧！我必不怒目看你们；因为我是慈爱的，我必不永远怀怒。这是耶和华说的。」请注意：「只要承认你的罪孽，就是你违背耶和华你的神，在各青翠树下向陌生人分散了你的道路，没有听从我的话。这是耶和华说的。退后的儿女啊，回来吧！因为我是你们的丈夫」——请想想，神竟来对人说：「我是你们的丈夫！」——「我也必从城中取一人，从一族取两人，将你们带到锡安。」

===Original===
“Only acknowledge thine iniquity.” How many times have I held that passage up to a backslider! “Acknowledge” it; and God says I will forgive you. I remember a man asking, “Who said that? Is that there?” And I held up to him the passage, “Only acknowledge thine iniquity;” and the man went down on his knees, and cried, “My God, I have sinned”; and the Lord restored him there and then. If you have wandered, He wants you to come back.

He says in another place, “O Ephraim, what shall I do unto thee? O Judah, what shall I do unto thee? for your goodness is as a morning cloud, and as the early dew it goeth away” (Hosea 6:4). His compassion and His love is wonderful!

===Chinese===
「只要承认你的罪孽。」我曾多少次把这节经文摆在一个退后之人面前！「承认」它；神说，我必赦免你。我记得一个人问：「这是谁说的？真有这话吗？」我便把那段经文指给他看——「只要承认你的罪孽」——那人便跪下去，喊着说：「我的神啊，我犯了罪」；主便当场恢复了他。你若已经流荡远了，祂愿你回来。

在另一处祂又说：「以法莲哪，我可向你怎样行呢？犹大啊，我可向你怎样做呢？因为你们的良善如同早晨的云雾，又如速散的甘露」（何西阿书 6:4）。祂的怜悯和祂的爱何等奇妙！

===Original===
In Jeremiah 3:22; “Return, ye backsliding children, and I will heal your backslidings. Behold, we come unto Thee; Thou art the Lord our God.” He just puts words into the mouth of the backslider. Only come; and, if you will come, He will receive you graciously and love you freely.

In Hosea 14:1, 2, 4: “O Israel, return unto the Lord thy God; for thou hast fallen by thine inequity. Take with you words, and turn to the Lord (He puts words into your mouth): say unto Him, Take away all iniquity, and receive us graciously; so will we render the calves of our lips… I will heal their backsliding, I will love them freely, for Mine anger is turned away from him.” Just observe that, Turn! *Turn!!* **Turn!!!** rings all through these passages.

===Chinese===
耶利米书 3:22 说：「你们这退后的儿女啊，回来吧！我要医治你们退后的病。看哪，我们来到你这里，因你是耶和华我们的神。」祂简直是把话放在退后之人的口里。只要回来；你若肯回来，祂必恩慈地接纳你，甘心地爱你。

何西阿书 14:1, 2, 4 说：「以色列啊，你要归向耶和华你的神；你是因自己的罪孽跌倒了。当归向耶和华，用言语祷告他（祂把话放在你口里）说：求你除净罪孽，悦纳恩惠；这样，我们就把嘴唇的祭献给你……我必医治他们的退后，甘心爱他们，因为我的怒气已转消。」请留意，「归回！*归回！！* **归回！！！**」的呼声贯穿了这些经文。

===Original===
Now, if you have wandered, remember that you left Him, and not He you. You have to get out of the backslider’s pit just in the same way you got in. And if you take the same road as when you left the Master you will find Him now, just where you are.

If we were to treat Christ as any earthly friend we should never leave Him; and there would never be a backslider. If I were in a town for a single week I should not think of going away without shaking hands with the friends I had made, and saying “Goodbye” to them. I should be justly blamed if I took the train and left without saying a word to anyone. The cry would be, “What’s the matter?” But did you ever hear of a backslider bidding the Lord Jesus Christ “Goodbye”; going into his closet and saying “Lord Jesus, I have known Thee ten, twenty, or thirty years: but I am tired of Thy service; Thy yoke is not easy, nor Thy burden light; so I am going back to the world, to the fleshpots of Egypt. Goodbye, Lord Jesus! Farewell”? Did you ever hear that? No; you never did, and you never will. I tell you, if you get into the closet and shut out the world and hold communion with the Master you cannot leave Him. The language of your heart will be, “To whom shall we go,” but unto Thee? “Thou hast the words of eternal life” (John 6:68). You could not go back to the world if you treated Him in that way. But you left Him and ran away. You have forgotten Him days without number. Come back today; just as you are! Make up your mind that you will not rest until God has restored unto you the joy of His salvation.

===Chinese===
你若已经流荡远了，请记住：是你离开了祂，不是祂离开了你。你要从退后之人的坑里出来，正像你当初掉进去时那样。你若循着当初离开夫子时所走的那条路回头，便要在你现在所在之处寻见祂。

我们若待基督像待地上任何一位朋友一样，就永远不会离开祂；世上也就永远不会有退后之人。我若在一个城里哪怕只住一个礼拜，临走时不与所结识的朋友握手道别，是绝不会离开的。我若一言不发地搭上火车就走，便理当受责备。人必喊着说：「这是怎么回事？」可是你曾否听说，有哪一个退后之人向主耶稣基督道「再见」；走进自己的内室，说：「主耶稣啊，我认识你已经十年、二十年、三十年了；可我厌倦了事奉你；你的轭并不容易负，你的担子也不轻省；所以我要回到世界去，回到埃及的肉锅那里。主耶稣，再见了！永别了」？你曾否听过这样的事？没有；你从未听过，也永远不会听。我告诉你，你若进到内室里，把世界关在门外，与夫子相交，你就不能离开祂。你心所要说的必是：「主啊，你有永生之道，我们还归从谁呢？」（约翰福音 6:68）你若这样待祂，便不能回到世界去。可是你离开了祂，逃跑了。你忘记祂，已有无数的日子。今日便回来吧；就照你现在的本相！你要立定心志：在神将祂救恩之乐恢复给你之前，决不安息。

===Original===
A gentleman in Cornwall once met a Christian in the street whom he knew to be a backslider. He went up to him, and said: “Tell me, is there not some estrangement between you and the Lord Jesus?” The man hung his head, and said, “Yes.” “Well,” said the gentleman, “what has He done to you?” The answer to which was a flood of tears.

In Revelation 2:4–5, we read: “Nevertheless I have somewhat against thee, because thou hast left the first love. Remember therefore from whence thou art fallen; and repent, and do the first works: or else I will come unto thee quickly, and will remove thy candlestick out of his place, except thou repent.” I want to guard you against a mistake which some people make with regard to “doing the first works.” Many think that they are to have the same experience over again. That has kept thousands for months without peace; because they have been waiting for a renewal of their first experience. You will never have the same experience as when you first came to the Lord. God never repeats himself. No two people of all earth’s millions look alike or think alike. You may say that you cannot tell two people apart; but when you get well acquainted with them you can very quickly distinguish differences. So, no one person will have the same experience a second time. If God will restore His joy to your soul let Him do it in His way. Do not mark out a way for God to bless you. Do not expect the same experience that you had two or twenty years ago. You will have a fresh experience, and God will deal with you in His own way. If you confess your sins and tell Him that you have wandered from the path of His commandments He will restore unto you the joy of His salvation.

===Chinese===
康沃尔有一位绅士，在街上遇见一位他知道是退后之人的基督徒。他走过去，说：「告诉我，你与主耶稣之间是不是有了些隔阂？」那人垂下头，说：「是的。」「那么，」那绅士说，「祂做了什么对不起你的事呢？」回答他的，是泪如雨下。

启示录 2:4–5 说：「然而有一件事我要责备你，就是你把起初的爱心离弃了。所以，应当回想你是从哪里坠落的，并要悔改，行起初所行的事。你若不悔改，我就临到你那里，把你的灯台从原处挪去。」我想提醒你们防备一个错误——有些人在「行起初所行的事」上所犯的错误。许多人以为，他们要再把当初的经历重演一遍。这一个误解，使成千的人数月之久没有平安；因为他们一直在等当初那段经历重新出现。你绝不会再有初次到主面前时那样的经历。神从不重复祂自己。全地上千千万万人中，没有哪两个人相貌相同、心思相同。你也许说你分不清某两个人；可一旦你与他们熟识了，便能很快分辨出不同之处。照样，没有哪一个人会第二次再有同样的经历。神若要把祂的喜乐恢复给你的灵魂，就任凭祂用祂的方式来作。不要替神画定一条赐福给你的路。不要指望二十年前或两年前的那种经历。你将有一次崭新的经历，神必用祂自己的方式对待你。你若认你的罪，告诉祂你已偏离了祂诫命的道路，祂必将祂救恩之乐恢复给你。

===Original===
I want to call your attention to the manner in which Peter fell; and I think that nearly all fall pretty much in the same way. I want to lift up a warning note to those who have not fallen. “Let him that thinketh he standeth, take heed lest he fall” (1 Corinthians 10:12). Twenty-five years ago—and for the first five years after I was converted—I used to think that if I were able to stand for twenty years I need fear no fall. But the nearer you get to the Cross the fiercer the battle. Satan aims high. He went amongst the twelve; and singled out the Treasurer—Judas Iscariot, and the Chief Apostle—Peter. Most men who have fallen have done so on the strongest side of their character. I am told that the only side upon which Edinburgh Castle was successfully assailed was where the rocks were steepest, and where the garrison thought themselves secure. If any man thinks that he is strong enough to resist the devil at any one point he needs special watch there, for the tempter comes that way.

Abraham stands, as it were, at the head of the family of faith; and the children of faith may be said to trace their descent to Abraham: and yet down in Egypt he denied his wife. (Genesis 12). Moses was noted for his meekness; and yet he was kept out of the promised land because of one hasty act and speech, when he was told by the Lord to speak to the rock so that the congregation and their beasts should have water to drink. “Hear now, ye rebels; must we fetch you water out of this rock?” (Numbers 20:10).

===Chinese===
我想请你们留意彼得跌倒的方式；我想几乎所有的人跌倒，方式都差不多。我要向那些尚未跌倒的人发出警号。「自己以为站得稳的，须要谨慎，免得跌倒」（哥林多前书 10:12）。二十五年前——也就是我归主后的头五年——我常想，我若能站得住二十年，便不必怕跌倒了。可是你越靠近十字架，争战越猛烈。撒但瞄准高处。它在十二门徒中出入，挑中了那位司库——加略人犹大，和那位使徒之首——彼得。大多数跌倒的人，都是在自己性格最强的一面跌倒的。有人告诉我，爱丁堡城堡唯一被攻破的那一面，正是岩石最陡峭、守军自以为最稳妥的一面。一个人若以为自己强到一个地步，能在某一点上抵挡魔鬼，那一点正是他需要格外警醒之处，因为那试探者正是从那条路而来。

亚伯拉罕可以说站在信心家族之首；信心之子都可以说是从亚伯拉罕追溯其源：可是在埃及，他竟否认了自己的妻子。（创世记第 12 章。）摩西以谦和著称；然而只因一次急躁的举动和言语，他便被挡在应许之地门外——那时主原吩咐他对磐石说话，好叫会众和他们的牲畜有水可喝。他却说：「你们这些背叛的人听我说：我为你们使磐石流出水来吗？」（民数记 20:10）。

===Original===
Elijah was remarkable for his boldness: and yet he went off a day’s journey into the wilderness like a coward and hid himself under a juniper tree, requesting for himself that he might die, because of a message he received from a woman. (1 Kings 19). Let us be careful. No matter who the man is—he may be in the pulpit—but if he gets self-conceited he will be sure to fall. We who are followers of Christ need constantly to pray to be made humble, and kept humble. God made Moses’ face so to shine that other men could see it; but Moses himself wist not that his face shone, and the more holy in heart a man is the more manifest to the outer world will be his daily life and conversation. Some people talk of how humble they are; but if they have true humility there will be no necessity for them to publish it. It is not needful. A lighthouse does not have a drum beaten or a trumpet blown in order to proclaim the proximity of a lighthouse: it is its own witness. And so if we have the true light in us it will show itself. It is not those who make the most noise who have the most piety. There is a brook, or a little “burn” as the Scotch call it, not far from where I live; and after a heavy rain you can hear the rush of its waters a long way off: but let there come a few days of pleasant weather, and the brook becomes almost silent. But there is a river near my house, the flow of which I never heard in my life, as it pours on in its deep and majestic course the year round. We should have so much of the love of God within us that its presence shall be evident without our loud proclamation of the fact.

===Chinese===
以利亚以他的勇敢著称：然而他竟像一个懦夫，走了一日的路程进入旷野，把自己藏在罗腾树下，求神让他死——只因他收到一个女人捎来的口信。（列王纪上第 19 章。）让我们谨慎。一个人无论他是谁——也许正站在讲台上——只要一自恃，就必跌倒。我们这些跟随基督的人，需要不断地求神使我们谦卑、保守我们谦卑。神使摩西的脸发光，别人都看得见；摩西自己却不知道自己的脸发光。一个人心里越圣洁，他日常的生活与言谈对外人就越发显明。有些人到处说自己多么谦卑；可他们若真有谦卑，便毫无必要去张扬。这是不必要的。一座灯塔不必敲锣吹号，好宣告附近有一座灯塔：它本身就是自己的见证。照样，我们里面若有真光，它必自己显明出来。最喧嚷的人，并不一定最有敬虔。离我住处不远有一条小溪——苏格兰人管它叫「burn」的——一场大雨之后，你远远便能听见它水声奔腾：可只要晴上几天，那小溪便几乎悄无声息了。可我家附近还有一条河，我一生从未听见过它的水流声，它一年到头在那又深又壮的河道里静静地流着。我们里面当充满神的爱，多到一个地步，无须我们大声宣扬，它的同在便自然显明。

===Original===
The first step in Peter’s downfall was his self-confidence. The Lord warned him. The Lord said: “Simon, Simon, behold, Satan hath desired to have you, that he may sift you as wheat: but I have prayed for thee, that thy faith fail not” (Luke 22:31–32). But Peter said: “I am ready to go with Thee, both into prison and to death.” “Though all shall be offended because of Thee, yet will I never be offended.” (Matthew 26:33). “James and John, and the others, may leave You; but You can count on me!” But the Lord warned him: “I tell thee, Peter, the cock shall not crow this day, before that thou shalt thrice deny that thou knowest Me.” (Luke 22:34).

Though the Lord rebuked him, Peter said he was ready to follow Him to death. That boasting is too often a forerunner of downfall. Let us walk humbly and softly. We have a great tempter; and, in an unguarded hour, we may stumble and fall and bring a scandal on Christ.

===Chinese===
彼得跌倒的第一步，是他的自信。主曾警告他。主说：「西门！西门！撒但想要得着你们，好筛你们像筛麦子一样；但我已经为你祈求，叫你不至于失了信心」（路加福音 22:31–32）。彼得却说：「主啊，我就是同你下监，同你受死，也是甘心。」「众人虽然为你的缘故跌倒，我却永不跌倒。」（马太福音 26:33）「雅各、约翰，还有其他的人，也许会离开你；可你尽可指望我！」主却警告他：「彼得，我告诉你，今日鸡还没有叫，你要三次说不认得我。」（路加福音 22:34）。

主虽然责备他，彼得却仍说自己甘心跟随祂到死。这种自夸，往往是跌倒的前奏。让我们谦卑、柔和地行。我们有一位大能的试探者；在不警醒的时刻，我们也许会绊跌、跌倒，使基督的名受羞辱。

===Original===
The next step in Peter’s downfall was that he went to sleep. If Satan can rock the Church to sleep he does his work through God’s own people. Instead of Peter watching one short hour in Gethsemane, he fell asleep, and the Lord asked him, “What, could ye not watch with Me one hour?” (Matthew 26:40). The next thing was that he fought in the energy of the flesh. The Lord rebuked him again and said, “They that take the sword shall perish with the sword.” (Matthew 26:52). Jesus had to undo what Peter had done. The next thing, he “followed afar off.” Step by step he gets away. It is a sad thing when a child of God follows afar off. When you see him associating with worldly friends, and throwing his influence on the wrong side, he is following afar off; and it will not be long before disgrace will be brought upon the old family name, and Jesus Christ will be wounded in the house of his friends. The man, by his example, will cause others to stumble and fall.

===Chinese===
彼得跌倒的下一步，是他睡着了。撒但若能把教会摇睡了，它便借着神自己的百姓作成它的工作。在客西马尼，彼得本该警醒短短一个时辰，他却睡着了；主问他：「怎么样？你们不能同我警醒片时吗？」（马太福音 26:40）。接下来，他在肉体的血气里动起武来。主又责备他，说：「收刀入鞘吧！凡动刀的，必死在刀下。」（马太福音 26:52）。耶稣不得不把彼得所做的收拾回来。再下一步，他「远远地跟着」。他一步一步地离远了。一个神的儿女远远地跟着，这是一件可悲的事。当你看见他与世俗的朋友结交，把自己的影响力摆在错误的一边，他就是远远地跟着；用不了多久，那古老家族的尊名便要蒙羞，耶稣基督要在祂朋友的家中被伤。这个人因着他的榜样，必使别人绊跌、跌倒。

===Original===
The next thing—Peter is familiar and friendly with the enemies of Christ. A damsel says to this bold Peter: “Thou also wast with this Jesus of Galilee.” But he denied before them all, saying, “I know not what thou sayest.” And when he was gone out into the porch another maid saw him and said unto them that were there, “This fellow was also with Jesus of Nazareth.” And again he denied with an oath. “I do not know the Man.” Another hour passed; and yet he did not realize his position; when another confidently affirmed that he was a Galilean, for his speech betrayed him. And he was angry and began to curse and to swear, and again denied his Master: and the cock crew. (Matthew 26:69–74).

He commences away up on the pinacle of self-conceit, and goes down step by step until he breaks out into cursing, and swears that he never knew his Lord.

===Chinese===
再接下来——彼得竟与基督的仇敌亲热结交。一个使女对这位刚才还勇敢的彼得说：「你素来也是同那加利利人耶稣一伙的。」他却在众人面前不认，说：「我不知道你说的是什么。」他出到门廊里，又一个使女看见他，对那里的人说：「这个人也是同拿撒勒人耶稣一伙的。」他又起誓不认。「我不认识那个人。」又过了一个时辰；他仍不省察自己的处境；当另一个人肯定地说他是个加利利人，因为他的口音把他露了出来。他便发怒，开始咒诅起誓，再一次不认他的夫子：鸡就叫了。（马太福音 26:69–74）。

他从自恃的顶峰出发，一步一步往下走，直到发作起来咒诅人，起誓说他从来不认识他的主。

===Original===
The Master might have turned and said to him, “Is it true, Peter, that you have forgotten Me so soon? Do you not remember when your wife’s mother lay sick of a fever that I rebuked the disease and it left her? Do you not call to mind your astonishment at the draught of fishes so that you exclaimed, ‘Depart from me; for I am a sinful man, O Lord?’ Do you remember when in answer to your cry, ‘Lord, save me, or I perish,’ I stretched out My hand and kept you from drowning in the water? Have you forgotten when, on the Mount of Transfiguration, with James and John, you said to Me, ‘Lord, it is good to be here: let us make three tabernacles?’ Have you forgotten being with Me at the supper-table, and in Gethsemane? Is it true that you have forgotten Me so soon?” The Lord might have upbraided him with questions such as these: but He did nothing of the kind. He cast one look on Peter: and there was so much love in it that it broke that bold disciple’s heart: and he went out and wept bitterly.

===Chinese===
夫子本可以转过身来对他说：「彼得，这是真的吗？你这么快就把我忘了？你不记得你岳母害热病躺着，我斥责那病，病就离开她了吗？你不想想那一网鱼所打的，叫你惊奇得喊着说：『主啊，离开我，我是个罪人！』你不记得你喊着『主啊，救我，我丧命啦！』我便伸手把你从水里拉住，使你不致淹没吗？你忘了在变像山上，你与雅各、约翰一同对我说：『主啊，我们在这里真好！可以搭三座棚』？你忘了你与我在晚餐的席上、在客西马尼同在吗？你这么快就把我忘了，这是真的吗？」主本可以用这样的话来责问他；但祂没有这样作。祂只向彼得看了一眼：那一眼里有那么多爱，便把那位刚强的门徒的心击碎了：他便出去，痛痛地哭了。

===Original===
And after Christ rose from the dead see how tenderly He dealt with the erring disciple. The angel at the sepulchre says, “Tell His disciples, *and Peter*.” (Mark 16:7). The Lord did not forget Peter, though Peter had denied Him thrice; so He caused this kindly special message to be conveyed to the repentant disciple. What a tender and loving Saviour we have!

Friend, if you are one of the wanderers, let the loving look of the Master win you back; and let Him restore you to the joy of His salvation.

===Chinese===
基督从死里复活之后，请看祂何等温柔地对待这位失足的门徒。坟墓那里的天使说：「你们去告诉他的门徒*和彼得*。」（马可福音 16:7）。主没有忘记彼得，虽然彼得已经三次不认祂；所以祂特意叫人把这亲切的特别口信带给这位悔悟的门徒。我们有一位何等温柔、何等慈爱的救主！

朋友，你若也是流荡者之一，愿夫子那慈爱的一眼把你赢回来；愿祂将祂救恩之乐恢复给你。

===Original===
Before closing, let me say that I trust God will restore some backslider reading these pages, who may in the future become a useful member of society and a bright ornament of the Church. We should never have had the thirty-second Psalm if David had not been restored: “Blessed is he whose transgression is forgiven, whose sin is covered”; or that beautiful fifty-first Psalm which was written by the restored backslider. Nor should we have had that wonderful sermon on the day of Pentecost when three thousand were converted—preached by another restored backslider.

May God restore other backsliders and make them a thousand times more used for His glory than they ever were before.

===Chinese===
在结束之前，容我说：我深信神必恢复某些正在读这些页的退后之人，他们日后也许会成为社会中有用的一员，成为教会中明亮的装饰。若不是大卫被恢复过来，我们就不会有诗篇第 32 篇：「得赦免其过、遮盖其罪的，这人是有福的」；也没有那篇佳美的诗篇第 51 篇——它正是那位被恢复过来的退后之人所写的。我们也不会有五旬节那日那篇奇妙的讲道，那一天有三千人归主——讲那篇道的，是另一位被恢复过来的退后之人。

愿神恢复其他的退后之人，使他们比从前任何时候更被重用于祂的荣耀，胜过千倍。
